# 02. Approvals and permission rules

Round 2 synthesis, research date 2026-11-10. Source: the Round 2 source-code
research files in `research/round2/`, each repository pinned to the commit in
`research/round2/00-repos.tsv`.

## 1. Scope

**The group.** Everything that decides whether a tool call the model asked for
runs, asks a human, or is refused:

- approval modes and their real defaults on each surface;
- rule grammars and precedence, including who overrides whom (user, project,
  administrator policy, session, sub-agent) and where a repository's own files
  can loosen the user's settings;
- how a shell command is parsed before matching;
- what an approval is bound to, and how long it lasts;
- model-based approvers, and flags the model sets about its own calls;
- plan and read-only modes;
- sub-agent inheritance;
- paths that bypass the approval path;
- hooks in the permission path;
- how an approval is answered remotely.

Sandboxing is covered only where it changes an approval outcome. Two examples:
an `allow` rule that also skips the sandbox, and a sandbox denial that offers
an unsandboxed retry. The sandbox itself is another group's subject.

**Files read.** All 31 Round 2 files, through one extraction per repository.
For each repository, the in-group findings were pulled from every cluster file
together with sections 4 (Round 1 claims), 5 (in the code but not in the
documentation), 6 (could not settle) and 8 (review). Each extraction then went
through a script that checked every permalink character for character against
the source files. The extractions hold 342 findings in this group:

| Project | Findings |
|---|---|
| Codex | 26 |
| OpenCode | 31 |
| OpenClaw | 34 |
| Hermes Agent | 30 |
| Oh My Pi | 25 |
| Pi | 24 |
| DeepSeek harness | 23 |
| goose | 27 |
| Cline | 33 |
| OpenHands | 22 |
| Zed | 13 |
| Warp | 21 |
| OpenCodex | 13 |

Three other inputs were read:

- `research/round1/own-rizzy-harness.md`, for our own state;
- `docs/nodes-credentials-environments.md`;
- our protocol and event types, `crates/protocol/src/lib.rs` and
  `crates/core/src/event.rs`, to state what our approval answer carries today.

**Trust labels.** Each claim carries one of these labels:

- `[checked in review]`: a reviewer re-read the cited code and agreed.
- `[corrected in review]`: a reviewer changed the wording; the corrected text
  is used.
- `[unchecked]`: no reviewer looked at it.
- `[inferred]`: reasoned from code, not traced.
- `[absence confirmed]`: a reviewer searched for the thing and did not find it.

**Contradiction resolved by opening the clone.** The two Pi files disagreed:

- `pi.sessions.md` says RPC clients can answer extension dialogs.
- The example permission gate in `pi.safety.md` treats a missing UI as "block".

I opened the clone. `ExtensionRunner.hasUI()` is `this.uiContext !== noOpUIContext`
(https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/extensions/runner.ts#L628-L630,
opened by me). RPC mode installs a real UI context at
https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-mode.ts#L324-L324
(opened by me). That context sends `extension_ui_request` frames, each with
an `id`, a `title` and a `message`
(https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-types.ts#L253-L254,
opened by me).

So in RPC mode `hasUI` is true, and the gate's question goes to the RPC
client. The "no UI, block" branch applies only to print and JSON modes. The
answer is bound to the dialog id. The client sees whatever text the extension
put in the message.

**What was not available.**

- **OpenHands:** the repository read is only the Agent Canvas client.
  Confirmation-policy enforcement, the security analyzers and ACP permission
  handling live in `OpenHands/software-agent-sdk`, which was not cloned.
- **Warp:** the client trusts two booleans that Warp's server sets,
  `is_risky` and `is_read_only`. How the server's model sets them, and any
  server-side re-check, is not in the repository.
- **OpenCodex:** the Claude Code and Pi remote-workspace runtimes were not read.
- **Not cloned at all:** Claude Code, Claude Desktop and Codex Desktop, because
  they are closed source.

## 2. Implementations by project

One account per repository, condensed from the extraction of that repository's Round 2 files. Each claim keeps its permalinks and its trust label.

### Codex

**Scope.** `openai/codex` at `82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053`, Rust workspace `codex-rs/`: the agent loop (`core`), the JSON-RPC "app-server" used by the TUI, `codex exec`, IDE and SDK clients, and the `exec-server`, a remote tool host that never calls the model. Approvals are decided in `core/src/exec_policy.rs`, `ToolOrchestrator::run`, the Guardian auto-reviewer and the hook engine. The Desktop app is closed source.

**Modes and defaults.** The approval policy (`AskForApproval`) has four values in code: `Never`, `OnRequest`, `Granular` and `UnlessTrusted`. `untrusted` is no longer a value users can set, but `UnlessTrusted` is still the internal mode for untrusted projects. The default requirement per policy is: `Never` skips approval; `OnRequest` and `Granular` ask only when the filesystem is restricted; `UnlessTrusted` always asks; `Granular` with sandbox approval turned off turns a prompt into `Forbidden` https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/tools/sandboxing.rs#L195-L236 [unchecked]. The default reviewer is the user, not the model https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/protocol/src/config_types.rs#L179-L190 [corrected in review; the correction concerns only the timeout, see below]. Per surface:
- The TUI connects with `required_human_approval: true` https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/tui/src/lib.rs#L478-L524 [checked in review].
- `codex exec` (headless) defaults to `Never`, rejects approval requests, `request_user_input` and dynamic tool calls with an error, and cancels MCP elicitations; with an `AutoReview` reviewer it drops the `Never` default so a model approver runs headless https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/exec/src/lib.rs#L622-L640 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/exec/src/lib.rs#L2228-L2300 [checked in review].
- The IDE defaults, and any desktop or chat-channel surface, are not in the files read.

**Rule grammar and precedence.**
- **Grammar.** `.rules` files are Starlark (a Python-like config language) with builtins `prefix_rule(pattern, decision, match, not_match, justification, canonical_matching)` (default decision `allow`), `network_rule` and `host_executable` https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/execpolicy/src/parser.rs#L347-L410. A pattern (a token list; a token may list alternatives) must be an exact token-by-token prefix of argv https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/execpolicy/src/rule.rs#L46-L73.
- **Decision.** Every matching rule is collected, and the result is the maximum of `Allow < Prompt < PromptUser < Forbidden`. A rule file can only write `allow`, `prompt` or `forbidden` https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/execpolicy/src/decision.rs#L7-L30 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/execpolicy/src/policy.rs#L577-L595 [checked in review].
- **Layers.** Rule files are read from `rules/` under every enabled config layer, from low to high precedence, and the managed `requirements` policy is merged on top https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/exec_policy.rs#L746-L806 [checked in review]. Because the decision is a maximum, a layer can add restrictions but cannot lower a stricter decision from another layer.
- **Repository files.** A repository's config, hooks and rules load only when the user's config marks the project as trusted. Otherwise the project layer is disabled with the message "To load project-local config, hooks, and exec policies, add `<key>` as a trusted project" https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/config/src/loader/mod.rs#L1100-L1170 [checked in review]. Once a project is trusted, its `allow` rules count like the user's own.
- **Inline tests.** Each rule's `match` and `not_match` examples are run when the file loads. If one file fails to parse or fails an example, `load_exec_policy_with_warning` keeps only the managed policy. That drops all user and project rules, including the user's own `forbidden` rules, for the whole session, with only a warning https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/exec_policy.rs#L729-L744 [checked in review].

**Shell parsing before matching.**
1. Only `bash`, `zsh` or `sh` called with `-c` or `-lc` and a script is unpacked https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/shell-command/src/bash.rs#L106-L127.
2. tree-sitter-bash parses the script. The parse is rejected on any error node, on any named node outside `program, list, pipeline, command, command_name, word, string, string_content, raw_string, number, concatenation`, and on any operator other than `&&`, `||`, `;` and `|` https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/shell-command/src/bash.rs#L29-L98.
3. Words that contain `{ } * ? [ ] \ ~ ^ # $` or a backtick, or that start with `=`, are rejected. Double-quoted strings pass only if they contain no expansion and no escape https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/shell-command/src/bash.rs#L260-L301.
4. So assignments (`FOO=1 cmd`), redirections, substitutions, subshells, functions and control flow fail to split, and the whole `["bash","-lc","<script>"]` argv is matched as one command, so only a rule whose first token is `bash` can match it https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/exec_policy.rs#L971-L1001 [checked in review].

Wrappers such as `sudo` and `env` are not unwrapped for rule matching. A second, looser parser walks literal commands inside any syntax, but it is used only to detect dangerous commands and is documented as "must not be used to prove that a command is safe" https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/shell-command/src/bash.rs#L129-L160 [checked in review].

Commands no rule matches go to a heuristic that flags almost nothing except forced `rm`. It looks through `sudo`, `env`, `trap` and nested literal commands, and nesting deeper than its limit counts as dangerous. A dangerous command prompts, or is forbidden under `Never`. Anything else is allowed inside the sandbox under `Never`, `OnRequest` and `Granular`, and prompts under `UnlessTrusted`. There is no list of known-safe commands https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/exec_policy.rs#L865-L950 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/shell-command/src/command_safety/is_dangerous_command.rs#L132-L160 [checked in review]. Safety therefore rests on the sandbox.

**Approval binding and "always".**
- **For this session.** "Approve for session" stores an entry in an in-memory map keyed by environment id, executable, canonicalised argv, cwd, tty flag, sandbox permissions and additional permissions. Patch approvals are keyed per file path. MCP calls, network access and permission requests are never cached https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/tools/runtimes/unified_exec.rs#L87-L98 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/tools/approvals.rs#L282-L322 [unchecked]. The map lives in memory and ends with the process. On resume, the approval policy, reviewer and permission profile are restored from the last `ThreadSettingsApplied` event in the log, and explicit resume parameters override them https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/persisted_resume_settings.rs#L1-L45 [unchecked].
- **Always.** "Accept and remember" (`acceptWithExecpolicyAmendment`) appends `prefix_rule(..., allow)` to `$CODEX_HOME/rules/default.rules` and hot-reloads it, so it lasts forever https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/exec_policy.rs#L516-L564 [checked in review].
- **An `allow` rule also removes the sandbox.** When every segment of a command matched an explicit `allow` rule, the requirement becomes `Skip { bypass_sandbox: true }` and the first attempt runs unsandboxed. The only exception is a policy with denied reads https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/exec_policy.rs#L492-L512 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/tools/sandboxing.rs#L253-L298 [checked in review]. A remembered approval is therefore a permanent sandbox escape for that prefix.

**Sandbox denial and retry.** Approval is asked before the first attempt.
1. If that attempt fails with `SandboxErr::Denied`, a retry without the sandbox is considered. `OnRequest` and `Never` never offer that retry, and neither does any policy with denied reads.
2. The retry needs a fresh approval unless the first approval came from the user or from a hook.
3. A Guardian approval never pre-approves the retry, and the retry is always re-reviewed https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/tools/orchestrator.rs#L446-L480 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/tools/orchestrator.rs#L491-L501 [checked in review].

"Denied" is a heuristic (exit codes, SIGSYS, keywords such as `sandbox`) https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/sandboxing/src/denial.rs#L13-L72 [checked in review].

**Model-based approver (Guardian).**
- **Routing.** Guardian runs only when the policy is `OnRequest` or `Granular` and `approvals_reviewer = auto_review` https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/routing.rs#L59-L67. It is a separate internal thread with a default overall budget of 90 s, configurable through `auto_review.timeout_ms`, and up to 3 attempts https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/lib.rs#L89-L89 [corrected in review: 90 s is a default, not fixed].
- **What it may do.** It gets only `exec_command`, `write_stdin`, `view_image`, `exec` and `wait`. It runs under a managed sandbox intersected with read-only, with approval policy `never`, and without the parent's exec-policy rules, so an `allow` rule cannot unsandbox it https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/settings.rs#L43-L65 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/guardian/review.rs#L239-L304 [checked in review].
- **What it sees.** Tool calls and outputs, no reasoning; 5,000 tokens per message, 1,000 per tool entry, 20,000/10,000 message/tool totals, last 40 non-user entries https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/guardian-context/src/preparation/transcript.rs#L66-L90.
- **Thresholds.** The policy template rates risk (low, medium, high, critical) and user authorization. Low and medium risk are allowed. High risk is allowed only with at least medium authorization and narrow scope. Critical is denied https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/prompts/templates/guardian/policy_template.md#L66-L78.
- **Trusted content.** Only user and developer messages, `AGENTS.md` files and `request_user_input` answers are trusted; tool output is untrusted evidence https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/prompts/templates/guardian/policy_template.md#L5-L13 [checked in review]. Because repository `AGENTS.md` files count as trusted, a hostile repository can "authorize" actions.
- **Failure modes.** Prompt-build, session, parse and stale-authorization errors deny. A timeout is `TimedOut`: the agent may retry once or ask the user. Over-budget input gives no decision and falls back to the human, unless review is mandatory (then fail closed). A cached low-risk async score can approve without a synchronous review https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/completion.rs#L86-L170 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/completion.rs#L50-L67 [checked in review].
- **Circuit breaker** (a denial counter that stops the turn). It interrupts the turn once after 3 consecutive denials or 10 of the last 50 (1 and 1 for cyber-specialty models). It counts per turn and resets with the turn https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/circuit_breaker.rs#L3-L98 [checked in review].

**Model-supplied flags.**
- A tool call carries sandbox permissions and additional permissions, which form part of the session approval key. `OnRequest` and `Granular` prompt for an unmatched command only "when the call asks for escalation" https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/exec_policy.rs#L865-L950 [checked in review].
- The model can propose a `prefix_rule` ("smart approvals"). It is offered only if it is not in a ban list (`bash`, `sh -c`, `env`, `git`, `bun run`, ...) and only if it would allow every segment of the current command https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/exec_policy.rs#L64-L94 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/exec_policy.rs#L1065-L1127 [checked in review].

**Plan and read-only modes.** Plan mode is prompt-only: developer text ("non-mutating actions only"), medium effort. In code only `update_plan` errors, `request_user_input` blocks, and automatic turns cannot switch mode; sandbox, policy and tools are unchanged, so `apply_patch` succeeds if the sandbox allows it https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/plan.rs#L87-L91 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn_input.rs#L79-L96 [checked in review]. The built-in `explorer` role points to a zero-byte `explorer.toml`, so it is a label, not a read-only agent https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/agent/role.rs#L331-L343 [checked in review].

**Sub-agent inheritance.** A role file is parsed into `AgentRoleOverrides`, which has only narrowing fields. The features `shell_tool`, `apps`, `plugins`, `memories` and `request_permissions_tool` can only be turned off. Sandbox, approval policy, provider and MCP servers are not fields at all https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/agent/role.rs#L88-L121. After the role is applied, the parent's runtime approval policy, reviewer, cwd and permission profile are copied onto the child again https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/agent/child_config.rs#L197-L218 [checked in review]. A Guardian override copied into a child's forked history is filtered out, so the child cannot reuse the parent's override https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/guardian-context/src/transcript.rs#L37-L52 [checked in review]. Depth is limited to 1 in code; the rule "do not spawn unless asked" exists only in the tool description https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs#L738-L739 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents/spawn.rs#L71-L80 [checked in review]. Internal children can have safety reduced: the memory consolidation agent runs with approvals `Never`, and if the parent profile is not a managed sandbox it inherits that profile unsandboxed https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/memories/write/src/phase2.rs#L339-L359 [checked in review].

**Bypass paths.**
- `exec --ignore-rules` skips the execpolicy rules [checked in review].
- Remembered `allow` rules run commands unsandboxed (above).
- A hook allow pre-approves the unsandboxed retry (below).
- `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust` runs untrusted hooks https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/utils/cli/src/shared_options.rs#L61-L64 [checked in review].
- **Remote executor.** It takes its sandbox from the caller's request; a request without `sandbox` runs unsandboxed, and the executor has no ceiling of its own https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/exec-server/src/process_sandbox.rs#L142-L172 [checked in review; absence of a ceiling confirmed].
- **Untrusted repositories.** Their skills still load into the prompt https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/config/src/state.rs#L742-L757 [absence confirmed].
- **Code Mode.** It is the default tool surface for most catalogued models, and its interaction with approvals is not traced https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/core/src/tools/mod.rs#L66-L76 [checked in review for the catalog value].

**Hooks.**
- **Fail open.** PreToolUse blocks only via exit 2 plus stderr or a JSON block; error, timeout (default 600 s), other exits or bad JSON mark it `Failed` and do not block.
- **PermissionRequest hooks.** Any deny wins over earlier allows. An allow approves with source `Hook`, which also pre-approves the unsandboxed retry https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/hooks/src/events/pre_tool_use.rs#L211-L300 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/hooks/src/events/permission_request.rs#L153-L173 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/hooks/src/engine/discovery.rs#L946-L970 [checked in review].
- **Trust.** A hook is trusted by a hash of its normalised declaration (event, matcher, handler). The hash is stored only in the user or session-flag layers, which the repository cannot write; plugin hooks add a hash of the whole plugin directory, re-checked before each run https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/hooks/src/config_rules.rs#L10-L30 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/hooks/src/engine/discovery.rs#L972-L1027 [checked in review].
- **Gap.** A project hook such as `command = "./scripts/check.sh"` is hashed by its command string only, so the script can change after approval with no new review [checked in review].

**Remote approval answering.**
- **Transport auth.** Approvals reach clients as JSON-RPC requests over stdio, a Unix socket, `ws://` or the vendor remote-control relay. WebSocket auth is a capability token or an HS256 JWT. It is checked once, at the HTTP upgrade before `initialize`, and expiry is not re-checked afterwards. Any request with an `Origin` header gets 403 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/app-server-transport/src/transport/websocket.rs#L99-L128 https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/websocket-auth/src/lib.rs#L279-L289 [checked in review].
- **Listeners.** The app-server refuses a non-loopback listener without auth; loopback is unauthenticated, so any local process can answer approvals; the exec-server `--listen` listener has no refusal https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/app-server-transport/src/transport/websocket.rs#L160-L189 [corrected in review: the refusal is app-server only].
- **Human-only approvals.** A `HumanCommand` approval ("a managed checkpoint requiring a fresh human decision") is routed only to connections that declared `requiredHumanApproval`. Headless and SDK clients therefore cannot answer it https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/82e6bacb9c6950d3b56cb153847fa80bdd698053/codex-rs/app-server/src/outgoing_message.rs#L414-L430 [unchecked].
- **Binding.** Whether an answer is bound to anything beyond the JSON-RPC request it answers is not in the files read [inferred].

### OpenCode

**Scope.** The files read cover `anomalyco/opencode` at commit `05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f` (branch `dev`, 2026-11-10), the V2 engine only. The engine is `packages/core` (TypeScript on Bun, written in Effect). `packages/server` serves it over HTTP and `packages/cli` launches it. V1 exists only as migration code that turns old `permission`/`tools`/`agent` keys into V2 rules. One server process per machine owns every session. The agent loop, every tool and every plugin run inside that process as the host user. The TUI, web app, desktop app, `opencode run` and the SDK are all HTTP clients of it. Nearly everything is a plugin, including the built-in agents, plan mode, the policy enforcer and the config readers. Plugins activate in a fixed order: internal `pre`, then user and package plugins, then internal `post`, which ends with the policy plugin. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/plugin/supervisor.ts#L94-L98 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/plugin/internal.ts#L285-L309 [checked in review]

**Modes and real defaults.** OpenCode has no approval "modes" as a setting. It has one rule list per agent. Every agent starts from `[*:* allow, read *.env ask, read *.env.* ask, read *.env.example allow]`. So by default nothing asks except reading `.env` files. Shell, edit, webfetch, MCP tools and `external_directory` (access outside the project) are all allowed. Even the `.env` asks can be bypassed, because `cat .env` is a shell resource and shell is allowed. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/schema/src/agent.ts#L39-L52 [checked in review]. The defaults differ by surface as follows:
- Interactive clients (TUI, web, desktop) get ask prompts only where a rule says ask.
- Headless `opencode run` answers every permission request in its session tree for you. By default it sends `reject` with feedback. With `--auto` it sends `once`. `--yolo` and `--dangerously-skip-permissions` are aliases that also set `auto`. Configured denies and policy denies still hold. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/cli/src/run/noninteractive.ts#L97-L112 [checked in review]; https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/cli/src/commands/handlers/run.ts#L17-L36 [unchecked]
- Schedules use an `Unattended` ruleset. An ask becomes allow only if the unattended rules allow every resource. Otherwise it is denied. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/permission.ts#L59-L65 [checked in review]
- IDE and chat-gateway surfaces: not in the files read.

**Rule grammar and precedence.** A rule is `{action, resource, effect}`. The effect is allow, ask or deny. `evaluate` flattens the rule lists and uses `findLast`: the last rule whose action and resource both match decides, and no match means ask. In the matcher, `*` becomes `.*` and crosses `/`. A pattern ending in ` *` also matches without that suffix, so `git status *` matches a bare `git status`. Matching is case-insensitive on Windows. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/permission.ts#L94-L104 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/util/wildcard.ts#L3-L14 [checked in review]. One call can name several resources. `evaluateInput` decides it in this order (https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/permission.ts#L184-L210 [checked in review]):
1. Build the configured rules: agent rules, then session rules. If any resource is denied by these alone, the answer is deny, and nothing below runs.
2. Append the saved "always" rules. If any resource evaluates to ask, the answer is ask. Otherwise it is allow.
3. Run the `permission.evaluate` plugin hooks. They may rewrite the effect.
4. The admin policy hook runs last and can only force a deny.
5. If an ask remains and an `Unattended` ruleset is in scope, settle it as described under modes.

An agent ID that resolves to nothing gets `[*:* deny]`, and a closed permission service denies everything. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/permission.ts#L20 [checked in review]

Config layers load in this order: managed policies (only their `policies` part), Console and integration documents, well-known documents, global files, explicit files, direct project files, the project's `.opencode` directories, `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`, and finally managed settings (system dir and macOS MDM, with policies stripped). Each agent's rule list is then built as: the built-in agent rules, then every document's top-level `permissions` in load order, then every document's `agents.<id>.permissions` in load order. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/config.ts#L305-L377 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/config/plugin/agent.ts#L64-L107 [checked in review]. Because last match wins, this order has four consequences:
- A user's global `edit * allow` overrides the plan agent's built-in `edit * deny`.
- Managed top-level `permissions` beat user and project top-level `permissions`.
- **Repository files can loosen managed settings.** A project file's `agents.build.permissions` comes after every top-level rule, managed ones included, so a repository can lift a managed top-level deny for a named agent. This follows from the order; no test shows it. [checked in review; consequence derived, not tested]
- Session rules sent by any HTTP client come after all of these and win. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/server/src/handlers/session.ts#L101-L120 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/server/src/handlers/session.ts#L254-L257 [checked in review]

A repository can also ship whole agents in `.opencode/agents/*.md` (and `.claude`, `.agents`, `mode(s)/`), including `permissions: [* allow]`, with no trust prompt. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/config/plugin/agent.ts#L22-L35 [unchecked]

The only real ceiling is admin `policies`. `ManagedPolicy.statements` reverses document order, so the highest-authority document decides if it has any matching statement. That order is MDM, then the system dir, then the Console, then well-known, global and project files. Inside one document, a later `allow` lifts an earlier `deny`. The `tool.use` hook only ever sets deny, so a policy allow grants nothing. Other policy actions remove items from catalogues: `provider.use`, `mcp.use`, `skill.use`, `websearch.use` and `integration.use`. `plugin.use` blocks a plugin from loading. Repository config cannot remove the policy plugin. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/managed-policy.ts#L9-L39 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/config/plugin/policy.ts#L98-L113 [checked in review]. Console policies are cached in memory with one-minute freshness. A failed fetch keeps the last copy, but nothing is persisted, so a process that starts while the Console is unreachable has no Console policies. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/plugin/provider/opencode.ts#L290-L301 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/shared-cache.ts#L21-L28 [checked in review; fail-open at startup inferred]

**Shell parsing.** `ShellParse.scan` is a hand-written, character-level scanner. It uses no tree-sitter and no bash library, and it has a 64 KiB input cap and a substitution depth of 32. It has a PowerShell dialect, used for `pwsh` or `powershell`, and POSIX-family dialects `bash`, `zsh` and `posix` (anything else, including `sh`, is `posix`). It returns either `scanned`, a list of simple commands, or `opaque` with one of nine reasons. An opaque result fails the tool with "Portable shell scanner cannot analyze command" before anything runs, so parsing fails closed. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/shell/parse.ts#L162-L171 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/shell/scan.ts#L3-L29 [checked in review; scanner body read only in part]. Windows `cmd.exe` (the last fallback) is scanned with POSIX rules. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/shell/select.ts#L96-L109 [checked in review]

The scanner handles these forms:
- Chains (`&&`, `||`, `|`, `;`, newlines, `if`/`for`/`case` bodies) yield one resource per simple command.
- `$(...)` and backticks are scanned recursively, and their inner commands become extra resources.
- `eval`, `set -k`, alias definitions, evaluation-enabling zsh options and `compgen -C/-F` are refused.
- Declarations (`export`, `declare`, `local`, `readonly`, `typeset`, `unset`) are not resources at all. So `export PATH=/tmp/x:$PATH; git status` asks only about `git status`.
- A scan that yields zero commands runs no shell check at all.

https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/shell/scan.ts#L376-L438 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/shell/parse.ts#L173-L175 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/tool/plugin/shell.ts#L147-L153 [checked in review]

A resource is the command's source text, matched by glob. That leaves four things the scanner cannot see:
- Redirects stay inside the resource, so `git status *` also allows `git status > ~/.bashrc`. Redirect targets are never checked against `edit` or `external_directory`.
- Wrappers and interpreters (`bash -c`, `python -c`, `node -e`, `xargs`, `find -exec`, `sudo`) are opaque single resources. Only `builtin` and `command` are unwrapped, and only to find `cd`.
- zsh still reads `.zshenv`. [inferred]
- Commands inside scripts the agent wrote and then runs.

https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/test/shell-parse-compatibility.test.ts#L133-L156 [checked in review]. `cd` targets are resolved lexically against the tool's workdir, not against an earlier `cd` in the same chain, and are checked as `external_directory`. Containment uses `path.relative`, so a symlink inside the project that points outside counts as internal. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/shell/parse.ts#L542-L563 [unchecked] https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/util/src/fs-util.ts#L269-L272 [checked in review]. There is no OS sandbox under any of this. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/shell.ts#L370-L382 [absence confirmed]

**Approval binding and "always".** A reply is bound only to the request ID. The three replies behave like this:
- `once` allows this request only.
- `reject` fails this request and every other pending request of the same session. With a message, the failures carry the feedback and the model continues. A plain decline is thrown as a defect, so a tool cannot catch it and turn it into model-visible output.
- `always` writes the request's `save` patterns into SQLite as allow rows for the current project. They apply to every agent of the project and never expire. It then re-evaluates all pending requests and auto-approves those now allowed.

https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/permission.ts#L306-L366 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/permission/saved.ts#L59-L84 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/permission.ts#L271-L284 [checked in review]

The saved pattern is usually much wider than what the user saw:
- Most tools save `["*"]`. One "always" on reading a single `.env` file saves `read:*`, which allows every read, every `.env` included, for every agent, permanently. The exception is `external_directory`, which saves the project-root glob.
- For shell, the saved pattern is an arity-table prefix plus ` *`. Commands not in the table keep only their first word. So `sudo apt update` saves `sudo *`, `env FOO=1 make` saves `env *`, `bash -c '...'` saves `bash *`, and `python -c '...'` saves `python -c *`.

https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/file-access.ts#L139-L155 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/shell/parse.ts#L603-L609 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/test/shell-parse.test.ts#L8-L19 [checked in review]. An admin policy deny still beats a saved "always".

**Model-based approvers.** None. Nothing in the permission path calls a model. The only model "judge" is the `/goal` reviewer, and it does not authorise tools. [inferred from search; not checked in review]

**Model-supplied flags.** Not in the files read. The tool schemas described carry no approval flag set by the model. The `subagent` tool's `background` and `model` inputs do not touch permissions. An `execute.before` repair hook coerces malformed input and maps a call to `bash` onto `shell`. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/plugin/tool-input-repair.ts#L8-L24 [unchecked]

**Plan and read-only agents.** The plan agent adds `question allow`, `edit * deny` and `edit ~/.opencode/plan/* allow` on top of the base allow-all. Nothing denies `shell`, `subagent` or `execute` (Code Mode). The rest is prompt: a hook rewrites blocked-edit errors into a friendlier message, and a `<system-reminder>` that forbids edits "or ask a subagent to do so" is re-injected every request if compaction drops it. So plan can edit files through `sed -i` or a redirect, can launch `general` (which may edit), and is overridden by any global `edit allow`. The `explore` subagent is described as read-only: its rules deny `*` and then allow shell, grep, glob, webfetch, websearch, read and `external_directory`. Its read-only shell is therefore prompt-only. In short, plan mode is enforced in code for the edit tools only, and by prompt for everything else. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/plugin/plan.ts#L33-L53 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/plugin/plan.ts#L55-L92 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/plugin/agent.ts#L74-L93 [checked in review]

**Sub-agent inheritance.** The `subagent` tool runs these steps:
1. Reject `primary` agents.
2. Assert the parent's `subagent` permission for the target agent ID.
3. Create a child session with `parentID`.

`Session.create` sets `permissions: input.permissions ?? parent?.permissions`, so the child copies the parent's session-level rules when it is created. The child's effective rules are `merge(child agent rules, inherited session rules)`. Its agent rules are its own: `general` is allow-all minus `question` and `subagent`. A child can therefore be broader than a plan-mode parent. The copy is taken once, so narrowing the parent later does not narrow an existing child. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/session.ts#L311-L314 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/session/context.ts#L142-L146 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/tool/plugin/subagent.ts#L140-L152 [corrected in review: the safety file first said children do not copy session rules; they do, as a creation-time copy]. Depth defaults to 1 (`experimental.subagent_depth`). `general` and `explore` carry `subagent: deny`. There is no concurrency cap. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/tool/plugin/subagent.ts#L123-L139 [checked in review; concurrency absence partly checked]. Headless `run` with `--auto` answers `once` for the whole session tree, children included. Children with safety disabled outright: not in the files read.

**Bypass paths.**
- *Per-tool authorisation.* The registry only hides tools that are wholly denied (`resource *`, deny). Each tool calls `permission.assert` itself, so a plugin tool that never asserts runs unchecked. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/tool.ts#L389-L392 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/tool/AGENTS.md#L51-L55 [checked in review]
- *Command templates.* `` !`cmd` `` blocks in custom commands run in the shell with no permission call, no `shell.create.before` hook and no policy hook. `$ARGUMENTS` is substituted first, so argument text that contains `` !`...` `` also runs. [inferred] Command files load automatically from the project's `.opencode/commands/`. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/config/plugin/command.ts#L165-L227 [checked in review]
- *Project plugins.* Plugins in `plugin(s)/` under the project's `.opencode` (also `.claude` and `.agents`) are dynamically imported into the server with no trust prompt. They can register `permission.evaluate` hooks. The only gate is an admin `plugin.use` policy. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/config/plugin/source.ts#L125-L136 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/plugin/module.ts#L168-L184 [checked in review; import itself not traced]
- *Code Mode.* `execute` is a custom acorn-based interpreter. Its nested calls do go through each tool's own `assert`. MCP tools assert `<server>_<tool>` on `*`. `fetch` asserts `webfetch`, refuses `file:` and URLs with embedded credentials, and re-checks each redirect hop against deny rules. Neither `fetch` nor `webfetch` blocks loopback or private addresses. Denying `execute` does not remove its tools; they become direct tools. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/codemode/tool.ts#L126-L154 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/codemode/fetch.ts#L17-L56 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/tool.ts#L265-L270 [checked in review]
- *Self-approval through the API.* Outside `stdio` mode the server password stays in `process.env` and is inherited by every shell. In service mode it sits in a 0600 file that the agent, running as the same user, can read. A prompt-injected agent could read it and call the local API to answer "always" to its own pending request or to set allow-all session rules. [inferred] https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/cli/src/server-process.ts#L85-L97 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/cli/src/services/service-config.ts#L175-L191 [checked in review for env handling]
- *Environment.* Shells inherit the full server environment. `shell.create.before` hooks may edit it before the check. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/shell.ts#L331-L348 [checked in review]

**Hooks in the permission path.** `permission.evaluate` hooks run in registration order and mutate a shared event. They can turn ask into allow, allow into deny, or ask into deny. They never see configured denies, because those return earlier. Their type has no failure channel: only `tool.execute.before` may fail. A hook that throws becomes a defect and the call fails rather than runs, but this is inferred from the Effect types; the Promise adapter was not traced. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/plugin/hooks.ts#L21-L30 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/permission.ts#L190-L201 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/plugin/src/effect/permission.ts#L10-L19 [checked in review; fail-closed inferred]. Hooks have no timeout: not in the files read.

**Remote answering.** Every client authenticates with one shared secret, presented one of three ways: Basic auth with fixed user `opencode`, a cookie, or `?auth_token=` in the URL. The password is compared with plain `===`. Authorisation returns only a boolean, and handlers receive no identity. A pairing code (16 bytes, 5 minutes, single use) yields a 30-day token whose HMAC key is derived from the password. That token works anywhere the password does and can only be revoked by rotating the password. `--remote` exposes the server through OpenTunnel at a secret subdomain, and the password is still required there. https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/server/src/middleware/authorization.ts#L13-L40 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/server/src/auth.ts#L33-L37 https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/server/src/auth.ts#L39-L55 [checked in review]. As a result, any holder of the password or a paired phone can answer any pending request by its ID. The reply names the request ID, not the action, and it is not tied to a user or device. Whether `permission.reply` is authorised beyond the shared password: not settled.

### OpenClaw

**Scope.** OpenClaw is a TypeScript monorepo, pinned at `8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881`. It runs one Gateway process that owns sessions, the agent loop, tools, approvals and the chat-channel plugins. Paired nodes connect with `role: node` and run `system.run` (shell commands) on their own machines. Cloud workers use a separate, closed RPC set. The approval code lives in `src/infra/exec-approvals-*`, `src/infra/system-run-*`, `src/agents/bash-tools.exec-*`, `src/agents/exec-auto-reviewer.ts`, `src/node-host/*` and `src/gateway/node-invoke-system-run-approval.ts`. The files read do not cover Windows `cmd`/PowerShell paths, channel plugins' native approval UIs, or the OpenShell and Crabbox back ends.

**Modes and real defaults.** Exec policy has three independent axes: `host` (`auto|sandbox|gateway|node`), `security` (`deny|allowlist|full`) and `ask` (`off|on-miss|always`). The named `mode` (`deny|allowlist|ask|auto|full`) is only a projection of those three (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-approvals-core.ts#L13-L17). The defaults are open:
- `host` defaults to `auto`, which means the sandbox when one is available and the Gateway otherwise (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/bash-tools.exec-target.ts#L96-L97). The sandbox is off by default, so in practice `auto` means the Gateway.
- On the Gateway and on nodes, `security` defaults to `full` and `ask` to `off`. The approvals file's constants are `DEFAULT_SECURITY = "full"`, `DEFAULT_ASK = "off"` and `askFallback = "deny"` (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/exec-defaults.ts#L171-L203 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-approvals-config.ts#L94-L96) [corrected in review].
- For a sandbox target, `security` defaults to `deny`, but that default does not block anything. The pre-run check throws `exec denied: ... security=deny` only when `host !== "sandbox"`, when the mode is explicitly `deny`, or when security was set to `deny` explicitly. A default-configured sandboxed agent therefore runs its commands in the container with no approval (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/bash-tools.exec-run.ts#L309-L317) [corrected in review].
- An approval request times out after 30 minutes by default (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-approvals-core.ts#L265-L266) [checked in review].
- A session marked `sandbox: required` throws if the sandbox is unavailable, and refuses the `elevated`, `gateway` and `node` targets (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/bash-tools.exec-target.ts#L45-L66) [corrected in review].

Because the three axes are independent, some combinations surprise users. `requiresExecApproval` prompts when `ask=always`. With `on-miss` it prompts only when `security=allowlist` and the command is unanalyzable or misses the allow-list, so `security=full` with `ask=on-miss` never prompts. The named mode `ask` is `allowlist`+`on-miss`, `auto` is the same plus the model reviewer, and `full` is `full`+`off` (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-approvals-policy.ts#L14-L32 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-approvals-core.ts#L100-L119) [checked in review]. Interactive and headless surfaces have no separate defaults in the files read. Non-interactive runs without a reviewer are denied with `approval_required` (see the binding paragraph).

**Rule grammar and precedence.** The layers are config defaults, the approvals file, and a per-session permission mode. The approvals file can only tighten the layered policy (`minSecurity`, `maxAsk`) (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-approvals-policy.ts#L34-L42) [checked in review]. The one exception is the admin-only `full` session mode, which skips the host floors. The session permission modes map onto exec modes:
- `read-only` maps to `deny`.
- `guarded` maps to `ask`.
- `workspace` maps to `auto`.
- `full` maps to `full`.

Every mode except `full` confines file tools and `apply_patch` to the workspace. When a retained mode and the current mode differ, they intersect to the stricter one, and switching between an explicit and a default policy in the middle of a turn throws (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/session-permission-exec-mode.ts#L13-L18 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/session-permission-exec-mode.ts#L29-L64) [checked in review].

Allow-list entries work as follows (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-command-resolution.ts#L184-L231) [corrected in review]:
- A glob with a path in it is matched against the executable's resolved real path.
- A bare name matches the executable's basename only when the command token itself had no path separator. So a bare `tool` grant never covers `./tool` or `/tmp/tool`.
- An optional `argPattern` regex is matched against the arguments joined with `\0`. An invalid regex never matches.

"Safe bins" (commands allowed without approval) must resolve from trusted directories, `/bin` and `/usr/bin` by default. `jq`, the awk family, `sed`/`gsed`, and a list of shells and interpreters (bash, python, node, perl, busybox, toybox and others) are refused as safe bins by name (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-safe-bin-trust.ts#L12 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-safe-bin-semantics.ts#L4-L40 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-safe-bin-runtime-policy.ts#L21-L60) [checked in review]. A writable trusted directory only logs a warning.

Whether repository files can loosen the user's settings: no project-level approval file is in the files read. The sandbox validator's stated threat model is "local-trusted config", and nothing stops an agent with host exec at `security=full` from editing `openclaw.json` directly [inferred].

**Shell parsing before matching.** `planShellAuthorization` parses the command with `web-tree-sitter` and the `tree-sitter-bash` WASM grammar, with a 128 KiB size limit and a 500 ms progress deadline (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/command-explainer/tree-sitter-runtime.ts#L11-L58 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-authorization-plan.ts#L529-L560) [checked in review]. The plan is split into steps, groups and candidates, and risks are sorted into three sets (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-authorization-risks.ts#L6-L36):
- **Prompt-only:** `eval`, `source`, `alias` and carriers.
- **Non-reusable:** inline eval.
- **Unanalyzable:** command, process and here substitutions, heredocs, redirects, dynamic executables or assignments, line continuations, function definitions and syntax errors.

`if`, `for`, `while`, `case`, subshells, groups and backgrounding are unsupported topology. A parse error or timeout produces `unanalyzablePlan`, which means "prompt for the whole command", never "allow". Windows `cmd` is unanalyzable.

In allowlist mode, a heredoc segment, and inline eval when `strictInlineEval` is on (`python -c` and similar), require the reviewer or a human even when the allow-list matched. If the Gateway cannot render an enforced command, for example because an argument contains shell expansion, a prompt is required. With `ask=off` and `askFallback=deny` such a call is denied before a request is registered (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/bash-tools.exec-host-gateway.ts#L361-L430) [unchecked].

**Approval binding and "always".** An approval is bound in three layers (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/system-run-approval-binding.ts#L42-L90 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/system-run-approval-binding.ts#L366-L458 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/system-run-mutable-file-policy.ts#L19-L66 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/system-run-cwd-binding.ts#L15-L61) [corrected in review]:
1. **Request.** `SystemRunApprovalBinding` holds the exact argv, cwd, agentId and sessionKey, plus `envHash`, a SHA-256 over the sorted env overrides in the request. The inherited environment is not hashed. With no overrides, `envHash` is null, and overrides sent against a null binding are refused.
2. **Executable.** Every segment executable and every wrapper hop (`env`, `nice` and so on) is resolved without a cache. If the file, its directory or a symlink component is writable or owned by the current process, the file's SHA-256 is stored. Otherwise only its real path is stored. PATH and PATHEXT are recorded, and script operands are hashed too.
3. **Cwd.** The cwd's realpath and its `stat` inode identity are captured. A symlinked cwd is refused, and so are symlink components that sit in a directory the process can write. Other symlink components are accepted.

To pin the executable, the command text is rewritten: each executable span is replaced with its resolved real path, so the shell no longer searches PATH at launch (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-authorization-render.ts#L144-L191). Binding is refused for these, and the call falls back to prompting (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/system-run-approval-binding.ts#L299-L340):
- `.`/`source`;
- commands that follow `cd`/`pushd` in a chain;
- shell startup variables (`BASH_ENV`, `ENV`);
- pipelines with more than one candidate per group;
- unresolved executables.

Just before `supervisor.spawn`, `revalidateBeforeExecution` re-resolves executables with the stored PATH, re-hashes mutable files and re-stats the cwd (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/system-run-approval-binding.ts#L550-L620) [checked in review]. It reads the file by path with `readFileSync` and holds no file descriptor, so a write between the hash and `execve` is not detected [inferred].

"Allow always" writes an allow-list entry, not a glob. The entry's pattern is the executable path, and its `argPattern` holds `sha256(len cwd | cwd | argc | len arg | arg ...)` over `argv[1..]`. So the entry covers one exact argument list, in one exact cwd, for that executable path (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-command-resolution.ts#L321-L378) [corrected in review: the hash does not cover argv[0]]. Mutable operands downgrade "allow always" to one use (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/bash-tools.exec-host-gateway.ts#L458-L459). For cron runs, an `allow-always` instead creates a SQLite standing grant bound to the job, the config revision and the operation. The grant is consumed at the pre-spawn hook, and a grant-store failure falls back to a prompt (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/bash-tools.exec-host-gateway.ts#L460-L529) [unchecked]. No session-scoped "always" is in the files read.

**Model-based approver.** In `auto` mode a separate one-shot completion, "the reviewer", decides (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/exec-auto-reviewer.ts#L26-L58 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/exec-auto-reviewer.ts#L73-L76 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/exec-auto-reviewer.ts#L91-L150) [corrected in review]:
- **Settings:** temperature 0, a 1024 output-token budget (raised for reasoning models), a 30 s timeout (minimum 1 s) and a 16,000-character input cap.
- **What it sees:** command, `executionCommand`, argv, resolvedPath, cwd, env key names without values, host, reason and analysis flags, all inside `UNTRUSTED_EXEC_REQUEST_JSON` markers. Session identifiers are left out.
- **Optional transcript:** at most 40 non-user entries, 4,000 characters per user or assistant entry, 1,000 per tool entry and 24,000 in total. Each entry is labelled with its origin (operator, channel, inter_session and so on) (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/exec-auto-review-transcript.ts#L13-L18).
- **Output:** strict JSON with no extra keys, no duplicate keys and no `__proto__`. "allow" with high or unknown risk becomes "ask" (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/exec-auto-reviewer.ts#L260-L315).

Failure mode:
- A timeout, an unavailable model, a non-`stop` finish, malformed JSON, an oversized input or an exception becomes `ask` with risk unknown (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/exec-auto-reviewer.ts#L396-L516).
- With no reviewer configured, every call becomes `ask` (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/exec-auto-review.ts#L148-L159).
- Before any model call, a regex looks for text aimed at the reviewer in the command, argv, path, cwd or env keys. A hit returns `deny` with risk high (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/exec-auto-reviewer.ts#L152-L191).

The reviewer is skipped, and a human is required, in these cases (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/bash-tools.exec-host-gateway.ts#L631-L643) [unchecked]:
- `ask=always`;
- more than 64 candidates;
- a POSIX login or interactive shell startup before the inline command;
- an unpinnable dispatch chain.

A reviewer `allow-once` must carry risk low or medium. After three reviewer denials in a row the next denial goes to a human. That counter is an in-memory LRU of 256 session keys, used on the Gateway host path only and reset only by an allow (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/bash-tools.exec-host-gateway.ts#L96-L110) [checked in review]. On a node in `auto` mode, the reviewer runs on the node with the node's own model credentials and no transcript. A node-side `ask` becomes a denial, because the node has no human route (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/node-host/invoke-system-run.ts#L491-L543) [checked in review]. Which model the reviewer uses when `tools.exec.reviewer.model` is unset is not settled.

**Model-supplied flags.** Not in the files read. The only related mechanism is on the Gateway: client-supplied `approved`/`approvalDecision` flags are always stripped (see remote answering).

**Plan and read-only modes.** There is no plan mode [absence confirmed]. The `read-only` session mode is enforced in code: it sets `readOnly: true` in the core tool policy and maps exec to `deny` (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/session-permission-exec-mode.ts#L55-L66) [checked in review].

**Sub-agents.**
- Hidden children always lose `gateway, agents_list, openclaw, session_status, progress_card, automations, message, sessions_send, conversations_list/send/turn`.
- Leaves, the children at the last allowed depth, also lose `subagents` and the `sessions_*` spawn and read tools.
- "Visible", user-steerable children skip both lists.
- A child inherits the parent's tool allow and deny lists as persisted envelopes, so it cannot widen them (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/agent-tools.policy.ts#L57-L89 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/subagents/spawn/subagent-capabilities.ts#L30-L39) [checked in review].
- Spawn depth is capped at 5 and active children at 5 per parent (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/child-admission.ts#L84-L109) [corrected in review].

Whether a child inherits the exec mode and approvals, and whether a child can run with safety off, is not in the files read. Cross-agent access is open by default: session visibility is `all` and agent-to-agent messaging is enabled (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/plugin-sdk/session-visibility.ts#L132-L155) [unchecked].

**Bypass paths.**
- **Defaults:** host exec at `full`/`off`, and sandboxed exec with no approval.
- **Cron:** standing grants.
- **Environment:** host exec inherits the Gateway's `process.env` minus about 110 injection variables, so a provider key such as `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` reaches exec children (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/infra/host-env-security.ts#L38-L65) [checked in review].
- **Agent-authored skills:** the Skill Workshop plugin is on by default in `auto` mode and saves skills the agent wrote, with no human step (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/extensions/skill-workshop/src/config.ts#L26-L32) [checked in review].
- **Memory:** writes to memory files have no approval step (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/workspace.ts#L896-L917) [unchecked].
- **Cloud workers:** the worker's `worker.gatewayTool.invoke` lets a worker run Gateway-side tools for its turn [checked in review].
- **Not in the files read:** MCP, code mode and client-side tools in the approval path.

**Hooks.** Tool calls pass an ordered `before_tool_call` chain: loop admission, owner probes, trusted plugin policies, approvals, plugin hooks, final owner approval (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/agent-tools.before-tool-call.policy.ts#L1-L7). If any stage throws, the call is blocked: the chain fails closed (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/agent-tools.before-tool-call.policy.ts#L397-L408) [checked in review]. Hooks may also rewrite parameters. No hook timeout is in the files read.

**Remote answering.** Chat channels resolve approvals with `/approve` against an approver id list from config. When that list is empty, the shared helper returns `authorized: true` as an implicit same-chat fallback, so anyone in the originating chat can approve (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/plugin-sdk/approval-auth-helpers.ts#L72-L89) [checked in review]. Whether each channel honours this marker was not traced.

For node exec, the answer names the action. Step by step:
1. `sanitizeSystemRunParamsForForwarding` strips the client's `approved`/`approvalDecision`.
2. It looks up the server record by `runId`.
3. It requires the record's `nodeId` to equal the target, and the requester's device id (or connId) to equal the recorded one.
4. It requires argv, cwd, agentId, sessionKey and envHash to match the stored binding.
5. It requires the decision to still be active.
6. `allow-once` is consumed atomically by `consumeAllowOnce`, and a reviewer allow is forwarded as `approvalSource: auto-review` (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/gateway/node-invoke-system-run-approval.ts#L197-L252 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/gateway/node-invoke-system-run-approval.ts#L434-L452) [checked in review].
7. The node then recomputes `security`/`ask` from its own config and approvals file. It refuses an approval without a `systemRunPlan` that matches argv, command text, cwd, agentId and sessionKey, and refuses a delayed approval if its own policy snapshot changed.
8. But `approved === true` sets `approvedByAsk`, which satisfies both "ask" and "allow-list miss". Only a local `security=deny` holds against a Gateway-asserted approval (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/node-host/exec-policy.ts#L60-L117 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/node-host/invoke-system-run.ts#L349-L357) [checked in review].

Before `node.invoke`, the Gateway also requires the command to be in its allow-list, to be non-private and to be declared by the node (https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/gateway/node-command-policy.ts#L460-L480) [checked in review]. Who may call `exec.approval.resolve` (the `operator.approvals` scope) was not traced.

### Hermes Agent

**Scope.** `NousResearch/hermes-agent`, pinned at `95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02`. Hermes is a Python harness. One `AIAgent` process on the host runs the agent loop, the tool registry and every approval decision. A "terminal backend" (local, Docker, SSH, Modal, Daytona, Vercel) decides only where shell commands run. The profile gateway owns execution; the CLI, TUI, Desktop app, ACP adapter and dashboard are viewers attached to it. Hermes is single-user by design. Command approval does not sit in the generic tool dispatcher. It sits inside the `terminal` tool and `execute_code`, and the combined entry point is `check_all_command_guards` (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L1276-L1323) [checked in review].

**Modes and real defaults.** `approvals.mode` ships as `smart` in the defaults file. If the key is missing or unknown, the code falls back to `manual`. A YAML `off` parses as `False` and maps to off (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/hermes_cli/config_defaults.py#L1646-L1671, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_context.py#L276-L315) [corrected in review; the correction concerns logging, below]. A human prompt times out after 300 s. Timeout, cancel, a failed notify and an interrupt all return BLOCKED with the message "silence is not consent" (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L988-L1009, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L1035-L1047). The CLI prompt treats any unknown answer as deny (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_prompt.py#L162-L175).

The per-surface defaults are:
- Cron runs, `-q` single queries and the "unattended" platforms (webhook, msgraph_webhook, api_server) use `cron_mode`, `single_query_mode` and `unattended_mode`. All three default to `deny`, and these contexts never park a prompt (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_context.py#L360-L384) [checked in review].
- `hermes -z` auto-approves. It marks an "unattended turn", and `_yolo_active()` treats that as YOLO (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L388-L394, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/gateway/session_finite.py#L27-L41).
- A process with no interactive marker, no gateway and no unattended context also auto-approves. "Interactive" is a contextvar that falls back to the env var `HERMES_INTERACTIVE`, so embedding Hermes as a library without setting it runs flagged commands (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_context.py#L48-L51, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L1300-L1308) [corrected in review]. On the terminal path this approval is silent: it returns approved with no log, and it covers every command that passed the floors, not only the flagged ones. Only the shared `_run_approval_gate`, used by the file-tool and plugin gates, logs a warning (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L1138-L1150).
- The API server is an unattended platform with no `/approve` channel, so it defaults to deny (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/gateway/platforms/api_server.py#L3907-L3931) [checked in review].
- ACP `session/new` creates sessions with an edit-approval policy of `ask` (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/acp_adapter/gateway_server.py#L271-L291) [unchecked].

**Order of checks, rule grammar and precedence.** For one terminal command, `check_all_command_guards` runs these steps in order [checked in review]:
1. Container fast path. On isolated backends it returns after the user deny globs.
2. Floors: hardline patterns, runtime self-delete, the `sudo -S` guard and user deny globs (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L1189-L1212).
3. A prepared batch verdict.
4. YOLO or `off`: approve.
5. The permanent `command_allowlist`, matched by exact text or glob.
6. If no human can answer, the unattended modes decide.
7. Dangerous-pattern findings, minus session or permanent grants.
8. The smart guardian, then the human (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L914-L933).

The `force` parameter is internal: the model-facing handler never passes it (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/terminal_tool.py#L1735-L1747). Other pre-exec guards (gateway lifecycle, dangerous workdir, self-repo, locked skills) run on a bounded worker and fail closed on timeout (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/terminal_tool.py#L1540-L1562). One ordering flaw: the backend, which for Docker may be a fresh container, is acquired before the guards run (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/terminal_tool.py#L1520-L1522 versus https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/terminal_tool.py#L1563-L1565).

The user's rules come in two kinds:
- **Deny list.** `approvals.deny` holds case-insensitive `fnmatch` globs. They are checked before YOLO and on every backend (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_floors.py#L23-L42) [checked in review]. An unreadable config yields no rules, so the deny list fails open (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_floors.py#L30-L33).
- **Allow list.** `command_allowlist` holds command text or globs. A compound command never matches it (see the binding paragraph below).

There is one config file per profile, `config.yaml`. The files read describe no project, admin or managed policy layer [not in the files read]. Repository files cannot loosen approval settings through any mechanism described. Project context files (repo AGENTS.md, .cursorrules, .hermes.md) are scanned for injection, and a file with any hit is replaced by a `[BLOCKED: …]` marker. The user's own SOUL.md is loaded with a warning instead (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/agent/prompt_builder.py#L99-L111) [checked in review].

Hermes protects its own policy file in three ways:
- The file tools refuse writes to the active `config.yaml` (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/file_tools_write_guards.py#L166-L173).
- The shell path treats `tee` or a redirect into `~/.hermes/config.yaml` or `.env` only as a dangerous pattern, which an approval or a smart APPROVE can pass. The config is mtime-cached, so a successful shell write changes policy mid-session (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_detection.py#L26-L29).
- Protected instruction files need approval for `write_file`/`patch` even under YOLO. This applies only to the file tools, and only while `security.protected_instruction_files` is true, which is the default (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/file_tools_write_guards.py#L196-L211) [corrected in review].

**Shell parsing before matching.** Hermes matches regexes over several "variants" of the command string. It does not use a real shell parser. The variants are:
- the normalised command;
- a quote-aware copy with command starts marked;
- `-c` payloads extracted from interpreters;
- Windows backslash paths;
- deobfuscated command words such as `r\m` and `r''m`

(https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_detection.py#L1667-L1745) [checked in review].

Command-name rules are anchored to command position (`_CMDPOS`). That means the start of the command, a newline, a backtick or `$(`, optionally after `sudo`, `env`, `exec`, `nohup`, `setsid` or `time` (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_detection.py#L99-L103).

For deny globs, each segment is projected separately: each command word's executable (and its basename), followed by the whitespace-collapsed rest of the segment. `env` and `-c` payloads are pushed back onto the work list and projected again. This is how `sudo`, `env`, `nohup` and `sh -c` are seen through (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_detection.py#L1623-L1664) [checked in review].

The hardline floor is the set of patterns blocked even under YOLO. It covers:
- recursive `rm` of `/`, system roots or `$HOME`;
- `mkfs`;
- `dd of=/dev/sd*`, and redirects to raw block devices;
- the fork bomb;
- `kill -1`;
- shutdown, reboot, halt, poweroff and `init 0/6`

(https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_detection.py#L124-L157). Piping remote content to a shell is an approvable dangerous pattern, not hardline (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_detection.py#L301-L324).

"Fails closed on parse failure" means two narrow things:
