- A parser limit is crossed: more than 128,000 chars, more than 4,096 chars with no separator, or 25,000 or more separators (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_detection.py#L933-L948).
- A `grep` operand cannot be lexed (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_detection.py#L1027-L1041).

When a parser limit blocks a command, the payload is saved under `~/.hermes/cache/blocked-scripts/` and the agent is told to run `bash <file>`, which goes through normal approval (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_floors.py#L54-L85).

The matcher is not an interpreter. A `python -c` with an encoded payload defeats it, and Hermes says so. The comments also record 118 benign greps among 125 hardline blocks in one week.

The guard sees the command after Hermes strips a trailing `&`. The later rewrite of `A && B &` in `execute()` happens after approval (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/terminal_tool.py#L1512-L1519, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/environments/base.py#L700-L717) [checked in review].

**Approval binding and "always".** An approval is bound to a pattern class, not to a command. A finding is a pair `(pattern_key, description)`, and the key is the rule's description string, for example "recursive delete" (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L731-L740). The answers work like this:
- "session" adds the key to an in-memory per-session set, which is lost on restart.
- "always" also writes the key into `command_allowlist` in `config.yaml` (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L474-L487, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L543-L574) [checked in review].

So one "always" on `rm -r build` approves every future recursive delete, in every session. Content rules (credential upload, invisible Unicode) still need their own consent. Removing an entry by hand takes effect on the next check (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L414-L429). Hand-written allowlist entries never match compound commands. A quote-aware scan refuses `;`, `|`, `&`, `$(`, backticks, and quoted control characters when a `-c`/`-e` option is present (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_floors.py#L140-L200).

Deferred approvals of memory and skill writes are stored as staged records under `pending/{memory,skills}/<id>.json`. A memory replace or remove pins the full matched entry when it is staged (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/memory_tool.py#L64-L82) [checked in review]. A skill replay re-runs `skill_manage` with no base-hash check. A targeted `patch` still has to match its `old_string`, but full rewrites and fuzzy matches are unguarded (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/skill_manager_tool.py#L716-L722, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/skill_manager_tool.py#L549-L553) [corrected in review]. Both write gates default to off (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/hermes_cli/config_defaults.py#L1295-L1302), and both fail open if their module cannot be imported (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/memory_tool.py#L84-L90) [checked in review].

**Model-based approver.** The "smart" guardian is an auxiliary model call. It runs only on commands the regexes already flagged, and works as follows:
- Unquoted `#` comments are stripped, and the command is wrapped in `<command>` tags (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_smart.py#L36-L65).
- The model is asked for one word, APPROVE, DENY or ESCALATE, at `temperature=0` and `max_tokens=16`, with reasoning off (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_smart.py#L17-L32, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_smart.py#L101-L146) [checked in review].
- Operator rules from `approvals.smart_policy` go into the system prompt only.
- The model defaults to the main model.
- The timeout is `auxiliary.approval.timeout`, 30 s by default (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/hermes_cli/config_defaults.py#L751-L751).

The verdicts mean:
- APPROVE runs this one command and persists nothing.
- DENY blocks at once on unattended platforms. With a human present, the human may override it once (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L882-L911, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L945-L951).
- ESCALATE, an empty reply, an unknown word, a timeout or any exception falls through to the human prompt.

After 3 consecutive DENYs (`denial_breaker_threshold`), the deny text gains "CIRCUIT BREAKER: STOP"; nothing else changes (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L64-L117). The guardian receives the raw, unredacted command, so secrets in a command reach the auxiliary provider (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_smart.py#L149-L170).

**Model-supplied flags.** The model cannot pass `force`, which is internal-only (see above). In `delegate_task` it cannot set the child's iteration budget: a supplied `max_iterations` is ignored, and the `role` argument is accepted but ignored, because capability comes from depth (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/delegate_tool.py#L607-L615) [checked in review]. Model-facing `cronjob` cannot set `model`, `provider` or `base_url` (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/cronjob_tools.py#L1233-L1242) [checked in review]. No "requires approval" or justification flag on tool calls is described [not in the files read].

**Plan mode.** `/plan` is prompt-only. It sends a "PLAN MODE: planning only … do not run mutating terminal commands" text as an ordinary turn. No tool is removed or gated, and only the normal approval layer applies (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/agent/plan_prompt.py#L1-L27) [unchecked].

The real read-only enforcement in Hermes is the background-review fork. Its advertised tool list stays byte-identical to the parent's, but dispatch allows only `skills`, optionally `memory`, and read-only `read_file`/`search_files`. Terminal and file writes are denied, and dangerous-command prompts auto-deny (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/agent/background_review.py#L1116-L1131, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/agent/background_review.py#L1067-L1071) [checked in review]. An unattended review cannot patch or remove memory even with approval off; those operations are staged, and a staging failure denies (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/memory_tool.py#L173-L219) [checked in review].

**Sub-agents.** A child's tools are the parent's toolsets minus `delegate_task`, `clarify`, `memory`, `send_message` and `cronjob_manage`. Explicit `toolsets` are intersected with the parent's, so a child never gains a tool its parent lacks (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/delegate_tool_toolsets.py#L14-L123) [checked in review]. A child is built with `clarify_callback=None`, so it cannot ask the user.

A dangerous-command prompt in a child thread resolves to deny. Setting `delegation.subagent_auto_approve: true` makes it approve instead, which runs children with dangerous commands auto-approved (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/delegate_tool_config.py#L41-L61) [checked in review]. Whether a child shares the parent's session grants is [not in the files read]. Nesting is allowed only with `orchestrator_enabled` and while `child_depth < max_spawn_depth`. The default depth is 1, and there is no hard ceiling (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/delegate_tool_config.py#L204-L212).

A session branch (fork) copies the parent's policy, except that edit approval resets to `ask` and editor MCP servers are rebound under the child's scope (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/hermes_state_mutation_branch.py#L34-L49) [unchecked].

**Bypass paths.**
- **Container backends skip almost every check.** `_should_skip_container_guards` skips the hardline floor, the sudo guard and dangerous-pattern prompts on Modal, Daytona, Vercel, flagged plugin backends, and Docker without host access. Only the deny globs remain (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L1161-L1175, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L1282-L1287) [checked in review]. The "host access" predicate ignores `-v /:/host` in `docker_extra_args` (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/terminal_tool.py#L145-L162) [checked in review].
- **`execute_code` escapes per-command approval.** Its scripts can call `subprocess` and `os.system` directly. The whole script is approved at once on isolated backends, under YOLO or `off`, in an interactive CLI and in a bare library context. Whole-script approval is asked only on gateway or ask surfaces. On local the script runs in a host Python kernel whose only containment is an env scrub (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L1363-L1394) [checked in review].
- **The local backend has no OS sandbox** (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/environments/local.py#L1149-L1168) [absence confirmed].
- **The ambient auto-approve paths:** `-z`, and a library context without `HERMES_INTERACTIVE`.
- **MCP tool descriptions are scanned, but a hit is only logged.** The tool is still registered (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/mcp_tool_registration.py#L288-L300) [checked in review]. MCP tools pass through any approval layer other than the untrusted-output wrap [inferred; not stated in the source].
- **The `tool_call` bridge.** The batch planner has to peel the `tool_call` wrapper to see the real tool, which shows that decisions keyed on the wrapper name are a real bug class (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/agent/tool_dispatch_helpers.py#L137-L150) [unchecked].
- **The skill lock is advisory.** It parses the command text for write targets, and a script that writes through its own logic is not seen (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/skill_lock.py#L10-L13) [unchecked].

**Hooks in the permission path.** `pre_tool_call` plugin hooks run before dispatch and may return one of three directives:
- `block`;
- `approve`, which escalates the call to the human gate; a plugin cannot auto-approve;
- `modify`, which shallow-merges new arguments. The rewritten command is what the terminal guard then checks.

`block` takes precedence over `approve`. A hook that raises is swallowed and the call proceeds with the original arguments, so a crashing hook fails open (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/agent/tool_executor.py#L662-L676) [checked in review]. An `approve` whose gate raises, denies or times out blocks: `request_tool_approval` runs with `fail_closed_when_no_human=True` (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/hermes_cli/plugins.py#L1198-L1227, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval.py#L1246-L1271).

Shell-script hooks need first-use consent per (event, command) pair. They fail open unless they are marked `fail_closed`, and only `pre_tool_call` can block, by exit code 2 or a JSON reply (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/agent/shell_hooks.py#L37-L44) [checked in review].

**Remote answering.** The gateway (chat channels) and the CLI show `redact_approval_display(command)`, but the raw command is what runs. So the person can approve text that differs from the argv (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_prompt.py#L110-L127) [unchecked]. A gateway card starts as "deny-only" and gains an approve option only once the renderer confirms that the card shows the whole command (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_gateway_wait.py#L182-L188) [unchecked]. Identical concurrent gateway approvals are coalesced into one prompt (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/tools/approval_gateway_wait.py#L155-L167) [unchecked]. Prompts from parallel tool calls are serialised, and human-wait time is excluded from the batch deadline (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/agent/tool_executor.py#L479-L497) [unchecked].

Over ACP, approvals become `request_permission` calls with option ids `allow_once`, `allow_session`, `allow_always`, `deny` and `deny_always`. ACP has no session scope, so `allow_session` is sent with kind `allow_always` and the meaning lives in the id. An unknown id or a non-allow outcome maps to deny. The wait is `approvals.timeout` (300 s), and a timeout is reported as `"timeout"`, separately from a denial (https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/acp_adapter/permissions.py#L17-L19, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/acp_adapter/permissions.py#L67-L74, https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/95fc3e668fc1e13ad05d22a4f02fccc6a6d6ce02/acp_adapter/permissions.py#L117-L124) [unchecked]. The files do not say whether an answer is bound to a request id, or whether a replayed answer is rejected [not in the files read].

### Oh My Pi (`omp`)

**Scope.** Repository `can1357/oh-my-pi`, pinned at `e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906`. All of omp runs in one Bun/TypeScript process: the TUI or headless host (RPC, ACP, print, collab), the agent loop, every tool and every extension. A Rust N-API addon (`pi-natives`, which embeds the `pi-shell` bash engine) runs shell commands for that process. There is no separate executor process and no privilege boundary between the loop and its tools. There is also no OS sandbox: no Landlock, seccomp, bubblewrap, Seatbelt or namespace code exists in the repository. The only isolation is `pi-iso`, which makes copy-on-write worktrees so sub-agent changes can be merged back. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/file-write-fallback.ts#L1-L15 [checked in review] [absence confirmed]. So the approval policy is the only thing between the model and the user's full rights. That policy is enforced in one place. `ExtensionToolWrapper` wraps every registry tool, and the runner that drives it is always created, so the gate cannot be left out: https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/sdk.ts#L3740-L3747 and https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/sdk.ts#L3889-L3894. The decision is made by `resolveApproval` and `resolvePolicy` in `tools/approval.ts`: https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L346-L390.

**Modes and real defaults.** The setting `tools.approvalMode` has four values, not the three the documentation lists: `always-ask`, `always-ask-cached` ("Ask and remember"), `write` and `yolo`. Each mode sets the highest tier that runs without asking. A tier is the risk class a tool declares (`read`, `write` or `exec`). The highest tier per mode is `read` for `always-ask`, `read` for `always-ask-cached`, `write` for `write` and `exec` for `yolo`. The default is `yolo`, so out of the box every tier runs without a prompt. An unrecognised value in the config also falls back to `yolo`. Only a call that has no settings object at all fails closed to `always-ask`. `--auto-approve` forces `yolo` but keeps the user's per-tool policy map. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/settings.ts#L314-L319 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L153-L158 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L97-L113 [checked in review]. Defaults by surface:
- **TUI and CLI:** `yolo`.
- **Headless (`omp -p`, `--mode json`):** a call that needs a prompt throws "requires approval but no interactive UI available", so it fails closed. The error message suggests setting `tools.approvalMode: yolo`, which pushes users toward the weakest setting. Under the default `yolo`, only three things reach this path: restricted folders, explicit `prompt` policies, and provider safety checks (computer-use). Safety checks prompt in every mode, `yolo` included. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/extensibility/extensions/wrapper.ts#L404-L409 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/extensibility/extensions/wrapper.ts#L446-L471 [checked in review]
- **ACP (an editor acting as client):** if the client supports `requestPermission`, omp re-resolves `bash` calls with the mode forced to `always-ask`, unless the user explicitly chose auto-approve. So a default-config ACP session still asks the editor before running exec calls. In explicit auto-approve mode, tools other than bash skip the client prompt unless a user policy says otherwise. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/session/session-tools.ts#L1174-L1217 [checked in review]
- **RPC (`--mode rpc`, newline-delimited JSON over stdio):** no authentication. The trust boundary is whichever parent process owns the pipes. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-types.ts#L48-L167 [checked in review]
- **Desktop app, server or gateway approvals:** not in the files read. The auth gateway is a model-provider proxy, not an approval surface.

**Rule grammar and precedence.** `resolvePolicy` decides in a fixed order. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L426-L464 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L466-L513 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L366-L390 [checked in review]
1. If the tool's own decision is `deny`, the result is `deny`.
2. If the user's `tools.approval.<key>` is `deny`, the result is `deny`.
3. In `yolo`: use the tool's explicit `policy` if it has one, else the user's policy, else `allow`. The tool's `override` flag is ignored.
4. Outside `yolo`: `override: true` forces a prompt (unless the tool said `allow`). If there is no override, the order is the explicit tool policy, then the user policy, then a cached grant, then a comparison of the call's tier with the mode's highest tier.
5. Last, `resolveApproval` checks `tools.restrictedFolders`. If the call touches a restricted folder, any result other than `deny` becomes a per-call prompt that cannot be remembered. This applies in every mode.

A tool that declares no `approval` is treated as `exec`. When an MCP tool is renamed, it carries over only `deny` or `prompt` from its legacy name, never `allow`.

Settings are layered global, then project, then a `--config` overlay, then runtime overrides. Layers are deep-merged, and arrays and scalars are replaced, not merged. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/config/settings.ts#L4287-L4291. **Repository files can loosen the user's settings.** The project layer is every `settings.json` or `config.yml` found in the repository's `.omp/`. It is merged with no key filtering: only `dropSettingsGroupShadows` and a filter for invalid `modelRoles` are applied. The approval mode, the per-tool map and the restricted folders are then read from the merged view. A repository can therefore ship `tools: {approvalMode: yolo, approval: {bash: allow}, restrictedFolders: []}` and undo a user's `always-ask`. The settings UI hides the restricted-folders editor when that value came from the project, but the value is still applied. Settings watch project files, so an agent with write access can also create such a file mid-session. `mcp.enableProjectConfig` is loosened the same way. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/discovery/builtin.ts#L898-L928 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/config/settings.ts#L2800-L2814 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/config/settings.ts#L4310-L4332 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L104-L112 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/config/settings-ui.ts#L44-L44 [checked in review].

There is one exception. Bash re-checks `bash.patterns` against `Settings.grantAuthority()`, a view that includes the global, `--config` and runtime layers but not the project layer. The code comment gives the reason: "repository settings files are writable by the agent". So a project cannot lift an operator's bash `deny` or `prompt`. No other tool-approval setting uses that view. The per-call model-grant settings for `task` also use it. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/bash.ts#L639-L656 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/config/settings.ts#L843-L866 [corrected in review: the original said "nothing else"; it now says no other tool-approval setting, because model grants also use `grantAuthority()`].

**Shell parsing before matching.** A `bash.patterns` rule has the form `{match, approval}`. `*` is the only wildcard, and each rule compiles to an anchored regex over the command with whitespace normalised. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/bash.ts#L253-L263 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/bash.ts#L106-L148 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/bash.ts#L291-L321 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/shell-tokenize.ts#L14-L28 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/shell-tokenize.ts#L243-L280 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/exec/settings.ts#L117-L128 [checked in review]
- `allow` and `deny` are matched differently. An `allow` rule must match the whole command. It never applies when the command contains unquoted shell control characters (`; & | < > \` $ ( )`), or quoted control characters together with a re-interpreting option such as `-c` or `-e`. A `deny` or `prompt` rule fires if it matches the whole command or any segment. `tokenizeShellSegments` makes the segments by splitting on `; & | ( )` and newlines after quotes are removed.
- With `bash.allowCompoundCommands`, which is off by default, a strict parser for literal `&&` chains lets each segment be matched separately. It rejects shell interpreters, stateful commands (`SHELL_INTERPRETER_COMMANDS`, `SHELL_STATEFUL_COMMANDS`) and any expansion.
- **What the parser cannot see.** It does not look inside backticks. It does not unwrap wrappers or interpreters for `deny`. A `deny` rule `rm *` does not fire on `bash -c "rm -rf x"`: the segment is `bash -c rm -rf x`, and the anchored regex needs `^rm`. It also misses `/bin/rm`, `command rm` and `env rm` [inferred]. The `allow` side is the conservative side.
- There is no parse-failure path: the splitter is a lexer and does not reject input. The files read do not describe a separate fail mode.

There is also a list of dangerous commands. `CRITICAL_BASH_PATTERNS` holds 21 regexes over the raw command text. They cover recursive `rm`, `chmod` and `chown` on an absolute path, `--no-preserve-root`, `sudo rm`, a fork bomb, writes to `/dev/sd*`, `mkfs`, `dd of=/dev/...`, `shred /dev/`, `cryptsetup`, writes or `tee` to `/etc/passwd`, `shadow` or `sudoers`, `curl`, `wget` or `fetch` piped into a shell or `eval`, `kill -9 1`, `shutdown`, `poweroff`, `reboot`, `halt`, `init 0`, and `nc -e`/`-c`. A match returns `{tier: "exec", override: true}` with no `policy`. `yolo` ignores `override`, so **the list has no effect in the default mode**. Because the regexes see raw text, `bash -c "rm -rf /x"` still matches, but `rm -rf ~` and `rm -rf "$HOME"` do not. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/bash.ts#L197-L244 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/bash.ts#L657-L660 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L446-L464 [checked in review]. Two optional steering aids are both off by default. `bashInterceptor.enabled` refuses commands that have a dedicated tool (for example `cat`, `grep` or `sed -i`). `bash.gitGuard` refuses `stash`, `reset --hard` and checkout, switch or restore in a main checkout. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/exec/settings.ts#L143-L154 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/exec/settings.ts#L170-L180 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/bash.ts#L621-L631 [unchecked]

**Approval binding and "always" semantics.** A prompt can be answered in three ways beyond a one-time yes. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L598-L612 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L176-L214 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval-cache.ts#L18-L27 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval-cache.ts#L89-L109 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L906-L919 [checked in review]
- **"Approve for this session"** is offered only for prompts raised by the mode. It is keyed by `subject\0tier`, so approving a write never covers an exec by the same tool, and it lives in memory only. For bash, the subject is the tool, not the command. One "approve bash for this session" therefore covers every later ordinary bash call at that tier.
- **Cached grants (`always-ask-cached` only)** are keyed by `tier\0path`. The path is the exact target or its folder, with symlinks resolved, and a grant matches the call's tier or a higher one. A grant lasts for the session, or for "always" it is stored in the SQLite file `approval-cache.db` with no expiry. A folder grant covers the folder's direct entries, not its subtree. The prompt shows what a folder grant would cover.
- **Never rememberable:** critical overrides, explicit `prompt` policies and provider safety checks never offer a session or "always" answer.
- **Failure handling:** any answer other than an explicit approval counts as `deny`. If reading the cache fails, nothing is treated as granted (fail closed). If writing the cache fails, the error is only logged.

**Model-based approvers.** The files read show no model-based approver or classifier in the approval path. The nearest thing is TTSR (rules matched against the model's output stream). A TTSR rule that has a `question` is judged by an LLM after a message completes: one request per output covers all eligible questions, with content capped at 32,000 tokens. A "yes" produces only a non-interrupting warning, the run waits at most 5 s for pending judgments, and judge failures are logged and dropped. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/session/ttsr-coordinator.ts#L564-L613 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/session/ttsr-coordinator.ts#L42-L43 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/export/ttsr.ts#L64-L64 [unchecked]. TTSR rules with an AST condition do block a tool: they run on the final arguments in `beforeToolCall`. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/session/ttsr-coordinator.ts#L187-L200 [unchecked]

**Model-supplied flags.** The model does not mark its own calls as safe. Tiers are declared by the tool, not by the call. Two call arguments do weaken controls. First, `timeout: 0` turns off the bash deadline (default 300 s, clamped to 1-3600 s) unless `tools.maxTimeout` caps it in `transformToolCallArguments`. Second, `async`, together with auto-backgrounding after 60 s (on by default), lets a job keep running after its single approval check. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/sdk.ts#L4814-L4824 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/tool-timeouts.ts#L10-L20 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/bash.ts#L419-L454 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/exec/settings.ts#L130-L141 [checked in review]

**Plan mode.** In plan mode, write and edit are restricted in code but bash is restricted only by the prompt. `enforcePlanModeWrite` rejects renames, deletes and any write outside the session's `local://` scratch area, and resolves symlinks and hashline headers so the guard and the write agree on the target. The plan-mode prompt asks the model not to run commands that change state. Entering plan mode keeps the previous tools (all but `write`), and the bash tool never checks plan-mode state. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/plan-mode-guard.ts#L89-L116 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/prompts/system/plan-mode-active.md#L1-L10 [checked in review] [absence confirmed for the bash check]. There is a similar gap in the `replace` edit mode: its prompt says the tool errors on an edit before a read, but the engine never consults the read store. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/crates/pi-edit/src/modes/replace.rs#L177-L195 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/crates/pi-edit/prompts/replace.md#L16-L18 [absence confirmed]

**Sub-agents.** Children run in-process. Every child gets a settings overlay that sets `tools.approvalMode: "yolo"` and turns off the advisor. The overlay is `derived`, so the user's `tools.approval` deny and prompt rules still apply. A child has no UI, so any prompt it raises fails closed, restricted folders included. In effect, the parent's approval of the `task` call is the only boundary: a parent in `always-ask` spawns children that run exec calls without asking. Per-spawn `overrides` are applied after the yolo key and can still change the mode. Tools come from the agent's `tools` frontmatter. An agent with no `tools` list gets the full default set, which is how the bundled `task` agent gets every tool. `exec` in that list expands to bash plus eval. Child depth is parent depth + 1, and a child at `maxRecursionDepth` (default 2) cannot spawn. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/task/executor.ts#L1259-L1278 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/task/executor.ts#L4543-L4586 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/task/types.ts#L319-L325 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/task/settings.ts#L473-L491 [checked in review]. Isolation (`task.isolation.enabled`, off by default) is filesystem-only within one process and is not a security boundary. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/task/settings.ts#L27-L79 [checked in review]

**Bypass paths.** Six ways around the approval policy:
- **`eval`.** The tool runs Python or JS at the `exec` tier. Calls from kernel code back into host tools go over a 127.0.0.1 bridge with a random bearer and are re-authorised. But `os.system` and `subprocess` inside the kernel never pass `bash.patterns` or the critical list. In `yolo`, a bash `deny` can be sidestepped by running the same command through Python [inferred]. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/eval.ts#L354-L354 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/eval/py/tool-bridge.ts#L153-L165 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/eval/preludes.ts#L94-L132 [checked in review]
- **MCP tools.** They are declared `write` tier whatever their annotations say, so `write` mode auto-approves every MCP call. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/mcp/tool-bridge.ts#L733-L735 [checked in review]. With `tools.xdev` (on by default), MCP and extension tools are moved out of the tool array and driven through `read`/`write` on `xd://` devices. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/xdev.ts#L1-L28 [unchecked; whether the approval key survives that indirection is not settled].
- **Project MCP servers.** They start without a prompt. omp reads `.claude/.mcp.json`, `.claude/mcp.json`, root `mcp.json` and `.mcp.json`, `.omp/mcp.json`, and `.cursor/`, `.vscode/` and `.gemini/` files, and connects at session start. The stdio transport spawns the command with `{...Bun.env, ...config.env}`. The Claude provider expands `${VAR}` in every string of those files. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/mcp/settings.ts#L9-L12 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/mcp/config.ts#L131-L152 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/discovery/claude.ts#L92-L96 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/sdk.ts#L2859-L2867 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/mcp/transports/stdio.ts#L593-L596 [corrected in review: the file locations are now attributed to the right providers, and the stdio spawn is confirmed rather than inferred]
- **Repository code runs on open, before any tool approval.** TypeScript and JS hook files under project `.claude/hooks/pre|post/` (and the Codex and plugin hook directories) are imported as extension modules at session start. Custom tools under `.claude/tools/` are imported the same way. `isProjectTrusted()` returns `true` at both call sites, so there is no trust gate. Only `--trusted-extension` or `--no-extensions` narrows what loads. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/discovery/claude.ts#L377-L396 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/extensibility/extensions/loader.ts#L774-L786 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/discovery/claude.ts#L454-L467 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/extensibility/extensions/types.ts#L926-L937 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/main.ts#L1782-L1800 [checked in review]
- **Project settings.** A repository's own settings can loosen the mode, as described under precedence above.
- **Not a bypass: speculative execution.** omp may start a call while the model is still streaming, but only when `resolveApproval` would return `allow`, no extension lifecycle handler is loaded, and the only effect is a single `local_read`. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/speculation/host.ts#L216-L235 [checked in review]

**Hooks in the permission path.** omp re-checks policy after a hook changes a call.
1. In the agent loop, `beforeToolCall` can block a call, add context or replace its arguments. Replaced arguments are validated against the schema again. If `beforeToolCall` throws, that call gets an error result and the rest of the batch continues.
2. The wrapper checks `deny` once on the original input.
3. `tool_call` handlers run in order. Each has a timeout (`extensionHandlers.toolCallTimeoutMs`, 30 000 ms). A timeout, a throw or an abort becomes a block (fail closed), and the first block wins.
4. The full approval gate is resolved again on the effective input, so a hook cannot rewrite a call into one that skips a prompt or a deny.

https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts#L3204-L3234 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/extensibility/extensions/wrapper.ts#L265-L291 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/extensibility/extensions/wrapper.ts#L306-L342 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/extensibility/extensions/wrapper.ts#L355-L374 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/extensibility/extensions/runner.ts#L2332-L2379 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/extensibility/settings.ts#L158-L161 [checked in review]. Handlers are in-process code with full rights, so failing closed protects against buggy hooks, not hostile ones.

**Remote answering.**
- **ACP:** permission requests go to the editor through `requestPermission`. After the client picks "Always allow", critical and explicit-prompt bash calls are still asked per call [checked in review].
- **RPC:** extension dialogs go to the client as `extension_ui_request`. Client-executed tools use `host_tool_call` and `host_tool_result` frames. The source describes these as a request answered by id and cancelled by id. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-mode.ts#L1226-L1250 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/host-tools.ts#L1-L60 [unchecked]. The files read do not say whether the answer carries a digest of the exact action, or whether approval prompts themselves travel as `extension_ui_request`.
- **Collab (a shared live session):** guests get write access only with a 16-byte write token, compared in constant time. A read-only peer that sends prompts or aborts gets an error. A guest's prompt becomes a custom message on the host session, so its tool calls run under the host's approval mode, but that approval wiring was not traced. https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/collab/host.ts#L856-L861 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/collab/host.ts#L1127-L1135 [checked in review, token and write gate only]

### Pi

**Scope.** The whole product is in `earendil-works/pi` at `91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af`. That covers the loop (`packages/agent`), the `pi` CLI with its TUI, RPC and print modes, tools, project trust and extensions (`packages/coding-agent`), and the experimental `packages/durable`, `packages/env` (pi-env, an SSH tool daemon) and `apps/p2` (web UI). One Node process runs the loop, every tool and every extension. There is no sandbox and no tool broker.

**There is no approval system.** Pi has no modes, rules, allowlists, approval prompts, classifier or model-based approver. A grep for `approver|autoApprove|dangerously|yolo|landlock|seatbelt|bubblewrap|sandbox-exec` over `coding-agent/src`, `agent/src`, `durable/src` and `env/src` returns no files. A grep for `maxTurns|maxSteps` also returns nothing. The reviewer re-ran both greps [absence confirmed]. The project's docs say Pi "does not ask for approval before every tool call" ([docs/security.md](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/docs/security.md#L3-L7)), and its [`SECURITY.md`](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/SECURITY.md#L1-L22) puts monitoring and containment on the user. With no extension loaded, every tool call runs unprompted on every surface (TUI, print, JSON, RPC, SDK). The `bash` tool is a plain `spawn(shell, ["-c", command], …)` with the user's permissions and no default timeout ([bash.ts#L124-L131](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/tools/bash.ts#L124-L131)) [absence confirmed]. The following topics are therefore not in the files read, because the code has nothing for them:
- rule grammar
- shell parsing before matching (core never parses commands)
- approval binding and "always" semantics
- model approvers
- flags the model sets on its own calls

**The hook that stands in for it.** An extension (a TypeScript module loaded in-process with full process authority) can register a `tool_call` handler. The coding agent binds the loop's `beforeToolCall` to these handlers ([agent-session.ts#L714-L715](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts#L714-L715)). Each tool call goes through these steps:
1. The tool is looked up by name.
2. `prepareArguments` repairs the arguments.
3. The arguments are validated against the schema.
4. `beforeToolCall` runs.
5. The tool executes.
6. `afterToolCall` runs ([agent-loop.ts#L718-L787](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts#L718-L787)) [checked in review].

Handlers run in load order and the first `block: true` wins. Non-blocking results overwrite one another ([runner.ts#L1256-L1274](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/extensions/runner.ts#L1256-L1274)) [checked in review]. A throwing handler is turned into an error result, so the gate fails closed ([agent-loop.ts#L735-L786](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts#L735-L786)) [checked in review]. `tool_result` and other observer events fail open: errors are logged and the run continues [checked in review].

Handlers receive the validated arguments object and may mutate it in place. Nothing re-validates the result ([docs/extensions.md#L981-L986](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md#L981-L986)) [checked in review].

In a parallel batch, preparation, including the hook, runs one call at a time. Approved calls then execute together under `Promise.all`, so confirmation prompts never interleave ([agent-loop.ts#L606-L627](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts#L606-L627)) [checked in review]. Separately from the hook, the loop never runs any tool call from a response cut off by the output-token limit ([agent-loop.ts#L273-L279](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts#L273-L279)) [checked in review].

**The shipped example gates.** Neither example is loaded by default.
- `permission-gate.ts` blocks a bash command only if one of three regexes matches (`rm -rf`, `sudo`, `chmod/chown … 777`). It asks through `ctx.ui.select` and blocks when there is no UI ([permission-gate.ts#L10-L33](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/permission-gate.ts#L10-L33)).
- `protected-paths.ts` blocks `write`/`edit` when the path string contains `.env`, `.git/` or `node_modules/` ([protected-paths.ts#L10-L30](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/protected-paths.ts#L10-L30)).

Neither parses shell or resolves paths, and `cat .env` through `bash` bypasses the path rule [checked in review].

**Plan mode** also exists only as an example extension. It is enforced in code, not by prompt, but only while the extension is loaded. It narrows the declared tool set to `read, bash, grep, find, ls, questionnaire` ([plan-mode/index.ts#L22-L25](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/plan-mode/index.ts#L22-L25)). A `tool_call` handler then blocks bash commands that `isSafeCommand` rejects ([#L167-L179](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/plan-mode/index.ts#L167-L179)) [unchecked]. How `isSafeCommand` handles chains and substitutions is not in the files read.

**The project trust gate.** This is the only built-in decision point, and it governs what project files load, not what tools do.
- *What needs trust:* `.pi/{settings.json, mcp.json, extensions, skills, prompts, themes, SYSTEM.md, APPEND_SYSTEM.md}` in the cwd, or `.agents/skills` in the cwd or any ancestor ([trust-manager.ts#L30-L39](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/trust-manager.ts#L30-L39)).
- *Resolution order* ([project-trust.ts#L47-L97](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/project-trust.ts#L47-L97)) [checked in review]:
  1. The `--approve` / `--no-approve` flags.
  2. User-level extensions answering `project_trust`.
  3. The nearest saved decision in `~/.pi/agent/trust.json`.
  4. `defaultProjectTrust` (default `ask`). With no UI, `ask` means untrusted.
- *Repository files cannot loosen it.* `defaultProjectTrust` is read from global settings only ([settings-manager.ts#L1020-L1023](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/settings-manager.ts#L1020-L1023)).
- *Weaknesses:*
  - Trust is a boolean keyed by path, with no hash of the contents. A pull that adds `.pi/extensions/` to a trusted folder loads with no new prompt ([trust-manager.ts#L217-L254](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/trust-manager.ts#L217-L254)) [checked in review; the pull consequence is inferred].
  - Instruction files (`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`) load regardless of trust ([resource-loader.ts#L679-L685](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/resource-loader.ts#L679-L685)) [checked in review].

**Sub-agents** exist only as an example extension. It spawns a fresh `pi --mode json -p --no-session` process with the parent's environment and no depth limit ([subagent/index.ts#L389-L396](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/subagent/index.ts#L389-L396)) [checked in review]. There is no permission state to inherit. Repo-defined agents get a confirm only when a UI exists ([#L671-L704](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/examples/extensions/subagent/index.ts#L671-L704)).

**Bypass paths.** These are paths around whatever gate an extension installs:
- **User `!` commands** emit `user_bash`, not `tool_call` ([runner.ts#L1276-L1301](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/extensions/runner.ts#L1276-L1301)) [checked in review].
- **Package installs** run lifecycle scripts: there is no `--ignore-scripts` in user-package installs ([package-manager.ts#L2026-L2048](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/package-manager.ts#L2026-L2048)). [corrected in review: `--ignore-scripts` appears only in the managed `npm ci` and the self-update commands, neither of which installs user packages; the finding stands]
- **`!cmd` config values** execute shell commands from settings ([resolve-config-value.ts#L185-L212](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/core/resolve-config-value.ts#L185-L212)) [checked in review].

Codemode and MCP are not bypasses. Nested calls re-enter the same pipeline through `runToolCall` ([agent-loop.ts#L800-L829](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/agent/src/agent-loop.ts#L800-L829)) [checked in review].

**Remote answering.** RPC mode is JSONL over stdio with no authentication. Its command union has no approval command ([rpc-types.ts#L22-L74](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/91d9719b4123a83816249d7a7917c75126bb55af/packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-types.ts#L22-L74)) [unchecked]. An unanswered extension dialog resolves to its default (cancel/false) on timeout or abort. Whether a dialog answer is bound to a specific tool call is not in the files read. The input files disagree on whether RPC clients answer dialogs or the example gate treats RPC mode as "no UI". I settled it in the clone (section 1): RPC mode installs a real UI context, so `hasUI` is true and the gate's question reaches the RPC client as an `extension_ui_request` with a dialog `id`. The "no UI, block" branch applies to print and JSON modes only.

### DeepSeek harness

**Scope.** The whole harness is in the repository `deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness`, pinned at `fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159`. It is TypeScript on Node. Every part is a Cordis plugin, and a "profile" is a stack of YAML bundle patches. The `web` profile is the `base` bundle plus the `web-app` bundle in one process, so the agent loop, the tools and the HTTP server share one process [checked in review] ([base patch L514-L533](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/bundle/base/cordis.patch.yml#L514-L533), [web-app patch L183-L189](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/bundle/web-app/cordis.patch.yml#L183-L189)). The other profiles are headless, ACP, SDK and `sdk-minimal`. Nothing was built or run; everything below comes from reading the code.

The most important fact comes first. **In the shipped composition, nothing asks the user before an ordinary tool call runs.** Under the default `workspace-write` preset, every `bash` command, every file write inside the workspace, every MCP tool and every `web_fetch` runs without a prompt, with the network open. Approval is reached in only three ways: a sandbox escalation the model requests, `plugin_manager`, and two experimental plugins [checked in review; absence confirmed].

**Modes and real defaults.** A preset pairs a sandbox mode with an approval policy. The presets are `read-only` (read-only + ask), `workspace-write` (workspace-write + ask) and `danger-full-access` (full access + never). The experimental `auto` preset is full access + ask, with an LLM reviewer in front. The base patch sets the sandbox default to `DSH_PERMISSION_MODE ?? 'workspace-write'`. The approval default is `never` only when that mode is `danger-full-access`, and `ask` otherwise [checked in review] ([base patch L237-L276](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/bundle/base/cordis.patch.yml#L237-L276)). The sandbox plugin's own schema default is `read-only`, but every shipped bundle overrides it ([sandbox-policy L134-L142](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/sandbox/sandbox-policy/src/index.ts#L134-L142)). The defaults per surface:
- web and desktop: `workspace-write` + ask. The Electron desktop app spawns the same host.
- headless: `workspace-write` ([headless patch L27-L33](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/bundle/headless/cordis.patch.yml#L27-L33)). It accepts `--permission-mode` / `--approval-policy` [unchecked] (https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/bundle/headless/src/startup.ts#L56-L72).
- `sdk-minimal`: pinned to `danger-full-access` ([sdk-minimal L70-L74](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/bundle/sdk-minimal/cordis.patch.yml#L70-L74)).

When a session is created, `pinInitialPermission` appends `permission/preset`, `sandbox/mode` and `approval/policy` events, so a resumed session never re-reads a changed config default [checked in review] ([permission-presets L429-L457](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/interaction/permission-presets/src/index.ts#L429-L457)). The `/permission` command switches presets ([permission-presets L254-L275](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/interaction/permission-presets/src/index.ts#L254-L275)). Config cannot use the names `custom` and `auto`. Composing presets over an unconfined shell executor throws at load ([permission-presets L209-L222](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/interaction/permission-presets/src/index.ts#L209-L222)). The reviewer did not check the acp-app and sdk-app bundle patches line by line, the desktop apps, or what a user-layer config could mount.

**Rule grammar and precedence.** There is no rule grammar. The code has no allowlist, no denylist, no pattern rules and no "allow always" grant [checked in review; absence confirmed] ([bash-sandbox L187-L189](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/shell/bash-sandbox/src/index.ts#L187-L189), [types.ts L28-L32](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/interaction/user-approval/src/types.ts#L28-L32)). "Precedence" exists only for the mode. The sandbox mode resolves in this order: a mode approved for this call, then the session's last `sandbox/mode` event, then the deployment default [checked in review] ([sandbox-policy L188-L205](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/sandbox/sandbox-policy/src/index.ts#L188-L205)). The approval policy is the session's last `approval/policy` event, else config ([user-approval L263-L281](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/interaction/user-approval/src/index.ts#L263-L281)). These are log-only events, and the effective value is a projection, meaning a registered fold over the log. A switch appends its event first and then queues a user-role notice to the model. A crash between the two leaves the event without the notice [unchecked] (https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/interaction/user-approval/src/index.ts#L205-L220, https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/sandbox/sandbox-policy/src/session-mode.ts#L25-L55).

Repository files cannot change the permission preset in the files read. They can still reach the agent in two ways:
- `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and their `.local` variants, from the project root down to the cwd, are delivered as a user-role message with no trust gate [checked in review] (https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/context/agent-instructions/src/files.ts#L336-L363).
- A `.env` in the cwd can supply a provider key ([credentials-local L127-L146](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/credentials/credentials-local/src/index.ts#L127-L146)).

The sandbox has no protected subpaths. Under workspace-write, a confined command can write `.git/hooks`, `.dsh/skills` and `AGENTS.md` [checked in review; the git-hook consequence is inferred] ([profiles.ts L11-L60](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/sandbox/sandbox-local/src/profiles.ts#L11-L60)).

**Shell parsing.** None. Bash wraps the whole string as `['bash', '-c', command]` and hands it to the sandbox. Nothing parses chains, substitutions, wrappers or redirects, because nothing matches commands [checked in review] ([bash-sandbox L187-L189](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/shell/bash-sandbox/src/index.ts#L187-L189)). The design bets entirely on the kernel sandbox. That sandbox confines writes only. The bwrap, Landlock and Seatbelt profiles have no read, network, IPC or Unix-socket rules. So an unprompted `bash` call can read `~/.ssh` or `~/.dsh/.credentials.yaml` and send it over the network [checked in review] ([profiles.ts L11-L60](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/sandbox/sandbox-local/src/profiles.ts#L11-L60)). Parse failure applies to tool arguments, not shell. Malformed JSON reaches policy listeners as a raw string, and schema validation runs inside `execute`, after policy and approval [checked in review] ([batch.ts L98-L106](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/core/tools/src/batch.ts#L98-L106), [schema.ts L598-L602](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/core/tools/src/schema.ts#L598-L602)).

**The permission path, step by step** [checked in review] ([pipeline.ts L197-L232](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/core/tools/src/pipeline.ts#L197-L232), [tools/index.ts L379-L401](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/core/tools/src/index.ts#L379-L401)):
1. `prepare` snapshots the arguments and deep-freezes them ([pipeline.ts L152-L176](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/core/tools/src/pipeline.ts#L152-L176)).
2. The `tools/pre-execute` waterfall runs. Its default is `allow` ([pipeline.ts L198-L202](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/core/tools/src/pipeline.ts#L198-L202)).
3. An `ask` goes to the approval service. With no service or no agent, `ask` becomes deny ([pipeline.ts L432-L473](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/core/tools/src/pipeline.ts#L432-L473)).
4. On `allow`, deny-only guards run.
5. The body runs. A throwing listener anywhere in `prepare` becomes an error result, so policy code fails closed.

Only the experimental Auto review and Claude Code hook bridge listeners return `ask` [checked in review; absence confirmed, though liveshare's verdict source was not read]. Bash, fs and `plugin_manager` ask through `approveEscalation` inside their own bodies.

**The approval service** [checked in review] ([user-approval L224-L261](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/interaction/user-approval/src/index.ts#L224-L261), [user-approval L293-L333](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/interaction/user-approval/src/index.ts#L293-L333)):
- `request()` refuses outside an open turn.
- It appends `approval/asked {id, toolName, callId?, reason?}`, decides, then appends `approval/decided {id, outcome}`. If either append throws, the request rejects, so no decision escapes without its audit pair.
- The outcomes are `allowed-once | rejected | cancelled | unavailable`, and only `allowed-once` grants.
- A missing answerer, a throwing answerer or an unknown reply becomes `unavailable`. An abort settles `cancelled` and discards a late answer.
- An `ask` has no timeout.
- For `never`, `decide()` returns `rejected` before dispatching the answerer waterfall, so a `prepend: true` listener cannot answer first ([user-approval L293-L301](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/interaction/user-approval/src/index.ts#L293-L301)).

**Approval binding and "always".** An approval is bound to one call id and lasts for that one call. The request does not carry the tool arguments. It holds agent, tool name, call id and reason, and relies on `callId` to link to the streamed call [checked in review] ([user-approval L124-L149](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/interaction/user-approval/src/index.ts#L124-L149)). The web card's only detail provider looks up the call by id and shows `args.command` if it is a string. So a bash approval shows the command, an fs escalation (`write`, `edit`) shows no path and no content, and other tools show name and reason only ([ApprovalCommand.tsx L16-L40](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/client/ui-chat/src/client/chat/ApprovalCommand.tsx#L16-L40)). The audit event never records arguments. `plugin_manager` works around this by putting `JSON.stringify(args)` into its justification ([plugin-manager tools.ts L104-L110](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/boot/plugin-manager/src/tools.ts#L104-L110)). Nothing persists a grant. The card's "Allow always" button appears only when a caller passes `onAlways`, and only the experimental computer-use approval does that ([ApprovalCard.tsx L79-L89](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/client/ui-approval/src/client/ApprovalCard.tsx#L79-L89)).

**Model-based approver (Auto review).** Auto review is experimental and opt-in: no shipped bundle or preset mounts it, and it is added by its own patch [checked in review] ([auto-review patch L1-L3](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/experimental/auto-review/cordis.patch.yml#L1-L3)). Under the `auto` preset, a prepended `tools/pre-execute` listener reviews every native call and every PTC inner call. PTC is the programmatic tool-calling runtime. The listener does not review the outer `run_code` call ([auto-review L741-L781](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/experimental/auto-review/src/index.ts#L741-L781)).

What the reviewer sees:
- A fixed `REVIEW_POLICY` prompt. Risk `low` must be allowed. `medium` is allowed only with explicit user or direct-parent authorization. `high`, meaning exfiltration of secrets, is always denied ([auto-review L53-L77](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/experimental/auto-review/src/index.ts#L53-L77)).
- User messages, tool schemas and tool-call arguments. Tool results are skipped, which closes the prompt-injection channel through tool output ([auto-review L477-L548](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/experimental/auto-review/src/index.ts#L477-L548)).

The request uses `temperature: 0` and the same provider and model as the session ([auto-review L568-L574](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/experimental/auto-review/src/index.ts#L568-L574), [auto-review L670-L693](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/experimental/auto-review/src/index.ts#L670-L693)). The output must be exactly one of six JSON shapes. Anything else, a stream error or a non-`stop` finish counts as a review failure, and a failure is a **deny** (`AUTO_REVIEW_FAILED`), not a user prompt ([auto-review L613-L667](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/experimental/auto-review/src/index.ts#L613-L667), [L721-L729](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/experimental/auto-review/src/index.ts#L721-L729)). A reviewer denial under `ask` becomes a user prompt; under `never` it is final ([auto-review L712-L719](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/experimental/auto-review/src/index.ts#L712-L719)).

Weak points:
- There is no reviewer-specific timeout. Only the tool signal bounds it.
- The review prompt is not logged.
- Removing the plugin switches every live `auto` session to `danger-full-access`, so disposal widens access. This happens only while the root fiber is active, so a normal shutdown keeps the selection ([auto-review L787-L801](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/experimental/auto-review/src/index.ts#L787-L801)).

**Model-supplied flags.** `bash` and the fs mutation tools advertise `sandbox_permissions` (`workspace-write | danger-full-access`) and a required `justification` [checked in review] ([escalation.ts L23-L42](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/sandbox/sandbox/src/escalation.ts#L23-L42)). The requested mode must be strictly wider than the call's effective mode; repeating the current mode needs no approval. The approval reason reads `escalate sandbox to <mode>: <justification>`. On `allowed-once`, the wider policy is stamped onto that one call and nothing is stored ([escalation.ts L172-L206](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/sandbox/sandbox/src/escalation.ts#L172-L206)). Bash asks before spawning ([tool-bash L478-L497](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/shell/tool-bash/src/index.ts#L478-L497)). fs asks per mutation ([tool-fs sandbox.ts L88-L113](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/fs/tool-fs/src/sandbox.ts#L88-L113)). Nothing requires a prior denial, so the model may escalate on its first attempt.

`plugin_manager` has a second model-supplied flag, `approvedBuilds`. The model is told to set it "only after the user explicitly approves", nothing checks that, and the flag becomes a persistent permission for the profile. Under full access the model alone decides. Every `plugin_manager` action, including list, asks for escalation to `danger-full-access`, and installed plugins run in the host process outside the sandbox [checked in review] ([plugin-manager tools.ts L79-L111](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/boot/plugin-manager/src/tools.ts#L79-L111)).

**Plan mode is enforced by the prompt only** [corrected in review]. Plan mode is a logged `plan/mode` state. It adds a guidance section and discloses `exit_plan_mode`. The module contract says sandbox and approval "do not read or write plan state" ([plan-mode L1-L22](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/plan/plan-mode/src/index.ts#L1-L22)). Mutation tools stay listed. The base text says this keeps the request shape stable ([base patch L336-L350](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/bundle/base/cordis.patch.yml#L336-L350)). The default web profile mounts plan mode per agent preset, and its text says this keeps "the tool catalog unchanged" ([web-app patch L547-L549](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/bundle/web-app/cordis.patch.yml#L547-L549), [standard preset L54-L68](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/bundle/web-app/presets/standard.patch.yml#L54-L68)). The reviewer's correction is that wording, and it changed no conclusion. A search of plan-mode for `pre-execute`, `guard`, `sandbox` and `deny` found nothing [absence confirmed]. In plan mode under `workspace-write`, a file-writing `bash` call runs without a prompt.

**Sub-agents.** For in-process children (spawn and fork), the delegation code captures the parent's explicit session `sandbox/mode` override and any `auto` or `danger-full-access` preset identity. It does this synchronously, before the first await. It then pins the child's approval policy to `never` whenever an approval service exists, whatever the parent's policy. All of this is written into the child's own log as `source: 'delegation'` events [checked in review] ([child-agent.ts L238-L256](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/subagent/subagent/src/child-agent.ts#L238-L256), [child-agent.ts L258-L280](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/subagent/subagent/src/child-agent.ts#L258-L280)). A child therefore cannot ask, and every escalation in it is rejected. The child is told to report the limitation to its parent instead of retrying (https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/subagent/subagent/src/child-agent.ts#L171-L175).

Children with safety disabled:
- A parent at `danger-full-access` produces a full-access child under `never`.
- An `auto` child keeps reviewer review, with denials final.
- External children run with a permission fixed in provider config, independent of the parent [checked in review]. Codex defaults to `approvalPolicy: never` with Codex's own sandbox default. It can be set to `dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` (https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/subagent/subagent-codex/src/run.ts#L58-L69). Claude Code defaults to `dontAsk`, and `canUseTool` denies except under `bypassPermissions` (https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/subagent/subagent-claude-code/src/run.ts#L316-L365). An ACP child gets a fixed `reject` (default) or `allow` answer (https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/subagent/subagent-acp/src/index.ts#L24-L64).

So a read-only parent can delegate to a fully unsandboxed Codex child. All external providers are add-on patches, not in base.

**Bypass paths.** Beyond "nothing asks by default":
- MCP tools register as ordinary tools with no approval hook. MCP stdio servers run unconfined with a scrubbed environment [checked in review; approval absence inferred] (https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/mcp/mcp-client/src/tools.ts#L126-L195).
- In-process tools (`web_fetch`, MCP clients, plugin code) run with the server's full authority. Only child commands are confined [checked in review].
- Any holder of the owner cookie can add an MCP stdio server, meaning an arbitrary command, through the settings RPC [unchecked].
- The cookie's HMAC secret sits in `~/.dsh/.credentials.yaml`, which any confined `bash` can read, because reads are unrestricted. Reading it lets a command forge the cookie that answers approvals [checked in review; readability inferred] ([browser-auth L182-L199](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/client/connection/src/browser-auth.ts#L182-L199)).
- Workflow scripts run in a `vm` context inside the PTC process. The host runs them under the parent's sandbox policy and refuses if it cannot enforce it (https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/workflow/workflow-ptc/src/host.ts#L283-L300). Which policy the PTC worker gets was not traced ([worker.ts L286](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/ptc-runtime/ptc-runtime-node/src/worker.ts#L286-L286)).

**Hooks.** The experimental Claude Code hook bridge maps a hook's `ask` to `kind: 'ask'` ([hooks-claude-code L242-L249](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/experimental/hooks-claude-code/src/index.ts#L242-L249)) [checked in review]. The experimental Codex bridge never asks. How either bridge behaves on a hook crash or timeout (`runPoint`) is not in the files read. The general pipeline rule is that a throwing pre-execute listener fails the call closed.

**Remote answering.** In the web profile, an `approval/request` becomes a `waterfall` frame on the owner's event stream. The frame carries the agent id and the JSON request, without tool arguments. The client answers with HTTP `$events/result {clientId, eventId, outcome}` [checked in review] (https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/api/gateway/src/index.ts#L638-L700, https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/api/gateway/src/stream-protocol.ts#L45-L64, https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/api/gateway/src/stream-protocol.ts#L87-L95).

The answer names an event id, not the action. Pending requests live in an in-memory gateway `Map`. They are re-delivered to any owner client that connects later, settled by the first answer, and never timed out. With no client connected, a web session stalls at the approval (read, not run). A restart drops the pending request, and resume closes the turn with an "outcome unknown" tool result ([checked in review], https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/core/session/src/repair.ts#L53-L98). Only owner peers declared `interactions: 'answer'` receive approvals, and every browser is "owner" [unchecked] (https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/client/connection/src/peer.ts#L14-L33). Headless composes no answerer, so an escalation there resolves `unavailable` [inferred]. ACP and the SDK server register `approval/request` listeners. No chat-channel or phone answering path appears in the files read.

### goose

**Scope.** `aaif-goose/goose` at `14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892`. goose is one Rust binary. The agent loop, the permission "inspectors" (pluggable checks that look at each tool call before it runs) and the built-in tools (`shell`, `write`, `edit`, Code Mode, sub-agents) all run inside the `goose` process. That process can be `goose session` (CLI), `goose serve`/`goose acp` (a server speaking ACP, the Agent Client Protocol), or the server the Electron Desktop starts and talks to over a WebSocket. Other tools come from MCP servers. There is no separate worker: the process that owns the code also makes the permission decision. The loop is an ordered list of steps over the stored conversation.
