Approval and the PreToolUse hook come before execution, and inference comes last ([state_machine_builder.rs L127-L166](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine_builder.rs#L127-L166)) [checked in review].

**Modes and defaults.** `GooseMode` has four values: `Auto`, `Approve`, `SmartApprove` and `Chat`. `Auto` is the `#[default]`, and an unset `GOOSE_MODE` resolves to `Auto` ([goose_mode.rs L22-L32](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose-provider-types/src/goose_mode.rs#L22-L32), [base.rs L1316-L1323](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/config/base.rs#L1316-L1323)) [checked in review]. The files describe one default for every surface, not one per surface. There is one exception: scheduled (cron) jobs always run in `GooseMode::Auto`, whatever mode the user configured ([full.rs L881-L907](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/scheduler/full.rs#L881-L907)) [checked in review]. The Permission inspector applies the mode in this order ([permission_inspector.rs L155-L196](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/permission/permission_inspector.rs#L155-L196)) [checked in review]:
1. `Auto` allows every call.
2. `Approve` and `SmartApprove` look up the user's per-tool level first.
3. In `SmartApprove` only, a tool whose MCP annotation says `readOnlyHint: true` is allowed.
4. `platform__manage_extensions` always asks.
5. In `SmartApprove`, any tool without a cached decision goes to the LLM judge.
6. Everything else asks.

`Chat` short-circuits before inspection. If the Permission inspector gives no result for a call (for example, because the call failed to parse), the call is asked "for safety". Five inspectors run in a fixed order: Security, Egress, Adversary, Permission, Repetition ([services.rs L564-L592](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/services.rs#L564-L592)) [checked in review].

**Rule grammar and precedence.** There is no rule grammar. The only rules are per-tool levels in `~/.config/goose/permission.yaml`, stored as `{category: {always_allow, ask_before, never_allow}}` under two categories, `user` and `smart_approve`. Lookup is by exact tool name. `never_allow` beats `always_allow`, which beats `ask_before`. Nothing is scoped to an argument, a project or a session ([permission.rs L19-L44](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/config/permission.rs#L19-L44), [permission.rs L134-L157](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/config/permission.rs#L134-L157)) [unchecked]. A corrupt file makes goose panic on purpose. A symlinked file is followed and its target is written. No admin or managed policy layer appears in the files read.

When several inspectors give results for one call, the Permission inspector's result is the baseline. Each other inspector can only tighten it: `Deny` moves the call to denied, and `RequireApproval` moves an allowed call to ask. `Allow` never overrides. An inspector that returns an error is logged and skipped ("Continue with other inspectors even if one fails") ([tool_inspection.rs L84-L118](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/tool_inspection.rs#L84-L118), [tool_inspection.rs L213-L257](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/tool_inspection.rs#L213-L257)) [checked in review]. Every denial, including one made by the adversary reviewer, reaches the model as the same `DECLINED_RESPONSE`: "The user has declined to run this tool" ([tool_execution.rs L151-L153](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/tool_execution.rs#L151-L153)) [checked in review].

Repository files cannot edit `permission.yaml`. They can, however, add executable behaviour without asking:
- Plugins under `<project>/.agents/plugins/*` are written into `config.yaml` as `enabled: true`. They sort before user plugins and win on a name clash. Their `hooks/hooks.json` is loaded and run ([discovery.rs L123-L152](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/plugins/discovery.rs#L123-L152), [discovery.rs L154-L183](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/plugins/discovery.rs#L154-L183), [hooks/mod.rs L422-L450](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/hooks/mod.rs#L422-L450)) [checked in review]. Rust has no trust prompt for them; the Desktop UI was not searched for one.
- Project agent files in `.goose/agents`, `.claude/agents` and `.agents/agents` become delegation targets ([summon.rs L248-L319](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/platform_extensions/summon.rs#L248-L319)) [unchecked].

**Shell parsing.** None. Per-tool levels match the tool name only. The pattern scanner, the egress inspector and the adversary all read the raw `command` string, through regexes or an LLM. Nothing splits chains, substitutions or wrappers ([scanner.rs L403-L421](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/scanner.rs#L403-L421), [egress_inspector.rs L293-L305](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/egress_inspector.rs#L293-L305)) [unchecked]. Without a parser there is no parse-failure behaviour. As a result, "Always allow" on `shell` allows every later shell command in every project.

**Approval binding and "always".** A one-time approval is a flag (`goose.executable: true`) in the persisted tool request's metadata. It is bound to the tool-call id, not to a hash of the arguments. On every pass, the call's state is re-derived from the messages: `Unchecked`, `AwaitingUser`, `UserAnswered` or `Executable` ([tool_call_state.rs L58-L77](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose-agent/src/tool_call_state.rs#L58-L77), [tool_approval.rs L161-L193](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose-agent/src/tool_approval.rs#L161-L193)) [unchecked]. Two guards protect a once-approval:
- A call runs only if its name was advertised to the model on the assistant message that produced it.
- Approved calls need a live "extension lease", a per-turn snapshot of extensions and tools. After a restart the lease is gone, and the call is answered "Tool approval expired ... Request the tool again" instead of running ([ops_toolcalling.rs L705-L742](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine/ops_toolcalling.rs#L705-L742), [ops_toolcalling.rs L999-L1039](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine/ops_toolcalling.rs#L999-L1039)) [checked in review].

On resume, a decision is persisted as a message before it takes effect. A conflicting second decision, or a decision for an already-answered request, is rejected ([tool_confirmation.rs L148-L186](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine/tool_confirmation.rs#L148-L186)) [checked in review].

"Always allow" and "Always deny" write the bare tool name into the `user` category of `permission.yaml`. The entry is global to the user, permanent and has no expiry. Writes use an `fs2` lock, then read, modify and write ([tool_approval.rs L108-L126](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine/tool_approval.rs#L108-L126), [permission.rs L196-L222](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/config/permission.rs#L196-L222)) [unchecked]. A better design exists in the code but is dead. `ToolPermissionStore` would key decisions by `tool_name:blake3(arguments)` with an optional expiry, in `permissions/tool_permissions.json`. Only a re-export references it, even though the docs describe the file as live ([permission_store.rs L79-L127](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/permission/permission_store.rs#L79-L127), [permission/mod.rs L10](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/permission/mod.rs#L10)) [checked in review].

**Model-based approvers.** goose has three, and they fail in different directions.

1. *Smart Approve judge (fails closed).* Uncached tools in `SmartApprove` go to `detect_read_only_requests`. It uses the session's own provider and model, with the system prompt `permission_judge.md`. The input is one user message, `UNTRUSTED TOOL REQUEST DATA (JSON)`, listing request id, tool name and arguments. The model is forced to call `platform__tool_by_tool_permission` with the list of read-only request ids. The judge sees no conversation. Its definition says "If you cannot make the decision, then it is not read-only". Returned ids are allowed for that call only. A tool judged not read-only is cached name-wide as `ask_before` in the `smart_approve` category and never judged again; only negative verdicts are cached. Every failure (no model config, provider error, no tool call) returns an empty list, which means ask. The judge has no timeout of its own and inherits the provider's ([permission_judge.rs L41-L89](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/permission/permission_judge.rs#L41-L89), [permission_judge.md L1](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/prompts/permission_judge.md#L1), [permission_judge.rs L145-L185](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/permission/permission_judge.rs#L145-L185), [permission_inspector.rs L229-L270](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/permission/permission_inspector.rs#L229-L270), [permission_inspector.rs L22-L34](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/permission/permission_inspector.rs#L22-L34)) [unchecked]. The judge is skipped entirely for any tool whose MCP server labels it `readOnlyHint: true`. Such a tool is auto-allowed in `SmartApprove`, so a hostile server can mark a destructive tool read-only ([permission_inspector.rs L52-L64](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/permission/permission_inspector.rs#L52-L64), [permission_inspector.rs L172-L176](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/permission/permission_inspector.rs#L172-L176)) [checked in review].

2. *Adversary reviewer (fails open).* It is enabled when `~/.config/goose/adversary.md` exists. An optional `tools:` header selects which tools it reviews; the default is `shell`. Each reviewed call goes to the session's model, without tools. The model sees:
   - the first user message, cut to 500 characters;
   - up to the last 4 user text messages, cut to 200 characters each;
   - the tool name and arguments;
   - the rules, which default to "Err on the side of ALLOW".

   It sees no tool results and no assistant text. Only a reply line starting with `BLOCK` denies, and that denial skips the user prompt. A missing provider, a provider error or an unparseable reply all allow. The reviewer has no timeout, and its config is read once per process ([adversary_inspector.rs L14-L27](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/adversary_inspector.rs#L14-L27), [adversary_inspector.rs L201-L247](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/adversary_inspector.rs#L201-L247), [adversary_inspector.rs L320-L346](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/adversary_inspector.rs#L320-L346), [adversary_inspector.rs L446-L464](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/adversary_inspector.rs#L446-L464)) [checked in review].

3. *Security inspector ("prompt injection detection", off by default).* It is enabled by `SECURITY_PROMPT_ENABLED`, which defaults to false. It scans only calls named exactly `shell`, matching 36 regexes, and asks (never denies) when confidence reaches `SECURITY_PROMPT_THRESHOLD`, default 0.8. High patterns score 0.75, so on patterns alone only Critical patterns prompt; SSH-key exfiltration does not ([mod.rs L46-L55](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/mod.rs#L46-L55), [scanner.rs L132-L144](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/scanner.rs#L132-L144), [patterns.rs L36-L45](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/patterns.rs#L36-L45), [patterns.rs L107-L113](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/patterns.rs#L107-L113)) [checked in review]. An optional Hugging Face-style classifier over HTTP has a 5 s timeout. It receives the command and the last 10 *user* messages, never tool output, so it cannot see indirect injection. On error it falls back to patterns, which is fail open ([scanner.rs L221-L265](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/scanner.rs#L221-L265), [classification_client.rs L141-L220](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/classification_client.rs#L141-L220)) [checked in review]. Scanned calls are logged with their full JSON, arguments included ([mod.rs L158-L203](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/security/mod.rs#L158-L203)) [unchecked].

A fourth inspector, Egress, is always on but only logs destinations and returns `Allow`. The fifth, Repetition, is inert: it is registered with `None` and updates a temporary clone of its state, so `--max-tool-repetitions` does nothing ([services.rs L588-L589](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/services.rs#L588-L589), [tool_monitor.rs L99-L134](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/tool_monitor.rs#L99-L134)) [checked in review].

**Model-supplied flags.** The files mention no `requires_approval`, "safe" or justification field. The model does set `timeout_secs` on `shell`. The value has no upper bound, and `0` disables the deadline entirely ([shell.rs L182-L189](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/platform_extensions/developer/shell.rs#L182-L189), [shell.rs L424-L426](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/platform_extensions/developer/shell.rs#L424-L426)) [checked in review]. MCP servers, not the model, supply `readOnlyHint`, which lowers approval as described above. No provider format writes the `goose.executable` flag, so the model cannot pre-approve its own call. Not every provider was audited [unchecked].

**Plan / read-only mode.** goose has no plan mode; `Chat` is the read-only mode. It is enforced in code: when the model calls a tool, the execution step does not run it and returns `CHAT_MODE_TOOL_SKIPPED_RESPONSE`, which asks the model to describe the call as a plan. The tool list is still sent, along with a prompt extra saying there is no tool access ([ops_toolcalling.rs L1060-L1081](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine/ops_toolcalling.rs#L1060-L1081), [tool_execution.rs L155-L159](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/tool_execution.rs#L155-L159), [prompt_manager.rs L153-L159](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/prompt_manager.rs#L153-L159)) [checked in review]. When goose hosts another vendor's agent through an ACP provider, Chat is only as strong as the mapping table:
- Claude gets `plan`.
- Codex gets `read-only`.
- Amp gets `default`, which is not restricted.
- Pi has no mapping at all.

Each of these is a separate permission mode in the vendor's own agent, which goose cannot inspect. Separately, the Memory extension's "Always confirm with the user before saving" is only a prompt instruction ([memory/mod.rs L118-L172](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose-mcp/src/memory/mod.rs#L118-L172)) [checked in review].

**Sub-agents.** A `delegate` child session is created with the parent session's `goose_mode`; it falls back to `Auto` only when there is no parent. The child's tool-confirmation and elicitation requests get forwarded ids registered with the parent, so the parent's user answers them ([summon.rs L410-L436](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/platform_extensions/summon.rs#L410-L436), [subagent_handler.rs L227-L245](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/subagent_handler.rs#L227-L245), [action_required_manager.rs L70-L84](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/action_required_manager.rs#L70-L84)) [checked in review]. Rules and "always" entries are global, so children share them. The docs say sub-agents run only in autonomous mode. The code does not enforce this: `delegate` is offered in every mode, and a reviewer's grep of `summon.rs` found no `GooseMode` check ([subagents.mdx L27-L27](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/documentation/docs/guides/context-engineering/subagents.mdx#L27-L27)) [absence confirmed]. Nobody ran it in `approve` mode, though. Other limits:
- Depth is one ([summon.rs L866-L876](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/platform_extensions/summon.rs#L866-L876)).
- Five children can run at once ([state_machine_builder.rs L157-L163](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine_builder.rs#L157-L163)).
- A child cannot get an extension the parent lacks.

[checked in review]. A child cannot be put in a less safe mode than its parent. A parent in `Auto` makes every child `Auto`. An external MCP server cannot forge the "delegated" marker: `subagent_session_id` is stripped from non-platform results, and the runner checks that the parent owns the session ([lease.rs L715-L740](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/extension_manager/lease.rs#L715-L740), [ops_subagent.rs L124-L134](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine/ops_subagent.rs#L124-L134)) [checked in review].

**Bypass paths.**
- *Code Mode.* This extension is off by default. It exposes `execute_javascript`, which runs in a Boa JS sandbox with no filesystem, network or shell of its own. Inside the sandbox, `tools.call(name, args)` checks only that the name is in the lease catalog, then calls `lease.call` directly. `ExtensionLease::call` performs no permission check. The approval step, all inspectors (Permission, Security, Adversary) and the PreToolUse hooks run only in the outer `dispatch_tool_call`. They therefore see one call, `execute_javascript`, and none of the shell, write or MCP calls the script makes ([code_execution.rs L172-L233](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/platform_extensions/code_execution.rs#L172-L233), [lease.rs L653-L663](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/extension_manager/lease.rs#L653-L663), [ops_toolcalling.rs L413-L474](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine/ops_toolcalling.rs#L413-L474)) [corrected in review: first marked inferred; confirmed that "per-tool approvals do not apply to calls made from inside the script", and pre hooks are skipped too]. `execute_javascript` is annotated destructive, so `Approve`/`SmartApprove` ask once per script; `Auto` asks nothing.
- *ACP providers.* When goose hosts Claude, Codex, Amp, Copilot or Pi, the tools run inside the other agent and goose's inspectors never see them. In `auto`, goose answers the adapter's own permission prompts with `AllowOnce`; Claude is put in `bypassPermissions` and Codex in `agent-full-access`. In `chat`, goose answers `RejectOnce` ([claude_acp.rs L74-L83](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/providers/claude_acp.rs#L74-L83), [codex_acp.rs L74-L77](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/providers/codex_acp.rs#L74-L77), [provider.rs L2388-L2394](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/acp/provider.rs#L2388-L2394)) [checked in review]. The deprecated `claude-code` CLI provider passes `--dangerously-skip-permissions` in Auto and no flag at all in Chat ([claude_code.rs L333-L400](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/providers/claude_code.rs#L333-L400)) [checked in review].
- *Policy files are writable by the agent.* `write`/`edit` accept any absolute path. The model can therefore rewrite `~/.config/goose/permission.yaml` or `adversary.md`, subject only to the mode's approval ([edit.rs L170-L181](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/platform_extensions/developer/edit.rs#L170-L181)) [unchecked].
- *Extensions.* The install allowlist exists only in the Desktop deeplink flow, as a `startsWith` prefix match. The CLI, `goose serve`, config edits and the agent's own `manage_extensions` are not covered ([ExtensionInstallModal.tsx L177-L204](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/ui/desktop/src/components/ExtensionInstallModal.tsx#L177-L204)) [corrected in review: the reviewer confirmed the claim that Rust has no extension allowlist and corrected only a side sentence about other allowlists]. Over ACP, any holder of the server secret can call `config/extensions/add` and `tools/call` without any per-method authorization ([custom_requests.rs L34-L848](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose-sdk-types/src/custom_requests.rs#L34-L848)) [checked in review]. The files do not say whether `tools/call` passes the approval pipeline.
- *Project plugins and scheduler.* Repository-shipped hooks run without a trust prompt (see rules above). Scheduled jobs are forced to `Auto`.
- *Environment variables.* `GOOSE_MODE` sets the mode. `SECURITY_PROMPT_ENABLED_OVERRIDE` wins over config. `OSV_ENDPOINT` controls the malware check's verdict.

**Hooks in the permission path.** Hooks are `sh -c` commands from `<plugin>/hooks/hooks.json`, matched by regex. Each gets the call's JSON on stdin and has a 30 s default timeout. The verdict rules:
- Exit 2, or `{"decision":"block"}` on stdout, is a policy deny.
- Exit 0 with empty stdout, or `{"decision":"allow"}`, is an allow.
- Anything else is a hook failure: another exit code, a signal, invalid UTF-8, a timeout, a spawn error, or failure to deliver stdin.

A failure allows the call unless the action is a `PreToolUse` with `on_failure: "block"`, which also covers timeouts. The option is ignored on other events, and the first deny ends the chain ([hooks/mod.rs L897-L947](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/hooks/mod.rs#L897-L947), [hooks/mod.rs L853-L871](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/hooks/mod.rs#L853-L871), [hooks/mod.rs L1031-L1046](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/hooks/mod.rs#L1031-L1046)) [checked in review]. The PreToolUse operation runs only on calls already marked `Executable`, that is, after approval. A hook can therefore veto an approved call but never stands in for the approval ([pre_tool_call_hook.rs L55-L92](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose-agent/src/pre_tool_call_hook.rs#L55-L92)) [checked in review]. Three more failure modes:
- A timeout kills only the `sh` child, not its process group.
- An invalid matcher regex silently drops the rule.
- Code Mode inner calls skip hooks.

**Remote approval.** As an ACP server, goose sends `session/request_permission` with four options: `allow_always`, `allow_once`, `reject_once` and `reject_always`. The option ids equal these kind names, and each request carries a tool-call update describing the tool. An unknown option id, an error or a cancel becomes `Cancel`, and a failed submission cancels the run ([server.rs L1520-L1585](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/acp/server.rs#L1520-L1585), [common.rs L55-L65](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/acp/common.rs#L55-L65)) [unchecked]. The answer is tied to the stored tool-request id. Its decision is persisted before it takes effect, and conflicting or repeated answers are rejected, so replaying an answer cannot approve a different call. The answer does not carry or check the arguments, though; it is bound to the id. "Allow always" from a remote client still writes the name-wide, user-global entry.

Forwarded sub-agent prompts are re-keyed with new ids. The UI answer path for them was not traced. Who may answer is decided by the transport alone: one shared secret, in header `X-Secret-Key` or query `token`, compared in constant time. `authenticate` is a no-op, and any roaming peer on the allowlist gets the same full surface ([auth.rs L9-L36](https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/acp/transport/auth.rs#L9-L36)) [checked in review]. The Telegram gateway code was not read, so chat-channel approval is not in the files read.

### Cline

**Scope.** The `cline/cline` monorepo at commit `500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663`: a TypeScript SDK (`sdk/packages/{shared,llms,agents,core}`), the CLI, the VS Code extension (now running on the SDK through `apps/vscode/src/sdk/*`), a per-user "hub" daemon (a local WebSocket server that owns sessions and lets several clients attach) and a desktop sidecar example. JetBrains, Kanban, the Desktop UI and the cloud agent are not in the files read. Nothing was executed; every claim is from reading code.

**Modes and real defaults.** There are three modes (act, plan, yolo) and, separately, an auto-approve switch. The real defaults differ per surface:
- CLI: auto-approve is on. `toolPolicies = {"*": {autoApprove: <flag > saved setting > true>}}`, and the help text says "(default: true)" ([main.ts L885-L895](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/cli/src/main.ts#L885-L895), [startup-settings.ts L21-L31](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/cli/src/utils/startup-settings.ts#L21-L31)) [checked in review]. With `--auto-approve false` and no TTY, every call is denied, so a headless run with approvals on does nothing ([approval.ts L69-L77](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/cli/src/utils/approval.ts#L69-L77)) [checked in review].
- ACP mode (Agent Client Protocol, editor integration): off unless `--auto-approve true` or `--yolo` ([main.ts L802-L811](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/cli/src/main.ts#L802-L811)) [checked in review].
- Desktop sidecar: on unless the UI sends an explicit false (`config.autoApproveTools !== false`) ([chat-session.ts L853-L861](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/examples/desktop-app/sidecar/chat-session.ts#L853-L861)) [checked in review].
- VS Code: per category. Defaults `readFiles:true, editFiles:true, executeSafeCommands:false, useBrowser:true, useMcp:true`. One `executeSafeCommands` toggle covers every shell command; the old safe/all and external/project fields are legacy and never read ([AutoApprovalSettings.ts L1-L43](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/shared/AutoApprovalSettings.ts#L1-L43), [sdk-tool-policies.ts L48-L68, L80-L82](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/sdk/sdk-tool-policies.ts#L48-L82)) [checked in review]. Because reads and edits accept any absolute path, these toggles cover the whole file system ([file-read.ts L231-L233](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/executors/file-read.ts#L231-L233), [editor.ts L42-L65](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/executors/editor.ts#L42-L65)) [checked in review]. Web fetch has no private-address filtering and is default-approved, so read-then-exfiltrate needs no prompt ([web-fetch.ts L100-L160](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/executors/web-fetch.ts#L100-L160)) [checked in review].
- Scheduled (cron) runs: `{"*": {autoApprove:true}}`, `ask` disabled, and any mode that is not exactly `plan` or `act`, including a missing one, becomes `yolo` ([cron-runner.ts L84-L108](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/cron/runner/cron-runner.ts#L84-L108), [L727-L750](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/cron/runner/cron-runner.ts#L727-L750)) [checked in review]. Agenda tasks the agent creates for itself start as `pending_approval`; tasks created from a hub client command start approved ([sqlite-task-store.ts L264-L266](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/tasks/store/sqlite-task-store.ts#L264-L266), [task-command-service.ts L70-L78](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/hub/server/task-command-service.ts#L70-L78)) [checked in review].
- `--yolo` is not "everything on". It selects a smaller preset (read, shell, editor, `submit_and_exit`; no search, web fetch, skills, ask or `spawn_agent`), sets auto-approve, forces the local backend and drops the CLI's in-process hooks ([presets.ts L98-L110](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/presets.ts#L98-L110), [hooks.ts L176-L181](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/cli/src/utils/hooks.ts#L176-L181)) [checked in review]. The VS Code YOLO toggle was removed and migrated into the four flags ([vscode-to-file-migration.ts L282-L313](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/hosts/vscode/vscode-to-file-migration.ts#L282-L313)) [checked in review].

**Rule grammar and precedence.** There is no rule grammar. The whole model is `{enabled?, autoApprove?}` per tool name, with `"*"` as a wildcard, merged by `resolveToolPolicy` as `"*"` then the tool's own entry then a `beforeTool` hook's override ([agent-runtime.ts L216-L234](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/agents/src/agent-runtime.ts#L216-L234)) [checked in review]. A missing policy means enabled and auto-approved. Nothing is keyed on a command string, a path or a prefix. Remote (enterprise) config is the only "admin" layer, and it fails open: the CLI fetches it only for users signed in to a Cline account, and a failed fetch, bad JSON or schema failure starts the session unrestricted. `yoloModeAllowed` exists in the schema but no code reads it ([schema.ts L199-L201](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/remote-config/schema.ts#L199-L201), [enterprise.ts L37-L73](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/cli/src/utils/enterprise.ts#L37-L73)) [checked in review]. The enterprise MCP allow-list, when non-empty, compares the server's local name, which the user chooses in their own settings file ([McpHub.ts L396-L439](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/services/mcp/McpHub.ts#L396-L439)) [corrected in review: the check applies only when the allow-list is non-empty]. Repository files cannot edit the approval settings directly, but they can add hooks, plugins and named sub-agents with no trust prompt (see bypass paths), and those change what runs. Over the hub, the client sets policy: `session.create` takes `toolPolicies` from the payload, and `toolPolicies["*"].autoApprove` becomes the session's `autoApproveTools` ([session-handlers.ts L497-L560](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/hub/server/handlers/session-handlers.ts#L497-L560), [L233-L243](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/hub/server/handlers/session-handlers.ts#L233-L243)) [checked in review, inferred, not run].

**Shell parsing before matching.** No shell parsing feeds approvals, because approvals never look at the command. The one parser is the plan-mode write filter `findFileEditingCommand`. It masks quotes, heredocs, escapes and comments, splits on `\n ; | & ( )` and backticks, skips env assignments and wrappers (`sudo env nohup time nice stdbuf timeout xargs command builtin doas`), and checks the first word's basename against a blacklist of writing commands, writing subcommands, in-place flags, and `>`/`>>` redirects outside `/dev/null`, `/tmp` and `$TMPDIR` ([command-guard.ts L23-L74](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/command-guard.ts#L23-L74), [L188-L201](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/command-guard.ts#L188-L201), [L479-L509](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/command-guard.ts#L479-L509)) [checked in review]. Its header comment says it is "not a shell interpreter" and will not catch `python -c "open(..., 'w')"` or a quoted `bash -c` string ([command-guard.ts L10-L17](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/command-guard.ts#L10-L17)) [checked in review]. By reading the lists, `node -e`, `eval`, `tar x`, `unzip`, `make` and `cargo build` also pass [inferred]. Structured `{command, args}` entries are checked as argv without shell parsing. On a parse failure the guard fails open: unparseable input goes on to the tool's own validation ([command-guard-extension.ts L49-L55](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/command-guard-extension.ts#L49-L55)) [checked in review].

**Approval binding and "always".** An approval answers exactly one `toolCallId`. There is no "always allow" store, no session-scoped grant, and nothing bound to argv, cwd, env or an executable hash ([agent-runtime.ts L2502-L2524](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/agents/src/agent-runtime.ts#L2502-L2524)) [checked in review]. The only longer-lived "yes" is a category toggle in VS Code, which the extension's callback re-reads on every call, so a toggle change applies mid-task ([sdk-interaction-coordinator.ts L118-L175](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/sdk/sdk-interaction-coordinator.ts#L118-L175)) [checked in review]. A single approval can still cover several actions. `run_commands` takes an array and runs every entry concurrently with `Promise.all` under one decision ([definitions.ts L180-L196](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/definitions.ts#L180-L196)) [checked in review]. Approving `spawn_agent` covers everything the child does (below). The flow is:
1. Parse arguments. Invalid JSON produces a skip ([L2445-L2447](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/agents/src/agent-runtime.ts#L2445-L2447)).
2. Run `beforeTool` hooks in order. A hook can rewrite input, override policy, skip or stop ([L2469-L2500](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/agents/src/agent-runtime.ts#L2469-L2500)).
3. Merge the policy. `enabled:false` skips the call.
4. If `autoApprove === false`, call the host's `requestToolApproval`. With no callback, or a callback that throws, the call is denied ([L2539-L2571](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/agents/src/agent-runtime.ts#L2539-L2571)).
5. After every call in the turn is prepared, execute, then run `afterTool` hooks ([L2370-L2408](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/agents/src/agent-runtime.ts#L2370-L2408)).

All of this is [checked in review]. Full schema validation happens inside each tool after approval, so a malformed call is shown to the user before it is rejected ([agent-runtime.ts L2432-L2467](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/agents/src/agent-runtime.ts#L2432-L2467)) [unchecked]. VS Code holds one pending-approval slot with no timeout [checked in review].

**Model-based approvers.** None. No LLM or classifier judges a call before it runs. The search covered approver, classifier, guardian, "auto mode" and risk-level logic [absence confirmed].

**Model-supplied flags.** None at this commit. `run_commands` takes `{commands: string[]}` or structured entries. The `requires_approval` field that Round 1 described survives only in a legacy string in `responses.ts`, its test, and the docs page ([schemas.ts L133-L157](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/schemas.ts#L133-L157), [responses.ts L112-L122](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/core/prompts/responses.ts#L112-L122), [auto-approve.mdx L51](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/docs/features/auto-approve.mdx#L51-L51)) [checked in review, absence confirmed]. Cline removed model-judged command safety and put nothing in its place. The only model-callable control tool is `switch_to_act_mode` (below).

**Plan mode: code or prompt.** Both, and neither part is a boundary. In code, the `plan` preset removes `editor` and `apply_patch` but keeps `run_commands`, web fetch, skills, `spawn_agent` and teams, and MCP tools load without any mode check ([presets.ts L45-L57](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/presets.ts#L45-L57), [runtime-builder.ts L615-L633](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/runtime/orchestration/runtime-builder.ts#L615-L633)) [checked in review]. The blacklist above runs as a `beforeTool` extension registered only when `mode === "plan"`. A match returns `skip`, before policy and approval ([runtime-builder.ts L578-L590](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/runtime/orchestration/runtime-builder.ts#L578-L590)) [checked in review]. In the prompt, the plan contract is appended to the system prompt, and every user message is tagged `<user_input mode="...">` ([cline.ts L15-L53](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/prompt/cline.ts#L15-L53), [format.ts L5-L10](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/prompt/format.ts#L5-L10)) [unchecked]. In the interactive CLI the model can leave plan mode itself. `switch_to_act_mode` sets a pending mode, ends the run, and the CLI rebuilds the session in act mode and auto-continues with "The user approved switching to act mode". Nothing checks that claim, and with default auto-approve the tool needs no click ([mode.ts L31-L68](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/cli/src/runtime/interactive/mode.ts#L31-L68), [L80-L108](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/cli/src/runtime/interactive/mode.ts#L80-L108)) [checked in review]. VS Code does not expose the tool ([cline-session-factory.ts L1057-L1061](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/sdk/cline-session-factory.ts#L1057-L1061)).

**Sub-agent inheritance.** Children run with safety off by construction. `spawn_agent` and configured agents both build the child through `createDelegatedAgent` and pass neither `toolPolicies` nor `requestToolApproval`. Every child call therefore takes the "no policy means allow" branch and never reaches the user ([spawn-tool.ts L121-L187](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/runtime/host/local/spawn-tool.ts#L121-L187), [delegated-agent.ts L120-L147](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/team/delegated-agent.ts#L120-L147)) [checked in review]. A code comment states the intent: "The parent approves delegation; child tools run autonomously ... Do not inherit approval policy" (https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/team/configured-agent-tool.ts#L162-L165). The child does inherit the parent's hooks and extensions, so the plan-mode guard still applies. It also inherits the provider credentials and the mode's built-in tools, without MCP. It can get `spawn_agent` again, so nesting is unbounded. No depth, fan-out or wall-clock limit exists; the declared `timeoutMs: 300000` has no reader in the loop ([spawn-agent-tool.ts L201-L202](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/team/spawn-agent-tool.ts#L201-L202)) [checked in review; the missing timeout consumer is inferred from text search]. With `--auto-approve false` the user is asked about `spawn_agent` once and never again for what the child does. VS Code turns sub-agents off ([cline-session-factory.ts L1160-L1161](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/sdk/cline-session-factory.ts#L1160-L1161)). Named sub-agents from `<workspace>/.cline/agents/*.md` become `subagent_<name>` tools, each with its own optional provider and model, with no trust prompt [unchecked].

**Bypass paths.**
- Sub-agents: no policy, no approver (above).
- Hub clients can contribute tool executors (`tool_executor.bash`, `custom_tool.*`, `hook.*`), so a client can say "run bash on my side" ([hub.ts L192-L224](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/hub.ts#L192-L224)) [unchecked].
- MCP tools in plan mode [checked in review]. In VS Code every MCP tool sits under one default-on `useMcp` flag [checked in review].
- Workspace hooks (`<workspace>/.clinerules/hooks`, `.cline/hooks`) and plugins (`.cline/plugins`, imported into a worker thread) load with no trust gate. The newer Agent-Plugins lane is home-only "so opening a repository cannot activate repository-controlled MCP servers" ([paths.ts L495-L508](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/storage/paths.ts#L495-L508), [paths.ts L629-L640](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/shared/src/storage/paths.ts#L629-L640)) [absence confirmed].
- The desktop file-drop approver: with `CLINE_TOOL_APPROVAL_MODE=desktop` the CLI writes a request file into `CLINE_TOOL_APPROVAL_DIR` and polls every 200 ms for a decision file. The directory has no mode set and the decision file has no integrity check, so anyone who can write there can approve. After 5 minutes the call is denied ([tool-approval.ts L29-L101](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/runtime/tools/tool-approval.ts#L29-L101)) [checked in review].
- Not sandboxes: `CLINE_SANDBOX` only moves the data directory ([helpers.ts L583-L605](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/cli/src/utils/helpers.ts#L583-L605)) [checked in review], and `CLINE_PLUGIN_MODE=sandbox` is crash isolation with the full environment [unchecked].
- Not enforced: `.clineignore` runs nowhere except an optional example hook ([ClineIgnoreController.ts L155-L228](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/core/ignore/ClineIgnoreController.ts#L155-L228)) [checked in review].
- The files read describe no command-permission environment variable (an allow/deny list set through env).

**Hooks in the permission path.** There are three layers, with different failure behaviour:
- In-process `beforeTool` hooks can skip, rewrite, force a prompt (`policy:{autoApprove:false}`) or stop. One that throws fails the whole run [checked in review].
- Plugin hooks have a 3 s timeout and ignore errors. After 5 consecutive failures a plugin is marked `failed` ([plugin-registry.ts L36-L40](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/plugin/plugin-registry.ts#L36-L40)) [checked in review].
- File hooks (`PreToolUse` and others) block only by printing `{"cancel": true}` on stdout; the exit code is ignored. Empty output, bad JSON, a crash, a spawn error or a timeout (120 s in the SDK, 30 s in VS Code) all let the call run ([hook-file-hooks.ts L415-L454](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/hooks/hook-file-hooks.ts#L415-L454), [hook-factory.ts L520-L546](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/core/hooks/hook-factory.ts#L520-L546)) [checked in review].

A file-hook `cancel` becomes a `ControlledStopError` that aborts the whole run, not a skip of one call. The parsed `review` flag is never used, so a file hook cannot force a prompt ([hook-file-hooks.ts L577-L606](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/hooks/hook-file-hooks.ts#L577-L606)) [checked in review]. VS Code hooks are on by default (`userSetting ?? true`) ([hooks-utils.ts L6-L8](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/apps/vscode/src/core/hooks/hooks-utils.ts#L6-L8)) [checked in review]. Whether `--yolo` stops file hooks is not settled: no code removes the file-hook extension in yolo ([local-runtime-bootstrap.ts L434-L447](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/services/local-runtime-bootstrap.ts#L434-L447)).

**Remote answering.** The hub keeps one map of pending approvals and broadcasts `approval.requested`, with the tool input, to every subscriber. The request has no timeout and is re-sent to clients that re-subscribe. `approval.respond` resolves by `approvalId` alone: it does not check that the responder is a participant of that session, holds a role, or advertised the `approval.respond` capability ([approval-handlers.ts L10-L60](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/hub/server/handlers/approval-handlers.ts#L10-L60), [L123-L161](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/hub/server/handlers/approval-handlers.ts#L123-L161)) [checked in review]. The answer names the action only through that opaque id, and nothing binds it to the responder. Client capability requests are bound: only the target client may answer a `capability.respond` ([capability-handlers.ts L174-L180](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/hub/server/handlers/capability-handlers.ts#L174-L180)). Hub authentication accepts the per-start token or, on a loopback bind, any loopback `Origin` header. Origin-only connections are refused `providers.*` and `mcp.*`, but session create, run and approval handlers ignore the tier ([hub-websocket-server.ts L676-L722](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/hub/server/hub-websocket-server.ts#L676-L722), [hub-server-transport.ts L874-L884](https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/hub/server/hub-server-transport.ts#L874-L884)) [checked in review; corrected in review: the Origin path does not require the caller to read the 0600 token file]. By reading the code, any local process or local web page could therefore create an auto-approved session in any directory and answer its approvals; nobody ran this.

### OpenHands (Agent Canvas)

**Scope.** The repository `OpenHands/OpenHands` at pinned commit `d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b` holds only Agent Canvas: the React/TypeScript browser client, Node launchers, an Electron shell, a Dockerfile layered on the upstream agent-server image, and a Helm chart [absence confirmed]. The agent loop, tool dispatch, the three security analyzers, confirmation-policy enforcement, ACP permission handling and hook execution all live in `OpenHands/software-agent-sdk` (the agent-server, pinned as `openhands-agent-server==1.61.0`), which was not read. What this repository does show is the client side of the approval system: the browser builds the whole start-conversation request, including the confirmation policy and the analyzer, and posts it to the agent-server. Every statement below about enforcement is therefore about what the client asks for, not what the server does.

**What is and is not in the code read.**
- In: the mapping from two settings (`confirmation_mode`, `security_analyzer`) to a `confirmation_policy` object and a `security_analyzer` object in the start request; the planner's hard-coded `NeverConfirm`; the confirm/reject UI and its `respond_to_confirmation` request; display of the action event's `security_risk` field; the type comment on that field; the `WAITING_FOR_CONFIRMATION` execution status; loading of `.openhands/hooks.json`; the plugin trust checkbox; browser-executed client tools; server-level bash calls.
- Not in: how the server enforces a policy, how `ConfirmRisky` compares a risk to its threshold, whether `LLMSecurityAnalyzer` makes its own LLM call, what the pattern and policy-rail analyzers match, the value a fresh server stores for `confirmation_mode`, ACP `session/request_permission` handling, hook run-time semantics, and any shell parser.

**Modes and real defaults.** There are three policy kinds: `NeverConfirm` (run everything), `AlwaysConfirm` (ask for every action), and `ConfirmRisky` (ask when the action's risk rating reaches a threshold). The client maps settings to a policy in `agent-server-adapter.ts`. If `confirmation_mode !== true` the policy is `NeverConfirm`. If it is true and the analyzer is `"llm"`, the policy is `ConfirmRisky{threshold: HIGH, confirm_unknown: true}`. If it is true and the analyzer is anything else, the policy is `AlwaysConfirm` ([agent-server-adapter.ts L777-L789](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L777-L789)) [checked in review]. The built-in client defaults are `confirmation_mode: false` and `security_analyzer: "llm"` ([src/services/settings.ts L11-L12](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/services/settings.ts#L11-L12), [L52-L56](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/services/settings.ts#L52-L56)). The policy is sent on every start ([agent-server-adapter.ts L1232-L1233](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1232-L1233)), so the agent-server's own default for "no policy given" never applies to a Canvas conversation.

The start path does not use those built-in defaults. It calls `getSettingsForConversation`, which returns the server's stored `conversation_settings` unchanged and throws if the fetch fails ([settings-service.api.ts L650-L686](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/settings-service/settings-service.api.ts#L650-L686)). The client then applies `!== true`, so a missing field also gives `NeverConfirm`. The effective default is `NeverConfirm` unless the stored object has `confirmation_mode: true`, and what a fresh server stores is not visible here [corrected in review: the original said a failed settings fetch falls back to built-in defaults at start; that fallback applies only to the display path `getSettings`]. The SDK documentation calls `ConfirmRisky` the default. Through Canvas, the effective default is no confirmation at all. The client has one surface (the browser UI, with local or Cloud backends). Other surfaces are not in the files read.

**Rule grammar and precedence.** There is none in this repository. Nothing parses shell commands or matches allow or deny rules. A search of `src/` and `scripts/` for `deny`, `allowlist`, `permission` and `shell-quote` users found only the ACP launch-command splitter ([src/utils/acp-command.ts L1-L20](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/utils/acp-command.ts#L1-L20)) [absence confirmed]. The only choices are the three policy kinds and the analyzer kind. Only the `llm` analyzer changes which actions are confirmed. Choosing `PatternSecurityAnalyzer` or `PolicyRailSecurityAnalyzer` with confirmation on yields `AlwaysConfirm`, so their ratings decide nothing and only colour the prompt ([agent-server-adapter.ts L784-L802](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L784-L802), attached at [L1267-L1271](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1267-L1271)) [checked in review]. The client has no `EnsembleSecurityAnalyzer` option. Policy layering works like this: the stored server settings feed the client, and the client writes the policy into each request. User/project/admin precedence is not in the files read. A repository's own files cannot change the confirmation policy through any path shown here. They can attach hooks, covered below.

**Shell parsing.** Not in this repository [absence confirmed]. If any command analysis exists, it is inside the SDK's pattern or policy-rail analyzers and was not read.

**Approval binding and duration.** An approval answers "whatever is pending". When the conversation status is `WAITING_FOR_CONFIRMATION`, the UI shows a fixed prompt with accept and reject buttons. It shows no tool arguments. It treats the newest agent-sourced event as the pending action ([conversation-confirmation-buttons.tsx L30-L62](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/components/shared/buttons/conversation-confirmation-buttons.tsx#L30-L62), [L99-L148](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/components/shared/buttons/conversation-confirmation-buttons.tsx#L99-L148)). The answer is `POST .../respond_to_confirmation` with body `{accept, reason?}`, and `reason` is never filled ([event-service.types.ts L1-L4](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/event-service/event-service.types.ts#L1-L4), [use-respond-to-confirmation.ts L15-L31](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/hooks/mutation/use-respond-to-confirmation.ts#L15-L31), [event-service.api.ts L25-L28](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/event-service/event-service.api.ts#L25-L28)) [checked in review]. The request carries no action id and no hash of the arguments. The action event id is used only in the browser, to ignore a second click or key press in the same tab [corrected in review: the agents-sessions study first said the request was keyed by action id; it is not]. If several actions are pending, for example parallel tool calls, one accept covers whatever the server holds. The client does not check for a race between the event it shows and the event the server holds. Cmd/Ctrl+Enter accepts and Shift+Cmd/Ctrl+Backspace rejects. There is no "always allow" and no session-scoped or persisted approval in the client. Each answer is one-shot.

**Model-based approvers and model-supplied flags.** The action event carries a `security_risk` field. The TypeScript type comment says the tool call "may contain `security_risk` field predicted by LLM when LLM risk analyzer is enabled" ([action-event.ts L44-L61](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/types/agent-server/core/events/action-event.ts#L44-L61)) [unchecked]. A captured session snapshot, a single-line fixture that cannot be line-cited, has `system_prompt_kwargs: {"llm_security_analyzer": true}`. Both point to an inline design: the acting model fills in `security_risk` as part of its own tool call, and `ConfirmRisky` asks only when that rating is HIGH or unknown. Whether `LLMSecurityAnalyzer` also makes a separate LLM call is not settled from this repository [inferred]. Its inputs, timeout and failure mode are SDK code. The UI shows a HIGH-risk banner when the newest agent action has `security_risk` HIGH ([conversation-confirmation-buttons.tsx L112-L134](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/components/shared/buttons/conversation-confirmation-buttons.tsx#L112-L134)). If the inline reading is right, the default opt-in risk mode trusts the model to report when its own action is dangerous. A prompt-injected model can simply report LOW.

**Plan / read-only mode.** Plan mode exists and is enforced by the tool set, not by a flag. "Create a Plan" starts a separate planner conversation, linked by `parent_conversation_id` and a `plannerparent` tag. It runs a raw agent spec with three tools, `glob`, `grep` and a planning file editor bound to one `plan_path`, under `confirmation_policy: NeverConfirm` ([agent-server-adapter.ts L1352-L1363](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1352-L1363), [L1400-L1402](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1400-L1402), full request [L1310-L1420](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1310-L1420)) [checked in review]. A send guard never routes a plan-mode message to the parent conversation, because that would run the code agent ([conversation-websocket-context.tsx L1566-L1579](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/contexts/conversation-websocket-context.tsx#L1566-L1579)) [checked in review]. The read-only guarantee depends on the server honouring the tool list and the editor's path binding (SDK) [inferred]. Writes to the plan file are never confirmed. The planner also receives every saved secret, along with the session-key header (review note). The chat's "confirmation on" lock icon reads the global setting, not the conversation's policy ([confirmation-mode-enabled.tsx L7-L14](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/components/features/chat/confirmation-mode-enabled.tsx#L7-L14)). It can therefore show "on" for a planner that never confirms [inferred].

**Sub-agent inheritance.** The agent calls `launch_child_conversation(target, task, title?, repository?, branch?, isolation?)`, and the browser executes it. The browser checks only the shape of the arguments and the cross-target rules ([child-conversation-launch.ts L120-L131](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/services/child-conversation-launch.ts#L120-L131), [L109-L204](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/services/child-conversation-launch.ts#L109-L204)). It then starts the child with the parent's agent profile and LLM profile ([L247-L263](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/services/child-conversation-launch.ts#L247-L263)), in the parent's directory, as a git worktree that silently falls back to the shared directory ([L265-L317](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/services/child-conversation-launch.ts#L265-L317)) [checked in review]. There is no human gate before a launch, and there is no depth or count cap ([handler L511-L551](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/services/child-conversation-launch.ts#L511-L551)) [absence confirmed]. The child's confirmation policy comes from the global setting read at launch, not from the parent's live policy ([agent-server-conversation-service.api.ts L660-L668](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/conversation-service/agent-server-conversation-service.api.ts#L660-L668)) [inferred]. Because the child gets the same profile, it gets the same tools and the same secrets scope, so delegation never narrows permissions. A child can run with confirmation off whenever the global setting is off, which is the default. For the SDK's `task_tool_set` sub-agents, inheritance is not in this repository.

**Bypass paths.** Paths that reach execution without passing a confirmation:
1. *Server-level bash.* The client calls `BashClient.executeCommand` on the agent-server outside any conversation, for login probes, git probes and automation output. Any holder of the session key can run host commands with no policy and no analyzer ([bash-service.api.ts L24-L44](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/bash-service/bash-service.api.ts#L24-L44), [use-bash-command-runner.ts L12-L42](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/hooks/use-bash-command-runner.ts#L12-L42), [acp-service.api.ts L110-L118](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/acp-service/acp-service.api.ts#L110-L118)) [checked in review].
2. *The agent holds the approval key.* The launcher puts `OPENHANDS_AUTOMATION_API_KEY = sessionApiKey` into the agent-server's environment "to terminal commands spawned by the agent-server" and also seeds it as a secret ([dev-with-automation.mjs L975-L985](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/scripts/dev-with-automation.mjs#L975-L985), [L1632-L1651](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/scripts/dev-with-automation.mjs#L1632-L1651), [docker/entrypoint.sh L343-L353](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/docker/entrypoint.sh#L343-L353)). The system prompt tells the agent to send it as `X-Session-API-Key` ([runtime-services-info.mjs L128-L141](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/scripts/runtime-services-info.mjs#L128-L141)) [checked in review; the prompt text appears only when the automation service is advertised]. The same key authenticates `respond_to_confirmation`, server-level bash and settings. A prompt-injected agent can therefore approve its own pending action or turn confirmation off [inferred from the key's reach]. Every saved secret is also attached to every conversation as a `LookupSecret` whose headers carry this key ([agent-server-adapter.ts L1114-L1144](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1114-L1144)) [checked in review].
3. *Client tools.* `launch_child_conversation`, `request_secret` and `canvas_ui_control` are acknowledged by the server at once, executed by the browser, and answered as a prefixed `role: "user"` message ([agent-server-adapter.ts L1217-L1231](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1217-L1231), [client-tool-result.ts L16-L29](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/services/client-tool-result.ts#L16-L29)) [checked in review]. Replay protection is a `localStorage` ledger, and the call proceeds anyway if the ledger write fails ([claim-client-tool-call.ts L10-L34](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/utils/claim-client-tool-call.ts#L10-L34)).
4. *Code loading.* `tool_module_qualnames` from settings is forwarded, and the server imports those Python modules ([agent-server-adapter.ts L1287-L1296](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1287-L1296)) [inferred].
5. *Canvas Apps.* Extension bundles are `import()`ed into the Canvas origin with no check beyond an `activate` export ([canvas-extension-module-loader.ts L25-L42](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/extensions/canvas-extension-module-loader.ts#L25-L42)). They can read every backend key in `localStorage` ([storage.ts L95-L102](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/backend-registry/storage.ts#L95-L102)) [checked in review].
6. *ACP agents.* ACP profiles get the same policy mapping and UI but no client tools. Nothing in `src` or `docs` handles ACP `session/request_permission`. A test fixture shows a saved ACP profile with `acp_session_mode: "bypassPermissions"`, a field the client round-trips but never reads ([merge-agent-profile-save-input.test.ts L47](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/__tests__/components/settings/agent-profiles/merge-agent-profile-save-input.test.ts#L47-L47), [acp-tool-call-event.ts L27-L60](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/types/agent-server/core/events/acp-tool-call-event.ts#L27-L60)) [corrected in review: the original absence claim missed `__tests__`]. What the server does with vendor-CLI permission requests is unknown.
7. *MCP.* Stdio MCP servers are `command`/`args`/`env` run by the agent-server on its host ([mcp-service.api.ts L22-L58](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/mcp-service/mcp-service.api.ts#L22-L58)) [unchecked]. Whether MCP tool calls are confirmed is not in the files read.

**Hooks in the permission path.** On every start, Canvas asks the server to read `<project_dir>/.openhands/hooks.json` within a 5 s load timeout and places the result in the request as `hook_config`, unless settings already carry one ([hooks-service.ts L12-L42](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/hooks-service.ts#L12-L42), [agent-server-adapter.ts L1281-L1285](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/agent-server-adapter.ts#L1281-L1285)). There is no trust prompt: a cloned repository's hook commands are attached to the next conversation. If loading fails or times out, the client logs `console.warn` and starts with no hooks, so a hook-based deny rule that cannot load does not apply. This fails open ([hooks-service.ts L30-L42](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/hooks-service.ts#L30-L42)). Hooks are not loaded for container workspaces ([conversation-workspace.ts L90-L92](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/conversation-workspace.ts#L90-L92)) [corrected in review: the 5 s is the load call, not a per-hook execution timeout; the failure is a `console.warn`, not silent; container workspaces skip hooks]. Hook run-time behaviour is in the SDK: exit codes, which hook types fail open, and per-hook timeouts. The only trust gate in the client is the plugin-launch checkbox, "I trust this plugin from {sources} with the agent secrets defined in my account". It is one-shot and not bound to a plugin version ([plugin-launch-modal.tsx L398-L420](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/components/features/launch/plugin-launch-modal.tsx#L398-L420)) [checked in review].

**Remote answering.** The answer comes from the web UI only. For local backends it goes to the agent-server with the single shared session key. The open build has no user accounts, so any key holder can answer any approval, and the request records no approver identity ([auth.ts L9-L18](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/backend-registry/auth.ts#L9-L18)) [checked in review]. For Cloud, the browser sends `respond_to_confirmation` directly to the per-conversation runtime sandbox with that conversation's `session_api_key` ([event-service.api.ts L17-L38](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/blob/d7293dfd671e23dab182fe71c247ff05f48e780b/src/api/event-service/event-service.api.ts#L17-L38)) [unchecked]. In neither case does the answer name the action.

### Zed

**Scope.** The source read covers the agent crates of the Zed editor at commit `3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd` (a shallow clone with no history). Zed has two kinds of agent. The native "Zed Agent" runs inside the editor process as a GPUI entity called `Thread`, and every permission decision for it is made in `crates/agent`. External agents (Claude Code, Codex, Gemini, Cursor and registry agents) are child processes that talk ACP (Agent Client Protocol, JSON-RPC over stdio); for them Zed is only the client that serves files and terminals and shows their permission requests. Both kinds feed the same UI thread model, `AcpThread` ([connection.rs trait with `truncate`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/acp_thread/src/connection.rs#L296-L302)) [unchecked]. The LLM provider crates, MCP internals, `agent_skills` and the remote server were not read.

**Modes and real defaults.** Zed has no named approval modes in the files read; there is one rule system with a default action. The shipped global default is `"default": "confirm"`, so an unmatched tool call asks the user ([default.json L1259-L1264](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/assets/settings/default.json#L1259-L1264)) [checked in review]. The OS sandbox for the terminal is on by default for local projects: the `sandboxing` feature flag is `enabled_for_all = true` ([flags.rs L107-L121](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/feature_flags/src/flags.rs#L107-L121)) [checked in review]. The model always sees a tool named `terminal`; when the project is local, the OS is macOS, Linux or Windows, and the user has not set `allow_unsandboxed`, that name is bound to the `sandboxed_terminal` implementation ([sandboxing.rs L1-L26](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/sandboxing.rs#L1-L26), [L205-L240](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/sandboxing.rs#L205-L240), [thread.rs L4412-L4450](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L4412-L4450)) [checked in review]. Remote projects are never sandboxed. On a platform with no sandbox integration the wrap is a no-op and commands run with the user's full permissions ([sandboxing.rs L18-L21](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/sandboxing.rs#L18-L21)) [checked in review]. Setting `allow_unsandboxed` switches the model back to the plain tool and removes the sandbox section from the system prompt. External ACP agents are a different surface: their own approval modes apply, and Zed adds nothing (see "Bypass paths").

**Rule grammar and precedence.** Rules are Rust regexes, case-insensitive by default, grouped per tool under `always_deny`, `always_confirm` and `always_allow`, with a per-tool `default` and a global `default`. One function, `ToolPermissionDecision::from_input`, decides, in this order ([tool_permissions.rs L208-L260](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L208-L260), [tool_permissions.rs L253-L306](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L253-L306)) [checked in review]:

1. Hardcoded security denies (terminal tool only).
2. If any regex in the tool's rules fails to compile, the whole tool is denied ([L447-L465](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L447-L465)).
3. Terminal tool only: the command is parsed; a command with a substitution or one that cannot be parsed is denied, unless the tool is effectively "allow everything".
4. Per-tool rules, per sub-command: any `always_deny` match denies and short-circuits; any `always_confirm` match makes the result confirm; `always_allow` allows only if every sub-command matches an allow pattern ([tool_permissions.rs L378-L431](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L378-L431)).
5. The tool's `default`, then the global `default`.

The asymmetry matters: one deny or confirm hit decides, but allow needs full coverage. File tools match the path twice, raw and lexically normalised (`..` collapsed, no filesystem access), and keep the more restrictive result ([tool_permissions.rs L514-L560](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L514-L560)) [checked in review]. A hole: if the tool has no entry and the global default is `allow`, the substitution check in step 3 is skipped, so "allow everything" means no validation at all [checked in review]. The files read do not say how user, project (`.zed/settings.json`) and any managed settings layer, so whether a repository's own settings can loosen the user's rules is not in the files read. What is in the files is a guard against the agent doing it: an edit under the local `.zed` folder always prompts, even when a rule allows it (see file tools below) [unchecked].

Hardcoded denies are five regexes that deny `rm` aimed at `/`, `~`, `$HOME`, `.` or `..`, applied to the raw string, to each sub-command, and to a multi-path `rm` expanded into one command per operand ([tool_permissions.rs L21-L61](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L21-L61), [L70-L108](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L70-L108), [L132-L203](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L132-L203)) [corrected in review: the patterns match `rm` with any or no flags, not only recursive or forced `rm`]. Settings cannot override them. They are narrow: `find / -delete`, `git clean -fdx /`, `mv` and `dd` are not covered.

**Shell parsing before matching.** `extract_commands` parses the command with `brush-parser`, a POSIX shell parser, and flattens the syntax tree into a list of normalised sub-command strings: each pipeline element, each `&&`, `||` and `;` branch, subshell and brace-group bodies, and nested command substitutions ([shell_command_parser.rs L23-L35](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/shell_command_parser/src/shell_command_parser.rs#L23-L35), [shell_command_parser.rs L559-L650](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/shell_command_parser/src/shell_command_parser.rs#L559-L650)) [checked in review]. Quotes are stripped, so `rm -rf '/'` and `rm -rf /` hit the same patterns; expansions are kept as source text ([shell_command_parser.rs L652-L710](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/shell_command_parser/src/shell_command_parser.rs#L652-L710)). A redirect other than to `/dev/null` becomes an extra pseudo-command such as `> out`, which an allow list must also match ([L712-L764](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/shell_command_parser/src/shell_command_parser.rs#L712-L764)).
