Substitutions are denied, not just confirmed: `$VAR`, `${}`, `$(...)`, backticks, arithmetic and process substitution make `validate_terminal_command` return `Unsafe`, and the tool returns a message telling the model to resolve the value first ([L273-L286](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L273-L286), [shell_command_parser.rs L315-L358](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/shell_command_parser/src/shell_command_parser.rs#L315-L358)) [corrected in review: this applies whenever the tool is not effectively allow-all, including when it has no rules and the global default is confirm or deny, not only when it has rules].

On parse failure, or when any word cannot be normalised, `extract_commands` returns `None`: the raw string is matched against the rules, deny and confirm still apply, and allow is disabled ([tool_permissions.rs L308-L355](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L308-L355)) [checked in review]. This fails closed.

What the parser cannot see:
- Wrappers are not unwrapped. A test pins `ls | xargs rm -rf` to `["ls", "xargs rm -rf"]` ([shell_command_parser.rs L1216-L1217](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/shell_command_parser/src/shell_command_parser.rs#L1216-L1217)), and nothing handles `sudo`, `env`, `bash -c '...'` or `find -exec` [checked in review]. A deny on `rm` fires only if `rm` is the command word or the regex is unanchored and matches inside the wrapper text.
- Every shell is parsed as POSIX. All 11 `ShellKind` variants, including PowerShell, cmd, nushell, fish and xonsh, return true from `supports_posix_chaining` ([shell.rs L186-L203](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/util/src/shell.rs#L186-L203)) [checked in review]. The code comment says an unsupported shell would turn `always_allow` into a deny ([tool_permissions.rs L311-L333](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L311-L333)), but that branch is dead for every shell. Whether POSIX parsing misreads PowerShell or cmd constructs was not tested.

**Approval binding and "always".** The prompt offers "once", which stores nothing, or "always", which writes into the user's `settings.json` ([thread.rs L6985-L7075](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L6985-L7075)) [checked in review]. For the terminal, "always" stores one regex per sub-command built from the command word and its first subcommand, for example `^cargo\s+test(\s|$)`; other tools get a tool-level default. Patterns are generated only for plain command words, never for `./x` or absolute paths ([pattern_extraction.rs L53-L95](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/pattern_extraction.rs#L53-L95)). The answer is chosen by option id prefixes `always_allow:`, `always_deny:` and `always_allow_mcp:`. Nothing binds to the full argument list, working directory, project or thread: a stored `^git\s+push(\s|$)` allows `git push --force origin main` in every later thread of every project. If the settings write fails, the current call is still allowed and the pattern may not persist.

The flow is: `authorize` checks settings first and prompts only if they do not decide ([thread.rs L6190-L6215](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L6190-L6215)); while a prompt is open, a loop races the user's answer against settings changes, so a sibling "always" answer can resolve a pending prompt automatically ([thread.rs L6872-L6985](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L6872-L6985)) [checked in review]. Some prompts ignore settings on purpose through `authorize_always_prompt`: symlink escapes, `.zed/` settings edits and skills ([thread.rs L6218-L6235](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L6218-L6235)) [checked in review].

For file tools, `authorize_file_edit` applies deny rules, then canonicalises the worktree roots and the target. A path that resolves through a symlink outside the project, or whose parent does, always prompts; so do paths under `.zed` and `~/.agents/skills`. The only auto-allow is `explicitly_allowed && !is_sensitive` ([tool_permissions/tool_permissions.rs L627-L735](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/tool_permissions.rs#L627-L735)) [unchecked]. Canonicalisation walks up to the deepest existing ancestor, so a symlinked parent of a new file is caught ([L72-L96](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/tool_permissions.rs#L72-L96)) [unchecked]. The resolved path is not pinned between check and write [unchecked].

Sandbox grants are a second approval layer with three lifetimes: once, thread (in-memory `ThreadSandboxGrants`, saved with the thread database), and always (`agent.sandbox_permissions` in settings). Grants from worktree roots, the thread and settings merge to the least restrictive policy ([sandboxing.rs L292-L400](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/sandboxing.rs#L292-L400)) [checked in review], ([sandbox.rs L220-L265](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/sandbox/src/sandbox.rs#L220-L265)) [unchecked]. Network host grants are shared between the terminal and `fetch`: approving `github.com` for one approves it for the other, and `fetch` re-authorises the host on every redirect hop ([L239-L330](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/fetch_tool.rs#L239-L330)) [checked in review].

**Model-based approvers.** Not in the files read. No classifier or LLM reviewer appears in the decision path described.

**Model-supplied flags.** The sandboxed terminal's input lets the model request more access per call: `allow_hosts`, `allow_all_hosts`, `fs_write_paths`, `allow_fs_write_all`, `unsandboxed`, plus a required `reason` ([terminal_tool.rs L94-L210](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/terminal_tool.rs#L94-L210)) [checked in review]. These flags are requests, not grants. The flow is:

1. The ordinary tool-permission check on the command runs first ([terminal_tool.rs L417-L472](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/terminal_tool.rs#L417-L472)) [checked in review].
2. An escalation with an empty `reason` is an error ([terminal_tool.rs L678-L705](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/terminal_tool.rs#L678-L705)) [checked in review].
3. If the request exceeds standing grants, `authorize_sandbox` shows the user a prompt with the model's reason ([thread.rs L6237-L6300](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L6237-L6300)) [checked in review].
4. A request weaker than a standing grant is rejected with an explanation, so the model is not misled about its real access ([terminal_tool.rs L474-L525](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/terminal_tool.rs#L474-L525)) [checked in review].
5. A command that ran without a sandbox gets an explicit note in its tool result ([terminal_tool.rs L947-L983](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/terminal_tool.rs#L947-L983)) [checked in review].

Git metadata cannot be granted; the model has to ask for `unsandboxed`.

When the sandbox cannot be created, Linux probes `bwrap` (`can_create_sandbox()`) and, on failure, prompts with Retry, Run unsandboxed (once, this thread, or always) and Deny ([terminal_tool.rs L722-L813](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/terminal_tool.rs#L722-L813), [thread.rs L6622-L6700](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/thread.rs#L6622-L6700)) [unchecked beyond L722-L760]. Windows WSL "environment unavailable" errors use the same prompt ([L859-L946](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/terminal_tool.rs#L859-L946)) [unchecked]. macOS fails closed: the command does not run ([L814-L846](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/terminal_tool.rs#L814-L846)) [unchecked]. A "this thread" answer becomes a thread grant, so later commands in that thread run unsandboxed without asking. On Windows, paths on a Windows drive (DrvFs) trigger a recurring warning prompt ([terminal_tool.rs L631-L677](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/terminal_tool.rs#L631-L677)) [unchecked].

**Plan and read-only modes.** Not in the files read. The only related facts: profile tool toggles use the canonical name `terminal` [checked in review], and a `plan` ACP client capability exists only behind the `acp-beta` flag ([acp.rs L124-L160](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent_servers/src/acp.rs#L124-L160)) [unchecked].

**Sub-agent inheritance.** Mostly not in the files read. Sub-agents share the parent's edit review through `linked_action_log`, but read times are deliberately not shared ([action_log.rs L534-L555](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/action_log/src/action_log.rs#L534-L555)) [unchecked]. The files read say nothing about whether children inherit rules or grants, or whether they can run with safety off. The `subagents` ACP capability is behind `acp-beta`.

**Bypass paths.**
- External ACP agents are not governed by `tool_permissions`. The settings doc comment and `default.json` claim that for external agents "deny and confirm still take precedence" ([settings_content/agent.rs L403-L413](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/settings_content/src/agent.rs#L403-L413), [default.json L1247-L1258](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/assets/settings/default.json#L1247-L1258)). No reader of those settings exists in `agent_ui`, `acp_thread` or `agent_servers` [absence confirmed]. A user's `terminal.always_deny` does not bind Claude Code or Codex over ACP.
- `terminal/create` from an external agent runs in a normal project terminal with the system shell and the directory's environment, with no sandbox and no permission check at the client ([v1.rs L10549-L10590](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent_servers/src/acp/v1.rs#L10549-L10590), [acp_thread/terminal.rs L949-L1000](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/acp_thread/src/terminal.rs#L949-L1000)) [unchecked]. `fs/read_text_file` and `fs/write_text_file` are limited to the project worktrees ([acp_thread.rs L8106-L8135](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/acp_thread/src/acp_thread.rs#L8106-L8135) and [acp_thread.rs L8186-L8260](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/acp_thread/src/acp_thread.rs#L8186-L8260)) [unchecked]. The agent process itself runs with the user's full privileges and the editor's whole environment ([transport.rs L31-L110](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent_servers/src/acp/transport.rs#L31-L110)) [unchecked].
- "Allow everything" (no tool entry, global default `allow`) skips the substitution check [checked in review].
- `allow_unsandboxed`, unsupported platforms and remote projects run the terminal unconfined; with the sandboxing flag off, `fetch` skips its host gate [checked in review].
- `fetch` is not in the OS sandbox. It runs in the editor process; its forbidden-IP check (loopback, private, link-local) is "a gate, not a full pin" because the HTTP client re-resolves at connect ([fetch_tool.rs L45-L60](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/fetch_tool.rs#L45-L60), [fetch_tool.rs L308-L318](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tools/fetch_tool.rs#L308-L318), [pinned_host.rs L154-L195](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/http_proxy/src/pinned_host.rs#L154-L195)) [checked in review].
- Wrapper commands (`bash -c`, `sudo`, `xargs`, `find -exec`) slip past anchored deny patterns [checked in review].
- MCP tools: only the `always_allow_mcp:` option prefix and tool-level "always" appear in the files read; MCP internals were not read.

**Hooks in the permission path.** Not in the files read.

**Remote approval answering.** Zed has no remote approval surface in the files read; prompts are shown in the editor UI. In the other direction, as an ACP client, Zed answers an external agent's `session/request_permission`: `handle_request_permission` converts the agent's options, calls `request_tool_call_authorization_with_id` and waits for the user, with no settings lookup ([v1.rs L10172-L10225](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent_servers/src/acp/v1.rs#L10172-L10225)) [absence confirmed for the settings lookup]. The user picks from the agent's own flat option list. A cancelled request cancels the pending prompt (`cancel_permission_request`) [unchecked]. The function name suggests the answer is tied to a tool-call id, but the files read do not show what the answer contains or whether it can be replayed. Per the ACP spec the request is advisory, and the client must apply its own checks; Zed applies worktree path checks to fs and none to terminals [unchecked].

### Warp

**Scope.** The `warpdotdev/warp` repository (pinned at `4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39`) holds the client only. The agent loop, the system prompt, the model call and the choice of tool calls run on Warp's server. Each turn the client sends one `POST {server}/ai/multi-agent` request and executes the tool calls that come back. The client's job is to be a tool executor, a UI and a policy gate. The request also carries the user's autonomy level to the server [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent/api.rs#L138-L188 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent/api/impl.rs#L152-L168 A reviewer confirmed that the client contains no direct call to any model provider [absence confirmed]. In the code read:
- The client holds the permission data type `AIExecutionProfile` with its defaults, the decision code `BlocklistAIPermissions`, the organisation denylist merge, the "run to completion" override, the `--sandboxed` handling, the ACP (Agent Client Protocol) permission responder and the flags used to launch child agents. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/cloud_object_models/src/ai_execution_profile.rs#L352-L392 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L133-L140
- Not in the files read: how the server's model produces the risk flags the client trusts, whether the server re-checks any tool call, and the code of the self-hosted worker (`oz-agent-worker`, a separate Go repository).

**Modes and real defaults.** A permission level (`ActionPermission`) is set per kind of action. It has three values, `AgentDecides`, `AlwaysAsk` and `AlwaysAllow`, plus a catch-all `Unknown` that behaves like `AgentDecides`. There is no `Never` value. A "never" exists only on other enums: computer use, ask-user and run-agents [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/cloud_object_models/src/ai_execution_profile.rs#L18-L28 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/cloud_object_models/src/ai_execution_profile.rs#L123-L132

The default profile, `AIExecutionProfile::default()`, sets [checked in review]:
- `execute_commands = AlwaysAsk`
- `apply_code_diffs = AgentDecides`
- `read_files = AgentDecides`
- `write_to_pty = AlwaysAsk`
- `mcp_permissions = AgentDecides`
- `run_agents = AlwaysAsk`
- `computer_use = Never`
- an empty allowlist and a pre-filled denylist.

https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/cloud_object_models/src/ai_execution_profile.rs#L394-L421 The headless TUI changes only one of these: `execute_commands` becomes `AgentDecides` [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/cloud_object_models/src/ai_execution_profile.rs#L426-L431 The reviewer added a caveat: profiles migrated from legacy user settings can carry other values.

`AgentDecides` means something different for each action:
- For file reads it always reads, with no path limit and no prompt. The code comment says "For now, we always read files". https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L746-L750
- For diffs it behaves like `AlwaysAsk`. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L897-L900
- For MCP it allows a call only if the server is allowlisted. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L881-L887

All three are [checked in review]. The CLI default profile has a six-entry allowlist (see below). In autonomous (headless) runs nobody can be asked. A command that cannot run on its own is therefore finished at once with `RequestCommandOutputResult::Denylisted`, and that result goes back to the model. This happens for every reason a command is held back, including "would ask", even though the code comment assumes the denylist is the cause [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/action_model/execute.rs#L599-L658 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/action_model/execute/shell_command.rs#L240-L244

**Rule grammar and precedence.** Allow and deny entries are regular expressions. The client wraps each entry in `^...$` and matches it against each sub-command [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/cloud_object_models/src/ai_execution_profile.rs#L205-L228

The default lists are [checked in review]: https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/cloud_object_models/src/ai_execution_profile.rs#L313-L341
- Default denylist, 18 entries: `bash`, `fish`, `pwsh`, `sh`, `zsh`, `curl`, `eval`, `exec`, `source`, `wget`, `dig`, `nslookup`, `host`, `ssh`, `scp`, `rsync`, `telnet`, `rm`.
- CLI default allowlist: `cat`, `echo`, `find .*`, `grep`, `ls`, `which .*`.
- The GUI default allowlist is empty.

The denylist beats the allowlist, `AgentDecides` and `AlwaysAllow` [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L948-L964

Organisation (admin) settings arrive as workspace settings [unchecked]:
- The organisation denylist is merged with the profile denylist, organisation entries first.
- When admin settings are set, they also replace the profile's allowlist and its read and write settings.
- So precedence runs organisation, then the user's profile, then the shipped defaults. The organisation denylist is the only list that survives every bypass covered below.

https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L410-L437 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L439-L447 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions_tests.rs#L1751-L1760

This organisation policy is enforced only by the client. A modified client does not apply it, and no server-side check is visible. Can a repository's own files loosen the user's settings? For MCP, no: servers configured inside a project never start by themselves and need an explicit opt-in [unchecked]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/mcp/file_based_manager.rs#L396-L416 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/mcp/file_based_manager.rs#L429-L434 For command rules, the files read say nothing either way.

**Shell parsing before matching.** The client prepares a command in four steps [checked in review]:
1. It normalises the string: a backslash or backtick followed by a newline is joined.
2. `decompose_command`, from a hand-written shell parser in `warp_completer`, splits the command into sub-commands. The output includes the contents of `$( )` and `( )`, the parts of concatenations, and each recomposed level.
3. Leading `KEY=VAL` assignments are removed before the denylist test. So the denylist matches the form without them, while the allowlist matches the raw sub-command.
4. A redirection (`>` or `<`) is reported as a flag.

https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L923-L936 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/warp_completer/src/parsers/simple/mod.rs#L95-L123 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/warp_completer/src/parsers/simple/mod.rs#L198-L240 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/warp_completer/src/parsers/simple/mod.rs#L249-L260 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L987-L996

A test pins the denial of a multi-line command written with backslash continuations. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions_tests.rs#L1883-L1890

The parser cannot see aliases. It does not expand `$VAR`. It does not unwrap wrappers: the reviewer searched and found no handling of `xargs`, `nohup` or `sudo` [absence confirmed]. Whether this can be exploited was reasoned from the parser and not tested. What happens when parsing fails is not in the files read.

**Approval binding and "always".** The files describe no "always allow this command" grant for the native agent. The lasting settings are the profile's allow and deny lists and a per-conversation override. That override, `autoexecute_override = RunToCompletion`, can be set four ways: Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+I, the inline approve keystroke for a CLI sub-agent, a flag on a new conversation, or `--auto-approve` in the TUI [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent/conversation.rs#L5028-L5039 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent/conversation.rs#L4149-L4164 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/warp_tui/src/session.rs#L215-L219

The override survives a restart only when `RememberFastForwardState` is on. That flag is in the dogfood list, so in release builds the override resets on restart. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/warp_features/src/lib.rs#L1068-L1068 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/warp_features/src/lib.rs#L1100-L1100 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent/conversation.rs#L682-L688

Writes to a running command's terminal (`can_write_to_pty`) have their own tier, `AskOnFirstWrite`. Once the user approves the first write to a terminal block, later writes to the same block are allowed. This approval is bound to the block [unchecked]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/action_model/execute/shell_command.rs#L247-L288 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/cloud_object_models/src/ai_execution_profile.rs#L55-L65

What an approved command releases also matters. The command is typed into the user's own interactive terminal session, not started as a separate process. It gets the user's whole shell environment, with no scrubbing [unchecked]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/action_model/execute/shell_command.rs#L324-L413

**Model-based approvers.** The client runs no classifier and no LLM reviewer. The only judgement is the model's own, described next. Thresholds and timeouts therefore do not apply.

**Model-supplied flags.** Each `RunShellCommand` tool call carries two booleans, `is_read_only` and `is_risky`, set by the server-side model [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/ai/src/agent/action/convert.rs#L21-L40

Under `execute_commands = AgentDecides` the client decides in this order [checked in review]:
1. Check the denylist.
2. If the feature `AgentDecidesCommandExecution` is on and `is_risky == Some(false)`, allow the command. This skips both the redirection check and the allowlist.
3. Otherwise a redirection blocks auto-execution.
4. An allowlist match allows the command.
5. Finally, `is_read_only` allows it. The executor reads it as `is_read_only.unwrap_or(false)`.

https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L972-L1008 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/action_model/execute/shell_command.rs#L211-L234

The feature is in the app crate's `default` features, so it is on in normal builds [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/Cargo.toml#L506-L506 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/Cargo.toml#L555-L555 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/features.rs#L208-L209

A prompt-injected model can label any command not risky. The denylist is then the only independent check, and the client never verifies that a command really is read-only. Combined with the TUI default (`execute_commands = AgentDecides`), the headless surface runs whatever the model calls safe and the denylist misses. How the server's model sets these flags is not in the files read.

**Plan and read-only modes.** The files read describe no plan, ask or read-only mode for the native agent.

**Sub-agent inheritance.** The parent's `run_agents` tool runs on the client and asks for approval by default (`run_agents = AlwaysAsk`). Each child gets a task row on the server with the parent's run id, and it inherits the parent's execution profile and base model [unchecked]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/child_agent_launch.rs#L34-L67 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/child_agent_launch.rs#L98-L124 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/action_model/execute/start_agent.rs#L251-L287

A child's own `run_agents` call is refused unless `MultiLevelOrchestration` is on, and that flag is dogfood-only. So the limit of one level is a feature gate, not part of the design. No cap on the number of children was found [unchecked]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/action_model/execute/run_agents.rs#L458-L481 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/action_model/execute/run_agents.rs#L567-L581 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/warp_features/src/lib.rs#L1079-L1079

Children that are third-party command-line agents run with their own safety turned off [checked in review]:
- Local children are typed into a hidden terminal pane: `claude --session-id <uuid> --dangerously-skip-permissions '<prompt>'`, `codex --dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox '<prompt>'` and `opencode --prompt '<prompt>'`. The code comment says a hidden pane cannot show an approval UI.
- Cloud and `oz agent run --harness` runs add `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust` for Codex.
- The driver writes `hasTrustDialogAccepted = true` into `.claude.json` in advance, and sets trust_level = trusted in Codex's config.
- Warp's own policy is not applied to these child processes.

https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/pane_group/pane/local_harness_launch.rs#L120-L137 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/pane_group/pane/local_harness_launch.rs#L159-L255 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/pane_group/pane/terminal_pane.rs#L1599-L1613 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/pane_group/pane/terminal_pane.rs#L1904-L1930 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent_sdk/driver/harness/claude_code.rs#L223-L240 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent_sdk/driver/harness/claude_code.rs#L812-L835 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent_sdk/driver/harness/codex.rs#L206-L227 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent_sdk/driver/harness/codex.rs#L878-L888

Messages from other agents reach a child Claude through its plugin hook, labelled as "user-level coordination messages" and capped at 6000 characters. They are exempt from secret redaction [unchecked]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent_sdk/driver/harness/claude_code/parent_bridge.rs#L1-L40 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent/redaction.rs#L90-L94 The `send_message` tool always runs without approval [unchecked]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/action_model/execute/send_message.rs#L131-L137

**Bypass paths.**

1. **Run to completion.** Commands skip the denylist when three things hold: the override is on, the process is not `is_sandboxed`, and `auto_approve_bypasses_command_denylist` is true. That setting is true by default. Only the organisation denylist is then checked, and everything else returns `Allowed(RunToCompletion)` [checked in review]. The same override also allows, without asking:
   - file reads and writes, except MCP config paths
   - all MCP calls
   - writes to a running terminal

   It also suppresses questions to the user unless that setting is "always ask" [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/settings/ai.rs#L2103-L2113 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L938-L970 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L706-L711 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L785-L790 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L869-L874 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L500-L505 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L1226-L1233 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions_tests.rs#L1110-L1118 Writes to MCP config files still need approval locally even then, because they "can inject arbitrary context". https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L1243-L1257

2. **`--sandboxed`.** This is a hidden CLI flag that the process declares about itself. When it is set, the client:
   - builds the CLI default profile with an empty denylist
   - skips the user's denylist
   - allows reads and writes, including MCP config writes.

   It does not drop every denylist. The organisation's separate "sandboxed agent" denylist (`sandboxed_agent_execute_commands_denylist_for_scope`) is still checked. If the organisation has configured none, nothing is denylisted. The client creates no operating-system sandbox, and the reviewer found no seatbelt, landlock, bwrap, seccomp or AppContainer anywhere in the code [corrected in review: the source first said the flag empties the denylist; the reviewer narrowed this to the profile denylist, with the organisation's sandboxed-agent list still enforced] [absence confirmed for the OS sandbox]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/warp_cli/src/agent.rs#L621-L623 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/lib.rs#L451-L463 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/cloud_object_models/src/ai_execution_profile.rs#L463-L471 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L779-L783 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions.rs#L233-L243 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/blocklist/permissions_tests.rs#L1663-L1715 `isolation_platform::detect` only detects a container in order to fetch a workload token; it confines nothing. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/isolation_platform/src/lib.rs#L29-L48 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/isolation_platform/src/lib.rs#L59-L80 A dogfood-only Docker sandbox, `LocalDockerSandbox` built on `sbx`, mounts an empty workspace. It confines only a shell, not the agent's file tools [unchecked]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/warp_terminal/src/local_tty/unix.rs#L65-L99 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/warp_features/src/lib.rs#L1070-L1070

3. **The ACP route.** Its `fs/read_text_file` handler reads any path the agent names, with no policy check [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent_sdk/driver/harness/acp/mod.rs#L1553-L1556

4. **Child CLI agents.** These run with their bypass flags, as described above.

5. **Keystroke injection.** Code-review comments are typed into a CLI agent's terminal as plain text with no approval. If the agent has exited, the text lands in the shell [unchecked]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/terminal/cli_agent.rs#L563-L577 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/terminal/view.rs#L24426-L24450

6. **MCP stdio servers.** They inherit the full environment, because no `env_clear` is called [checked in review]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/mcp/src/runtime.rs#L235-L256

**Hooks in the permission path.** None in the files read. Warp has no hook that can allow or deny its own agent's actions. Hooks appear only as the Claude plugin hook that delivers mailbox messages to a child.

**Remote answering, and whether the answer names the action.** The files describe no web, phone or chat way to answer the native agent's prompts. There are two related paths.

The first path covers CLI agents a user types into a Warp terminal. Warp only observes them. Plugins it installs into those agents emit OSC 777 notifications (sentinel `warp://cli-agent`), including permission requests, and these drive status UI and notifications. Warp does not answer the approvals. The events carry no authentication. Any process that can write to the terminal can fake one, and the only filter is that a session must already exist for that terminal [unchecked]. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/crates/warp_core/src/cli_agent_protocol.rs#L1-L38 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/terminal/cli_agent_sessions/listener/mod.rs#L39-L89 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/terminal/cli_agent_sessions/mod.rs#L514-L523

The second path is the ACP client. Warp answers `session/request_permission` by program in `answer_permission_request` [checked in review]:
- Only `Execute` requests (through `can_autoexecute_command`) and file writes are evaluated.
- `Deny` is returned only for an explicit denylist match or a protected path. Every other refusal reason is logged and allowed "in an unattended run".
- When allowing, `AllowAlways` is picked over `AllowOnce`, so the grant persists inside the foreign agent.
- If no native conversation is bound, the request is allowed.
- Reads, searches, fetches and other kinds are never checked.

The action is described by the agent adapter's own `raw_input`, title and locations, so the answer is only as specific as the adapter makes it. https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent_sdk/driver/harness/acp/policy.rs#L1-L9 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent_sdk/driver/harness/acp/policy.rs#L34-L70 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent_sdk/driver/harness/acp/policy.rs#L80-L117 https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/blob/4b790f5fac9a2cc637c865aacbfa4f2cf64f6c39/app/src/ai/agent_sdk/driver/harness/acp/mod.rs#L1489-L1551

### OpenCodex

**Scope.** OpenCodex (`lidge-jun/opencodex`, pinned at `ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396`) is a model-provider gateway written in TypeScript on Bun. It converts three inbound wire protocols into one internal request and forwards it to a provider. It has no agent loop of its own and no per-tool-call approval system. Two parts of it matter for approvals. The first is the experimental Remote Workspace feature: the hub (the central OpenCodex server) runs a Codex agent and the tools run on a paired remote machine called the executor. Here the policy is a capability set (the list of tool families a session may use) that is fixed when the session is created and enforced on the executor. The second is a set of permission and consent rules on the gateway itself: key scopes, consent for paid ("metered") targets, human-only telemetry consent, and an unauthenticated loopback listener. The research was a focused read. The Claude Code and Pi Remote Workspace runtimes, the macOS/Windows native helper and the web GUI were not read.

**Modes and real defaults per surface.** There are no approval modes in the sense of ask/auto/plan. For the Codex runtime of Remote Workspace, the hub sends every turn with `approvalPolicy: "never"` and configures its own MCP server `ocx_remote_workspace` as required and auto-approved (`default_tools_approval_mode="approve"`). Approval is therefore never asked per call. What limits the agent is the session's `accessMode`, which defaults to `read-only` and narrows the session to `["workspace.read"]`; any other mode gives the session the device's full capability list. Remote Workspace is off unless both `config.runtimeRole === "hub"` and `OCX_REMOTE_WORKSPACE_ENABLED=1` are set. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-activation.ts#L4-L9 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-codex-runtime.ts#L500-L513 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-sessions.ts#L406-L418 [checked in review]. How the Claude Code and Pi runtimes set approval is not in the files read.

On the gateway's data plane, the default is "no authentication on loopback": `isApiAuthRequired` is simply "hostname is not loopback", and on a loopback bind no token is read. A non-loopback bind refuses to start without `OPENCODEX_API_AUTH_TOKEN` or a configured `apiKeys[].key`. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/auth-cors.ts#L337-L339 [checked in review].

**Rule grammar and precedence.** There is no rule grammar for tool calls. The permission rules that exist are:

- Key scopes. A configured data key may carry `allowedProviders`, `allowedModels` and `allowMeteredComboTargets`. The scope is checked against the provider and model the router resolved (after aliases, combo picks, fallback and compaction rewrites), never against the model string the client sent; out of scope is a 403. The environment token and loopback admission carry no scope and are unrestricted. A scope can only narrow; it never grants management authority. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/admission-model-scope.ts#L8-L29 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/admission-model-scope.ts#L71-L76 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/types/config.ts#L335-L350 [checked in review].
- Metered-target consent. A key reaches combo targets marked `metered` only with `allowMeteredComboTargets: true`; the environment token never qualifies; on loopback (no key) consent is declared on the combo itself (`allowMeteredTargets`). Ineligible metered targets are filtered out of every pick, including recovery picks. No human is prompted; the key simply stops at the last unmetered target. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/responses/core-combo.ts#L561-L561 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/responses/core-combo.ts#L663-L678 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/types/config.ts#L346-L350 [unchecked].
- Remote Workspace capabilities. There are three levels: what the executor declares at pairing, what the executor currently offers on each presence message, and the per-session `accessMode` on the hub. Presence may not claim more than was declared at pairing (`updateDeviceCapabilities` throws). The reviewer corrected the source here: the stored "grant" is declared by the executor in its own pairing request (`capabilities: parseRemoteWorkspaceCapabilities(raw.capabilities)`), and the dashboard-created pairing grant carries no capability list, so the operator does not choose the capability set at enrollment; the hub's only narrowing is `accessMode`. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-hub.ts#L427-L463 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-hub.ts#L480-L491 [corrected in review]. Roots (the folders the executor exposes) and toolchain roots are chosen on the executor at pairing (`--root`, `--toolchain-root`); the hub sees only root ids and labels. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-device.ts#L146-L164 [checked in review].

Repository files cannot loosen anything here: the agent's working directory on the hub is an empty `mkdtemp` directory, and the hub disables the user's own MCP servers and hooks found in the effective Codex config (finding 29, below). Whether files inside an executor root can influence policy is not in the files read; the executor's policy comes from its pairing state.

**Shell parsing before matching.** None, because there is no shell. The remote `exec` tool takes an argv vector (at most 64 items), a relative `cwd` and `timeoutMs` up to 60 s; it is run as `bwrap ... -- <argv>`, so there is no shell string to split, no chain or substitution to miss, and no command matching. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-tools.ts#L52-L120 [unchecked] https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-command-runner.ts#L375-L436 [checked in review]. The model can still pass `sh -c '...'` as argv if a shell exists inside the sandbox; the files read do not say anything filters that, and the containment is the sandbox, not a parser [inferred]. There is no dangerous-command list.

The sandbox, step by step: `bwrap --die-with-parent --new-session --unshare-pid --unshare-ipc --unshare-uts --unshare-net --proc /proc --dev /dev --tmpfs /tmp`, read-only binds of `/usr /bin /sbin /lib /lib64` and a short `/etc` list, read-only toolchain roots, the Bun executable at `/ocx-runtime/bin/bun`, one writable `--bind <root> /workspace`, `--clearenv` with only `HOME`, `PATH`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`. Network stays unshared because no caller sets `networkAccess`. The `bwrap` binary must be an absolute, private regular file with one link, outside every writable root, with no group- or world-writable parent. Before each command the workspace tree is scanned (up to 250,000 entries) and the run is refused if any file has more than one hard link. Not present: `--unshare-user`, `--cap-drop`, `--unshare-cgroup`, seccomp. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-command-runner.ts#L23-L47 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-command-runner.ts#L469-L534 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-command-runner.ts#L438-L467 [checked in review; absence of those flags confirmed by the reviewer].

`exec` exists only if a sandbox runner exists. At pairing and every connect, the executor builds a runner; on Linux this needs `bwrap` to pass `trustedBubblewrap`, the roots to pass the hard-link scan, and a trial `bwrap ... /bin/true` to succeed within 2 s. Only then is `workspace.exec` added to the capabilities; `workspace.read` and `workspace.write` are always offered. Without a runner the executor refuses `exec` outright ("disabled until an OS sandbox is configured") and the hub UI says "Edit files only". https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-command-runner.ts#L706-L747 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-command-runner.ts#L682-L703 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-device.ts#L372-L384 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-executor.ts#L362-L396 [checked in review]. On macOS and Windows the docs say the native helper's probe fails by design, so those executors offer file tools only; this was not verified in code [unchecked].

**Approval binding and "always" semantics.** There is no "always allow", because nothing is asked. What a tool call is bound to is the session. The remote-tool RPC has four message kinds: `prepare` (the full request plus a timeout of 1 to 120000 ms), `grant` (a separate go-ahead, sent only if the request has not already timed out), `cancel` and `response`. "Grant" here is a protocol step from the hub's coordinator, not a human approval. The coordinator allows 8 active requests, defaults to a 65 s timeout and sends `cancel` on timeout. The executor refuses a request whose session id or root id differs from the one bound at the session handshake, whose tool needs a capability not granted to that session, or whose id or grant is a duplicate. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-rpc.ts#L17-L47 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-rpc.ts#L162-L207 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-rpc.ts#L298-L346 [unchecked]. A device whose capabilities changed after a session started is refused with "start a new session", so a capability set lasts exactly one session. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-sessions.ts#L690-L695 [checked in review].

The device itself is bound by a bearer token `ocxrw_<43 base64url>` issued at pairing; the hub stores only its SHA-256. The bearer has no expiry and is not bound to a connection; a new connection for a device closes the old one ("latest connection wins"), with no per-message generation counter. `revokeDevice` removes the device from durable state first, then closes the connection. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/index/serve-options.ts#L567-L603 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-device.ts#L386-L450 [corrected in review, for the capability wording; fencing confirmed].

File operations are bound to the root by the executor, independent of the hub: relative paths only, at most 4,096 characters, no NUL; a `..` segment is not rejected by name on POSIX, but the resolved path must stay inside the root; every component is `lstat`ed and any symlink is an error; opened files are checked by `fstat` against `lstat` for identity and type; files with more than one hard link are refused; the root's device, inode and birth time are rechecked before every operation ("pair the folder again"); writes use temp-file plus rename and require the previous content's SHA-256 (or null for create-only). https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-executor.ts#L88-L120 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-executor.ts#L122-L166 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-executor.ts#L173-L190 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-executor.ts#L219-L240 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-executor.ts#L338-L360 [corrected in review: `..` is contained by a resolve-then-inside check, not rejected lexically]. The window between `lstat` and `open` is narrowed by `O_NOFOLLOW` and the post-open check, not eliminated.

**Model-based approvers.** None in the files read.

**Model-supplied flags.** None. The tools take no "safe" or "requires approval" flag; `exec` has no sandbox-escape parameter, and `networkAccess` is never set by any caller. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-tools.ts#L123-L128 [unchecked for the tool list; `networkAccess` checked in review].

**Plan and read-only modes.** The read-only mode is enforced in code: `accessMode: read-only` reduces the session's capabilities to `["workspace.read"]`, and the executor refuses any request needing a capability not granted to the session. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-sessions.ts#L406-L418 [checked in review] https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-rpc.ts#L298-L346 [unchecked]. The separate claim "the agent uses no local tool" is prompt plus configuration: the developer instruction tells the model to use no other tool and to stop rather than fall back locally, and the user's MCP servers and hooks are disabled, but the source says this is not a hard guarantee that Codex has no local tools; it depends on the Codex permission profile. The hub refuses to start if the profile still sets legacy `sandbox_mode`. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-tools.ts#L212-L225 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-codex-runtime.ts#L330-L349 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-codex-runtime.ts#L392-L414 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-codex-runtime.ts#L538-L543 [checked in review]. A disconnected executor makes the remote tools fail with "offline"; nothing falls back to the hub filesystem.

**Sub-agent inheritance.** Not in the files read. Per device, at most 4 concurrent sessions; each session has its own handshake-bound capability set. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/protocol.ts#L3-L4 [corrected in review, finding 31].

**Bypass paths.**
1. The unauthenticated loopback listener. It serves the inference routes only (`/v1/responses`, compact, messages, count_tokens, chat completions, audio, search, images, models, realtime/live), and start-up prints a warning that any local process can use it and spend account quota and paid credentials. Loopback admission carries no key scope, so on a shared host it bypasses `allowedModels`/`allowedProviders` and per-key metered consent (combo-level `allowMeteredTargets` still applies). https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/index.ts#L404-L433 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/index.ts#L790-L802 [unchecked for finding 15; Round 1 row confirmed].
2. The environment token `OPENCODEX_API_AUTH_TOKEN` is never scoped and never qualifies for metered targets; it is unrestricted for everything else [checked in review, finding 14].
3. Executor-declared capabilities: a modified executor can declare any capability at pairing, and the hub does not choose the set. This is limited by the executor being the machine whose files are at stake [corrected in review; the security reading is inferred].
4. The gateway does not execute client tools. In the web-search sidecar, a real client tool call (shell, patch) ends the gateway's loop so the client receives it; approval of those calls stays with the client harness. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/web-search/loop.ts#L1166-L1190 [unchecked].

**Hooks in the permission path.** None. For Remote Workspace the hub disables the user's hooks found in the effective Codex config. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-codex-runtime.ts#L392-L414 [checked in review].

**Remote approval answering.** There is no remote approval answering, because nothing asks. Two related "human-only decision" rules exist. Telemetry consent changes through the HTTP API require a human dashboard session and are refused for token or agent callers (`agent_consent_required`). And the CLI consent resolver lets agent-driven, non-TTY or `OCX_SERVICE` starts print a notice but record neither a notice nor a decision, so an agent cannot opt the user in. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/telemetry/routes.ts#L100-L113 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/telemetry/consent.ts#L90-L127 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/cli/telemetry.ts#L114-L152 [checked in review]. Pairing is the one unauthenticated write path: a single-use 12-character code (about 60 bits, 10 minutes, at most 16 outstanding, stored as a hash), requests with an `Origin` header refused, 10 failures in 10 minutes per peer address then 429, and the grant is deleted before the device metadata is validated, so a code is one-shot even if the request is rejected. https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-hub.ts#L377-L425 https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/server/index/serve-options.ts#L484-L513 [checked in review].

## 3. Comparison

The cells condense the accounts in section 2. The permalinks and trust labels
are in section 2.

### 3.1 Default mode and the rule model

| Project | Default mode (real) | Rule model and precedence | Can repository files loosen the user's settings? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Codex | **CLI/TUI:** reviewer `user`. **`exec`:** forces `Never`, so approvals are rejected. **Untrusted projects:** the internal `UnlessTrusted` mode. | **Grammar:** Starlark `prefix_rule` over the argv prefix. **Decision:** the maximum of allow < prompt < forbidden across all layers, plus managed requirements. **Load failure:** one broken file drops every user and project rule. | Project rules load as a layer, but they can only add to the maximum. Repository skills load without a trust check. |
| OpenCode | **Default:** allow everything (shell, edit, web, MCP, external directories); only reads of `.env` ask. **Headless `run`:** rejects asks. **`--auto`:** answers `once`. | **Grammar:** a flat `{action, resource, effect}` list; last match wins; no match asks. **Order:** configured deny is checked first. **Later layers win:** config layers, per-agent rules and HTTP session rules. **Admin policy:** a separate last pass. | Yes. Project config is a later layer. Project plugins load with no trust prompt. |
| OpenClaw | **Host exec:** `security=full`, `ask=off`. **Sandbox:** off. **Sandbox `deny` default:** does not block sandboxed exec. | **Axes:** three independent ones (host, security, ask). **Approvals file:** only tightens. **Session modes:** intersect to the stricter. **Exception:** admin `full` skips the floors. | Not shown in the files read. |
| Hermes Agent | **Default:** `smart`; the code falls back to `manual`. **Deny:** cron, `-q`, API and webhooks. **Auto-approve silently:** `-z` and a bare library context. | **Order:** hardline floor > `approvals.deny` globs > YOLO/off > `command_allowlist` > unattended modes > grants > guardian > human. **Config:** one `config.yaml` per profile. | No project layer described. Grants are written into the agent-reachable `config.yaml`. |
| Oh My Pi | **Default:** `yolo`; an invalid value also falls back to `yolo`. **ACP:** forces `always-ask` for bash unless auto-approve was chosen explicitly. | **Deny:** tool deny > user deny, then the mode ladder. **Restricted folders:** always prompt. | Yes. The project layer can set `yolo` and loosen everything except `bash.patterns`. |
| Pi | **Default:** no approval system; every call runs. | **Rules:** none. **Extensions:** `tool_call` handlers run in load order; the first block wins. | The trust gate only decides which project files load, and a repository cannot relax `defaultProjectTrust`. |
| DeepSeek harness | **Default:** `workspace-write` plus ask; no ordinary call prompts. **sdk-minimal:** `danger-full-access`. | **Rules:** none. **Mode:** approved-for-call > last `sandbox/mode` event > deployment default. | Not described. |
| goose | **Default:** `Auto` on every surface. **Scheduled jobs:** forced to `Auto`. | **Levels:** per tool name in the user-global `permission.yaml`: never_allow > always_allow > ask_before. **Inspectors:** can only tighten. | No project layer. Project plugins auto-enable, and the agent can rewrite its own policy files. |
| Cline | **CLI, Desktop and cron:** approve everything. **VS Code:** prompts only for shell commands. **ACP:** prompts. | **Policy:** `{enabled, autoApprove}` per tool name, plus `"*"`. **Missing policy:** allow. **Enterprise config:** fails open. | Workspace hooks, plugins and agents load untrusted. |
| OpenHands (client) | **Default:** `NeverConfirm`, unless the stored settings enable confirmation. **With confirmation and the `llm` analyzer:** `ConfirmRisky{HIGH, confirm_unknown}`. | **Policy:** one of three kinds, chosen by the browser per conversation. **Rules:** none. | Repository `hooks.json` is attached with no trust prompt. |
| Zed | **Global:** `default: confirm`. **Terminal:** OS-sandboxed by default for local projects. **External ACP agents:** use their own modes. | **Per-tool regex lists:** hardcoded `rm` deny > invalid regex = deny > substitution deny > deny > confirm > allow (every sub-command must match) > tool default > global default. | Not in the files read. Edits to `.zed/` always prompt. |
| Warp | **GUI:** commands `AlwaysAsk`. **TUI:** commands `AgentDecides`. **Headless:** "would ask" becomes `Denylisted`. | **Lists:** anchored regex allow and deny lists. **Precedence:** the denylist beats everything; the organisation list merges first. **Enforcement:** client-side only. | Not described. A self-declared `--sandboxed` argument weakens the policy. |
| OpenCodex | **Tool approvals:** none. **Remote Workspace:** `approvalPolicy: "never"`, sessions `accessMode: read-only`. | **Capabilities:** a set the executor declares, narrowed per session. | Not applicable. |

### 3.2 Shell parsing, binding and "always"

| Project | Shell parsing before matching | What an approval is bound to | "Always" scope |
|---|---|---|---|
| Codex | **Splitting:** a tree-sitter-bash node and operator allow-list for `sh -c`. **Fallback:** anything else is one opaque argv. **Wrappers:** not unwrapped. | **Session key:** environment id, executable, canonical argv, cwd, tty, sandbox and extra permissions. **Patches:** per path. **Never cached:** MCP and network. | **Writes:** an `allow` prefix rule to `default.rules`. **Lasts:** permanently. **Side effect:** also skips the sandbox. |
| OpenCode | **Parser:** a hand-written POSIX and PowerShell scanner. **Splits:** chains, and scans `$(...)`. **Refuses:** eval forms and unparseable input. **Ignores:** redirects, declarations and wrappers. | The request id only. | **Stored:** in SQLite, permanently, project-wide, for all agents. **Pattern:** often `*` or a first-word prefix (`sudo *`). |
| OpenClaw | **Parser:** tree-sitter-bash WASM, limited to 128 KiB and 500 ms. **Unanalyzable:** substitutions, redirects, heredocs, control flow. **Parse failure:** prompts. | argv, cwd (realpath and inode), agentId, sessionKey, a hash of the environment overrides, the real-path executable (SHA-256 if mutable), nodeId and the requester device. | **Persisted entry:** the exact executable path plus a SHA-256 of cwd and argv[1..]. **Cron:** job-bound grants. |
| Hermes Agent | **Matching:** regex over several string variants. **Deny:** per-segment executable projection that sees through `sudo`, `env` and `sh -c`. **Fails closed:** only on size limits. | A pattern class (a rule description such as "recursive delete"). | **Writes:** the class to `command_allowlist`, for every session, permanently. **"Session":** in memory only. |
| Oh My Pi | **Patterns:** anchored `*` globs. **Allow:** the whole command, with no shell control characters. **Deny or prompt:** per segment. **Wrappers:** not unwrapped. | **Session answer:** `subject\0tier`; for bash the subject is the whole tool. **Cached grant:** `tier\0path`. | **Only in** `always-ask-cached` mode. **Stored:** SQLite, no expiry. **Never offered:** for critical calls. |
| Pi | None in core. | Not applicable. | Trust only: per folder, in `trust.json`. |
| DeepSeek harness | None. The string runs as `bash -c`. | One call id. Neither the request nor the audit event carries the arguments. | None. Only `allowed-once`. |
| goose | None. Regexes and models read the raw `command` string. | A once flag on the persisted tool-call id, without the arguments. It expires with the extension lease. | **Key:** the bare tool name. **Stored:** user-global `permission.yaml`, no expiry. |
| Cline | **Approvals:** none. **Plan-mode blacklist:** splits on separators; fails open on parse errors. | One `toolCallId`. One approval covers a whole `run_commands` batch. | None. Only live category toggles. |
| OpenHands (client) | None in this repository. | **None.** `{accept}` answers whatever is pending. | None. |
| Zed | **Parser:** a brush-parser POSIX AST for every shell, PowerShell included. **Redirects:** treated as pseudo-commands. **Wrappers:** not unwrapped. **Parse failure:** disables allow. | The tool plus a prefix regex of the command word and subcommand. | **Written to:** user `settings.json`, for all threads and projects, permanently. |
| Warp | **Decomposes:** `$( )`, `( )` and chains. **Strips:** a leading `KEY=VAL`. **Not handled:** wrapper unwrapping, `$VAR`. | **Per call.** The model's `is_risky` and `is_read_only` can pre-approve. | **Run-to-completion:** for the conversation. **Lasting rules:** the profile lists only. |
| OpenCodex | None. `exec` takes an argv only, inside bubblewrap. | Each RPC is bound to the handshake session id, root id and capability set. | A capability set for one session. |

### 3.3 Model approvers, plan mode and sub-agents

| Project | Model approver and its failure mode | Model-supplied flags | Plan or read-only mode | Sub-agent inheritance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Codex | **Guardian:** a read-only sandboxed thread, 90 s default, risk thresholds. **Errors:** deny. **Timeout or over budget:** back to the human. **Breaker:** per turn. | `sandbox_permissions` and a justification on escalation. | **Prompt only.** The `explorer` role file is empty. | **Roles:** can only narrow; the parent's policy is re-applied last. **Depth:** 1. **Exception:** the memory agent runs with approvals off. |
| OpenCode | None found. | No such flags were found. | **Code-enforced:** edit tools only. **Prompt only:** shell, sub-agents, Code Mode. | **Rules:** a creation-time copy of the parent's session rules plus the child agent's own (often allow-all). **Depth:** 1. |
| OpenClaw | **`auto` reviewer:** temperature 0, 30 s, 16k characters, strict JSON. **Any failure:** ask. **Injection regex match:** deny. **On a node:** ask becomes deny. | None in the files read. | **No plan mode.** The `read-only` session mode is enforced in code. | **Inherits:** the parent's tool allow and deny envelopes. **Exec mode:** not in the files read. |
| Hermes Agent | **Auxiliary model:** sees flagged commands only, answers one word, temperature 0, 30 s. **Any failure:** to the human. **DENY:** overridable once. | None described. | Prompt only. | **Tools:** the parent's minus five. **Dangerous prompts:** auto-deny unless `subagent_auto_approve`. |
| Oh My Pi | **None in the approval path.** The TTSR judge only warns. | None described. | **Code-enforced:** write and edit. **Prompt only:** bash and eval. | **Forced `yolo`,** but the user's deny and prompt rules are kept. **Prompts:** fail closed. **Depth:** 2. |
| Pi | None. | None. | Example extension only. | Example only: a fresh process with the parent's environment. |
| DeepSeek harness | **Experimental Auto review:** the same model, never sees tool results. **Failure:** deny. **Timeout:** none. **Removing the plugin:** widens to full access. | `sandbox_permissions` plus a justification. `approvedBuilds` on `plugin_manager`. | Prompt only. | **In-process:** approval pinned to `never`. **External children:** a fixed, parent-independent permission. |
| goose | **SmartApprove:** fails closed. **Adversary reviewer:** fails open. **Injection classifier:** fails open, never sees tool output. | MCP `readOnlyHint` auto-allows. | Chat mode is enforced at execution, but the tools are still advertised. | **Mode:** inherited from the parent. **Approvals:** forwarded to the parent's user. **Depth:** 1. |
| Cline | None [absence confirmed]. | `requires_approval` removed. | **Code-enforced in part:** edit tools are removed. **Blacklist:** can be bypassed. **CLI:** the model can switch to act itself. | **Policies and approver:** none, so everything runs. **Nesting:** unbounded. |
| OpenHands (client) | Inline `security_risk`, probably; the rest is in the SDK. | `security_risk` on each action. | A separate planner conversation with three tools. | **Takes:** the global settings. **Limits:** no cap, no narrowing. |
| Zed | None. | A non-empty `reason` is required for sandbox escalation. | Not in the files read. | Not in the files read. |
| Warp | **None on the client.** The model's `is_risky=false` auto-runs the command. | `is_risky` and `is_read_only`. | Not in the files read. | **Native children:** inherit the profile. **Claude and Codex children:** launched with their safety flags off. |
| OpenCodex | None. | None. | `accessMode: read-only`, enforced on the executor. | Not in the files read. |

### 3.4 Bypass paths, hooks and remote answers

| Project | Known bypass paths | Hook failure mode | Does a remote answer name the action? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Codex | `allow` rules skip the sandbox. `--ignore-rules`. A hook-trust bypass flag. The exec-server runs the caller's sandbox, or none. | **Fails open** on error, timeout and bad JSON. **Precedence:** deny wins. **Hook allow:** pre-approves the unsandboxed retry. | **Not shown.** It is a JSON-RPC response; binding to the action is [inferred]. **`HumanCommand`:** routed to opted-in connections only. |
| OpenCode | Plugin tools without `assert`. `` !`cmd` `` templates. Shell redirects. The server password is readable, which enables self-approval [inferred]. | **`permission.evaluate`:** can loosen asks. **A throw:** inferred to fail the call. | **No.** Any password or pairing-token holder answers by request id. |
| OpenClaw | Open defaults. Cron standing grants. A Gateway approval overrides the node's allow-list. | **Fails closed:** any throw in `before_tool_call`. | **Node exec:** yes (runId, node, device, binding, single use). **Chat `/approve` with an empty approver list:** anyone in the chat. |
| Hermes Agent | Container backends skip the floor and the prompts. `execute_code`. `-z`. | **`pre_tool_call` crash:** fails open. **`approve` escalation:** fails closed. | **Card:** shows the redacted command and stays deny-only until the full command is visible. **Request-id binding:** not in the files read. |
| Oh My Pi | `eval` kernels. MCP at the `write` tier. Project settings can set `yolo`. Auto-loaded project hooks and tools. | **Fails closed:** a throw, a 30 s timeout or an abort blocks. **After a rewrite:** policy is re-resolved. | Not in the files read. |
| Pi | `!` commands. Install scripts. Hook argument mutation without re-validation. | **`tool_call` throw:** blocks the call. **Observers:** fail open. | **RPC dialog:** answered by dialog id, showing the extension's message text (clone check, section 1). |
| DeepSeek harness | MCP. In-process tools. `approvedBuilds`. External children. | **A throwing listener:** fails the call closed. **The hook bridges:** not read. | **No.** The answer is `{clientId, eventId, outcome}`. |
| goose | Code Mode `tools.call` skips approval, inspectors and hooks. ACP-hosted agents. `readOnlyHint`. Writes to the policy files. | **Default:** fails open. **PreToolUse `on_failure: block`:** fails closed, timeouts included. | **Call id:** persisted first; conflicting answers rejected. **Arguments:** not bound. |
| Cline | Sub-agents. Hub client tools. MCP. Untrusted workspace hooks. Origin-only hub sessions. | **Fails open** on a crash, a timeout or bad output. A cancel aborts the whole run. | **Opaque `approvalId` only.** Any attached client may answer. |
| OpenHands (client) | Server-level bash. The session key is in the agent's environment. Client tools. `tool_module_qualnames`. | **Load failure or 5 s timeout:** all hooks are dropped. | **No.** The body is `{accept, reason?}`. |
| Zed | External ACP agents ignore `tool_permissions`. ACP `terminal/create` is unsandboxed. Allow-all skips the substitution check. | Not in the files read. | **ACP:** the agent's options are passed through. **Binding:** not shown. |
| Warp | Run-to-completion (default on). `--sandboxed`. CLI children with their safety flags off. | No hooks. | **ACP:** answered by policy, which prefers `AllowAlways`. |
| OpenCodex | An unauthenticated loopback listener. Executor-declared capabilities. | No hooks. | No remote answering. |

## 4. Patterns and trade-offs

### 4.1 Five shapes of policy

**1. No gate, or a gate that is open by default.**
- Pi has no gate at all. DeepSeek harness's pipeline reaches approval only when a plugin returns `ask`, and none does by default.
- OpenCode's base rule is `*:* allow`. Oh My Pi defaults to `yolo`, and so does an invalid value. goose defaults to `Auto`.
- Cline's CLI, Desktop and cron surfaces approve everything. OpenHands through Canvas sends `NeverConfirm`. OpenClaw's host exec ships `security=full, ask=off`.
- Cost: the approval code exists but is not the boundary. Whatever these projects do contain comes from a sandbox (DeepSeek's write-only kernel sandbox, OpenCodex's bubblewrap and capability set) or from nothing (Pi, OpenCode, Oh My Pi: no OS sandbox).
- Evidence: https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/schema/src/agent.ts#L46-L51 ; https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/approval.ts#L97-L113 ; https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/config/base.rs#L1316-L1323 ; https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/fcc0ad462172522aaf5c93a7b2e01f6b9f0b0159/packages/core/tools/src/pipeline.ts#L197-L206 ; https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/8bac830dcb8ff99055ba71558dd369a989cc7881/src/agents/exec-defaults.ts#L171-L188

**2. Per-tool-name levels.**
- Who: goose (`permission.yaml`), Cline (`{enabled, autoApprove}`), and our own `main` (`ask` covers mutating tools).
- What it is: simple and easy to show in a UI.
- Cost: it cannot tell `git status` from `git push --force`, so every "always" on the shell tool is total. goose's "always" on `shell` allows every command in every project: https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose/blob/14bf51817b79f6e98ca73c6eda61c11d9d5cc892/crates/goose/src/agents/state_machine/tool_approval.rs#L108-L126

**3. Pattern rules over the command.** Each project matches differently:

| Project | Pattern type | Precedence |
|---|---|---|
| Codex | argv-prefix Starlark | maximum decision |
| OpenCode | wildcard | last match wins |
| Zed | regex | deny > confirm > allow, with allow needing every sub-command |
| Oh My Pi | glob | deny per segment, allow on the whole command |
| Warp | anchored regex | denylist first |
| Hermes Agent | regex classes | hardline floor and `approvals.deny` first |
| OpenClaw | allow-list over resolved executable paths | — |

  The cost is always the same: the rule is only as good as the parser in front of it (4.2).
- Two precedence models exist:
  - **A lattice (Codex, Zed).** It is order-independent and easy to reason about: a deny anywhere wins.
  - **An ordered list (OpenCode).** A later, wider layer silently overrides an earlier one. OpenCode then needs a separate administrator pass to restore a ceiling: https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/05d347d75054bf27778750ad5f2cd30336e81d8f/packages/core/src/config/plugin/policy.ts#L98-L113

**4. A capability set fixed per session.**
- Who: OpenCodex (`accessMode`, session id and root id enforced on the executor per RPC), and OpenClaw's session modes, which intersect to the stricter.
- Cost: no per-command decision at all. This is the right shape for a ceiling, not for interactive approvals: https://github.com/lidge-jun/opencodex/blob/ddee2c20030c1f0ec852202256be877397e8f396/src/remote-control/workspace-rpc.ts#L298-L346

**5. Model-in-the-loop.** The model either reviews the call or rates its own call (4.4).

### 4.2 Shell parsing: what each approach cannot see

| Approach | Who | What it cannot see |
|---|---|---|
| Real AST, allow-list of node kinds; anything else becomes one opaque argv | Codex (tree-sitter-bash), OpenClaw (tree-sitter WASM with size and time limits), Zed (brush-parser) | Wrappers (`bash -c`, `sudo`, `env`, `xargs`, `find -exec`) are not unwrapped by any of the three. Zed parses PowerShell and cmd as POSIX. Substitutions are denied (Zed) or prompt (OpenClaw). |
| Hand-written scanner | OpenCode (POSIX and PowerShell), Warp (`decompose_command`) | OpenCode ignores redirects and declarations as resources. Warp does not unwrap wrappers or expand `$VAR`. Both are large hand-written code paths with parts nobody read (OpenCode's 2,565-line scanner). |
| Regex over string variants | Hermes Agent (normalised, quote-masked, `-c` payloads, deobfuscated), goose, Cline plan-mode blacklist, Pi example gate | Anything the regex author did not think of. Cline's own comment admits `python -c` and `bash -c` pass: https://github.com/cline/cline/blob/500a40e7b76692153906b44dd68732520a966663/sdk/packages/core/src/extensions/tools/command-guard.ts#L10-L17 |
| No parsing | DeepSeek harness, OpenCodex (argv only), OpenHands client | Not applicable: they rely on a sandbox or on nothing. |

Two good asymmetries are worth keeping:

- **Allow must cover the whole command; deny may hit any segment.** Oh My Pi and Zed both do this:
  - https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/e325ffc73ab86e03bdd20a91b77d8e2b3964c906/packages/coding-agent/src/tools/bash.ts#L311-L321
  - https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/3def8bad0b62dbda02d5b67b00df96fe8d3957bd/crates/agent/src/tool_permissions.rs#L208-L260
